# COMPLEMENT
## OF THE EXORCISTIC ART
### SECOND PART.
->WHICH is called THE BENEDICTIONAL<-
->part.<-

### *THE BLESSING OF SALT,*
->*and of water.*<-

->Our help is in the name of the † Lord.<-
->Who made Heaven and earth.<-

### EXORCISM OF THE SALT.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop cap 'E' featuring complex floral and scrollwork ornamentation.</image-desc>

I exorcise <term>exorcise: The act of commanding a person or object to be free from the influence of evil spirits.</term> you, creature of Salt, by the † living and true God, who commanded that offerings and sacrifices be seasoned with Salt: by that same God † I exorcise you, who established for the children of Israel a covenant of Salt forever before Him <note>original: "pactum Salis in æternum". This refers to the ancient practice of using salt to seal agreements, mentioned in the Bible (Numbers 18:19).</note>: By † Him I exorcise you, who commanded Ezra the Priest <note>original: "Esdræ Sacerdoti". Ezra was a key biblical figure who led a group of Judean exiles from Babylon back to Jerusalem.</note> that Salt be given without measure, so that you may be for all who take you a source of health for soul and body; and that there may flee and depart from every person and place where you are sprinkled, consumed, or placed, every Phantom <note>original: "Phantasma". In this context, it refers to a deceptive spiritual apparition or hallucination caused by demons.</note>, every wickedness, and every craftiness of Diabolical deceit, and every unclean spirit, when commanded: By Him who is coming to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

I exorcise you again, creature of Salt, by Jesus † Christ our Lord: who, teaching His Disciples, said: Salt is good; have Salt within yourselves: and again: you are the Salt of the earth: by † Him I exorcise you, who deigned to teach us through the Apostle, saying: Let your heart and your speech always be seasoned with Salt in grace: so that you may have no communication with evil spirits, but may be Salt exorcised for the salvation of believers,

<meta>catchword: & Salt</meta>

<summary>This page contains the formal Latin ritual for the exorcism and blessing of salt, invoking biblical precedents to imbue the salt with the power to repel demons and provide spiritual healing. It emphasizes salt's symbolic role as a preservative of faith and a tool for purification.</summary>

# COMPLEMENT OF THE ART

...And the salt of wisdom, tempering all human acts; the salt of penance, preserving from the rot of damnation; the salt of friendship with God and neighbor; the salt for the extermination of all sorceries <note>original: "maleficiorum". In a medieval or early modern context, this refers specifically to harmful magic or "black" witchcraft meant to cause physical or spiritual injury.</note>. Therefore, may the salt be made to empty out, expel, and exterminate those very enemies of ours who harass the vessels of God <note>This refers to the human body or soul, often described as a vessel or temple for the Holy Spirit.</note> protected by the Sacraments. In the name of the † Father, and of the † Son, and of the † Holy Spirit. Amen.

V. The Lord be with you.
R. And with your spirit.

->Let us pray.<-

We pray for the unconquered strength of Your power, Holy Lord, Almighty Father, Eternal God, over this Salt, upon which You have deigned to bestow no small grace, so that from it all things may be seasoned <note>original: "conditi". This implies both the culinary seasoning of food and the spiritual preservation of the soul.</note> which You have commanded to be produced for humans for food and drink through Your Son: through whom we humbly beseech You, Lord, that You might deign to sanctify †, bless †, and consecrate † this Salt out of Your piety, so that it may receive Your power from the face of Your divine Majesty against all unclean spirits. May it expel from the habitations <note>original: "Tabernaculis". Though it literally means "tents" or "tabernacles," it is used here to mean any place where God's servants dwell.</note> of Your male and female servants whatever can be pestilential; may it put to flight from their dwellings all the snares of the lurking enemy; may it destroy every wicked work from all things used by humans. May it strike terror into illusions <note>original: "præstigia". These were thought to be demonic tricks or sensory deceptions that made people see things that weren't there.</note>, restrain phantasms, and offer a fullness of safety; and may it confer the effect of health through the power of the Holy Cross † of our Lord Jesus Christ. May it provide a most faithful protection to those who ask and desire it through this, Your seal †, Lord, by whose power I bless † this creature of Salt, and in Your holy name, three and one, I sanctify † and consecrate † it, so that against striking spirits <note>original: "Spiritus percutientes". These were specific types of demons believed to cause sudden physical ailments or "blows" to the body.</note> it may be made a salvific Sacrament <term>Sacrament: Here used in a general sense to mean a "sacred sign" or a "sacramental," rather than one of the seven formal Sacraments of the Church.</term> and a most perfect medicine in the inner parts of the faithful, through the merits of the passion of Your Son our Lord, who lives and reigns with You forever and ever. Amen.

The prayer emphasizes the salt's role in purifying dwellings and protecting the "vessels of God" (the faithful) from the "lurking enemy."</summary>

FIRST PART OF EXORCISM.
821

## THE EXORCISM OF WATER.

> I exorcise you, creature of Water, in the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit: That you may not be common <note>original: "commune". In this ritual sense, "common" means spiritually unprotected or available for use by profane or evil forces.</note> to any unclean spirit: But give honor to the living and reigning God, so that for whomever [you are used] as a drink, or wherever you are sprinkled and mixed, every unclean spirit may depart from that body and from that place. Therefore I adjure you, creature of Water, in the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth, Son of the living God, our Redeemer and Judge, that you may be the cleansing and sanctification of people, whom God has deigned to create in His own holy image and to call to His glory, Amen.

-> Antiphon. <-
<note>An antiphon is a short sentence, often from Scripture, sung or recited before and after a psalm to set the spiritual tone.</note>

> If anyone thirsts, let him come to me and drink. Alleluia.
> He who believes in me, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water. Alleluia.

-> Psalm 41. <-
<note>The numbering follows the Latin Vulgate; in most modern English Bibles, this is Psalm 42. It is chosen for its imagery of the soul thirsting for God like a deer seeks water.</note>

> As the deer longs for the springs of water: so my soul longs for You, O God.
> My soul has thirsted for God, the living fountain: when shall I come and appear before the face of God?
> My tears have been my bread day and night: while it is said to me daily, "Where is your God?"
> I remembered these things, and I poured out my soul within me: for I shall pass over into the place of the wonderful tabernacle, even to the house of God.
> With the voice of joy and praise: the sound of one feasting.
> Why are you sad, my soul? And why do you trouble me?
> Hope in God, for I will yet give praise to Him: the salvation of my countenance, and my God.
> Within myself, my soul is troubled: therefore I will remember You from the land of Jordan, and from the Hermonites, from the little hill.
> Deep calls unto deep at the noise of Your floodgates <note>original: "cataractarum". This refers to the celestial windows or waterfalls through which God sends rain or floods.</note>. All Your heights and Your waves have passed over me.
> In the daytime the Lord has commanded His mercy, and in the night His song: with me is a prayer to the God of my life.
> I will say to God: You are my upholder; why have You forgotten me? And why do I go mourning while the enemy afflicts me?

# COMPLETION OF THE RITUAL

While my bones are being broken, my enemies who trouble me have reproached me. 
While they say to me every day: "Where is your God?" Why are you sad, my soul? And why do you disturb me? 
Hope in God, for I will yet praise him: the salvation of my countenance, and my God. <note>original: "salutare vultus mei". This refers to God being the one who brings health or radiance to the speaker's face.</note> Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: As it was in the beginning, and now and always, and for ages of ages. Amen. 

If anyone thirsts, let him come to me and drink, Alleluia. <note>original: "Alleluja", a Hebrew expression of praise meaning "Praise Yahweh," used here to celebrate the spiritual gift of water.</note> He who believes in me, rivers of living water shall flow from his belly. 
Alleluia. 

Our Father, who art in heaven, etc. 
And lead us not into temptation. 
But deliver us from evil. 
Grant, Lord, a blessing. 
May the Only-begotten † Son of God deign to bless and help us. Amen. 

## Reading from the book of Genesis.

IN those days: Rebecca came out—the daughter of Bethuel, who was the son of Milcah the wife of Nahor, brother of Abraham—carrying a water-jar on her shoulder. She was a girl of exceeding beauty, a most fair Virgin, and unknown by man. She had gone down to the spring, and had filled her water-jar, and was returning. And the servant ran to meet her, and said: "Provide me a little water to drink from your jar." And she replied: "Drink, my lord." And quickly she lowered the jar onto her arm and gave him a drink. And when he had drunk, she added: "I will also draw water for your Camels, until they all have drunk." And pouring out the jar into the troughs, she ran back to the well to draw water, and gave what was drawn to all the Camels.

-> Thanks be to God. <-

O God, who for the salvation of the human race established the greatest Sacraments <term>Sacraments: original "Sacramenta". In this context, it refers to the sacred mysteries or hidden spiritual powers that God has instilled in physical things, like water.</term> within the substance of water, be favorably present to our invocations: And to this element, prepared by many kinds of purifications,

# SECOND PART OF THE RITES OF EXORCISM

...pour forth the power of your blessing, so that this, your creature <note>In liturgical language, "creature" (creatura) refers to a created element of nature—in this case, water—which is being addressed as part of God's creation to be set apart for a holy purpose.</note> serving your mysteries, may receive the effect of divine grace to drive away demons and expel diseases. May whatever in the houses, places, and bodies of the faithful is sprinkled with this water be free from all uncleanness and delivered from harm. Let no pestilent spirit or corrupting air remain there; let all the snares of the hidden enemy depart. And if there is anything that envies the safety or the peace of those living there, may it flee at the sprinkling of this water, so that the healthfulness sought through the invocation of your holy name may be defended from all attacks. Through our Lord Jesus Christ, your Son. Amen.

->Antiphon<- <note>An antiphon is a short sentence sung or recited before and after a psalm.</note>

> The Angel of the Lord descended at a certain time into the Pool, and the water was moved. Alleluia. <note>original: "Piscina". This refers to the Pool of Bethesda described in the Gospel of John, where the first person to enter the water after it was "stirred" by an angel would be healed.</note> And he who descended first into the Pool after the motion of the water was made whole from whatever infirmity held him. Alleluia. <note>original: "Alleluja", a Hebrew expression of praise meaning "Praise Yahweh."</note>

->Psalm 22<- <note>In modern English Bibles, this is known as Psalm 23 ("The Lord is my Shepherd"). The numbering differs because the Latin Vulgate combines certain psalms that other translations keep separate.</note>

> The Lord rules me, and nothing shall be wanting to me; he has set me there in a place of pasture.
> 
> He has brought me up beside the water of refreshment; he has converted my soul.
> 
> He has led me on the paths of justice, for his own name’s sake.
> 
> For even if I walk in the midst of the shadow of death, I will fear no evils, for you are with me.
> 
> Your rod and your staff, they have comforted me.
> 
> You have prepared a table before me against those who trouble me.
> 
> You have anointed my head with oil, and my cup which inebriates me—how excellent it is! <note>original: "calix meus inebrians". While "inebriate" usually suggests drunkenness, in this spiritual context, it refers to being filled to overflowing with divine joy or grace.</note>
> 
> And your mercy shall follow me all the days of my life.
> 
> And that I may dwell in the house of the Lord for length of days. Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit. As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen.

### READING FROM THE BOOK OF NUMBERS.

In those days: Moses and Aaron, having entered the Tabernacle of the Covenant, fell prostrate on the ground and cried out to the Lord, and said: Lord God, hear the cry of this people, and open to them your treasury, the fountain of living water, so that being satisfied, their murmuring may cease: and the glory of the Lord appeared over them. And the Lord spoke to Moses, saying: Take the rod, and gather your people, you and Aaron your brother, and speak to the rock before them, and it shall give forth waters. And when you have brought forth water from the rock, all the multitude and their cattle shall drink. Moses therefore took the rod, which was in the sight of the Lord, as he had commanded them, the multitude being gathered before the rock, and he said to them: Hear, you rebels and unbelievers: Can we bring forth water for you from this rock? And when Moses had lifted up his hand, striking the flint twice with the rod, waters came out most abundantly, so that the people and their cattle drank. <note>This passage from Numbers 20:6-11 is chosen to show that God can imbue physical elements (like a rock or water) with life-saving and miraculous properties through the mediation of His servants.</note>

FIRST PART OF THE EXORCISM.

...of enemies, the infestations of Demons, the shadow of phantoms, the torments of witchcraft <note>original: "maleficiorum". While often translated as "evildoings," in an exorcistic context, it specifically refers to harm caused by sorcery or curses.</note>, the incursion of whirlwinds, every spirit of lightning, of thunder, of tempests, and of storms may be driven away, through you yourself the almighty: Who lives and reigns with the Son and the Holy Spirit, forever and ever. Amen.

### Antiphon <note>An antiphon is a short sentence sung or recited before and after a Psalm.</note>

The Angel showed me a river of living water, sparkling like crystal, proceeding from the throne of God and of the Lamb. Alleluia; and to the thirsty, I will give from the fountain of the water of life freely, says the Lord. Alleluia.

### Psalm 68 <note>In the Latin Vulgate, this is Psalm 68; in modern English Bibles, it is Psalm 69. It is a cry of distress from someone overwhelmed by "the waters" of affliction.</note>

Save me, O God, for the waters have entered even unto my soul.

I am stuck in the mud of the deep, and there is no solid ground <note>original: "substantia". Refers to a firm place to stand.</note>.

I have come into the depth of the sea, and a storm has submerged me.

I have labored crying out, my throat has become hoarse: my eyes have failed, while I hope in my God.

They who hate me without cause have been multiplied more than the hairs of my head.

My enemies who persecute me unjustly have been strengthened: I then paid back things I did not steal.

God, you know my foolishness, and my offenses are not hidden from you.

Let them not be ashamed on my account: those who wait for you, Lord: Lord of virtues <note>original: "Domine virtutum". This is the Latin equivalent of "Lord of Hosts," referring to God's command over the heavenly armies.</note>.

Let them not be confounded over me, those who seek you, O God of Israel.

For because of you I have sustained reproach: shame has covered my face.

I have been made a stranger to my brothers, and a foreigner to the sons of my mother.

For the zeal of your house has eaten me up, and the reproaches of those reproaching you have fallen upon me.

And I covered my soul in fasting: and it was made a reproach to me.

And I put on sackcloth <note>original: "cilicium". A garment of coarse animal hair worn as a sign of mourning or penance.</note> for my clothing: and I became a parable <note>Meaning a proverb, a byword, or a target of mockery.</note> to them.

<meta>This page continues the liturgical recitation of Psalm 68 (Psalm 69 in modern Bibles), which began on the previous page as part of the Rite for the Blessing of Water or a related exorcistic "Art." # COMPLETION OF THE ART <note>original: "COMPLEMENTUM ARTIS". This header suggests this section concludes a specific ritual process or "art," likely referring to the ceremonial consecration or exorcism involving the water mentioned on the previous page.</note>

They who sat in the gate spoke against me: and those who drank wine made me their song.

But as for me, my prayer is to you, O Lord: it is the time of your good pleasure, O God.

In the multitude of your mercy, hear me: in the truth of your salvation.

Rescue me from the mud, that I may not be stuck: deliver me from those who hate me, and from the deep waters.

Let not the storm of water submerge me: nor let the deep swallow me up: nor let the pit close its mouth over me.

Hear me, O Lord, for your mercy is kind: according to the multitude of your tender mercies, look upon me.

And do not turn your face away from your servant <note>original: "puero tuo". While "puer" can mean boy, in this biblical and liturgical context, it refers to a humble servant or child of God.</note>; for I am in trouble, hear me quickly.

Draw near to my soul, and deliver it: rescue me because of my enemies.

You know my reproach, and my confusion, and my shame.

In your sight are all who trouble me: my heart has looked for reproach and misery.

And I looked for someone who would grieve with me, and there was no one: and for someone to comfort me, and I found no one.

And they gave me gall for my food, and in my thirst they gave me vinegar to drink. <note>In Christian tradition, this verse is heavily associated with the Passion of Christ and the offerings made to him on the cross.</note>

Let their table before them become a snare: and a recompense, and a stumbling block.

Let their eyes be darkened, so that they may not see: and keep their backs always bowed down.

Pour out your indignation upon them: and let the fury of your anger overtake them.

Let their dwelling place be deserted: and in their tents, let there be no one to dwell.

For they have persecuted him whom you have smitten: and they have added to the pain of my wounds.

Add iniquity to their iniquity: and let them not enter into your justice.

COMPLETION OF THE ART <note>original: "COMPLEMENTUM ARTIS"</note> 828
<meta>catchword: well</meta>

the well is deep. Where then do you get "living water"? Are you greater than our father Jacob, who gave us the well, and drank from it himself, as did his sons and his livestock? Jesus answered and said to her: Everyone who drinks of this water will thirst again; but whoever drinks of the water that I shall give him will not thirst forever. Instead, the water that I give him will become in him a fountain of water springing up into eternal life. The woman said to him: Lord, give me this water, so that I may not thirst. <note>This passage from the Gospel of John (4:11-15) establishes the spiritual authority of the water being blessed, transforming it from a physical substance into a vehicle for divine grace.</note>

->But you, O Lord, have mercy on us.<-
->Thanks be to God.<-

->*Let us pray.*<-

Holy Lord, Father Almighty, Eternal God, who through Jesus Christ your Son our Lord saw fit to heal our wounds, we beseech your mercy, that you might deign to cleanse † and bless † this water, and deign to visit those who drink from it. Just as you did for Tobias and Sarah, and the son who was a lunatic, deaf, and mute <note>Sarah and Tobias are figures from the Book of Tobit who were delivered from a demon; the "lunatic" son refers to the boy healed by Jesus in the Gospels (Matthew 17)</note>: so also deign to free all who drink from this water from every evil spell <term>maleficio: harm or curses believed to be caused by sorcery</term> and the infestation of demons; and grant them the medicine of heavenly grace, through the same Christ our Lord, who lives and reigns with you and with the Holy Spirit forever and ever. Amen.

->*Let him put Salt into the water, saying:*<-

Just as Elisha, going out to the spring of waters, cast salt into it and said: "I have healed these waters, and there shall no longer be death or barrenness in them" <note>This refers to the miracle in 2 Kings 2:19-22, where the prophet Elisha purified a spring using salt</note>: so, through this mixing of Salt and Water, let there be no further infestation of the poisonous Serpent <note>A traditional reference to the Devil or demonic influence</note> among the creatures of God. In the name of the Father † and of the Son † and of the Holy † Spirit, to whom be honor and glory forever and ever. Amen.

### Exorcism over Water mixed with Salt.

I exorcise you, creature of Salt and water, by the living God, by the true God, by the God who is the creator of the universe: and I adjure you by Him who by His power suspended you above the heavens, gathered you on the earth, mixed you in the sea, and made you flow from the fountain of Paradise, commanding you to go forth in four rivers and ordering you to water the whole earth.

# EXORCISTIC RITES, PART ONE <note>original: "EXORCISTICA PARS PRIMA"</note>

*Here let him divide the water into four parts, in the form of a cross.*

> THAT YOU MAY SHOW YOURSELF PURE, and retain no power of the Devil: but be Holy † Water: called blessed † water: water † washing away filth and sins: water † destroying evil spells <term>maleficia: harm or curses believed to be caused by sorcery</term>: water † exorcised to drive away the enemy: and wherever you are sprinkled, whether in the house, or in the corners of rooms, or in sown fields, vineyards, and in all fruits, or in bread, wine, and all things to be eaten, or in feathers, wools, straw, beds, and garments, or in medicines, syrup, baths, and other remedies, or if anyone should taste, or drink, or wash from you, may you become for them a most certain defense, a most effective medicine, a remedy for health: and a harbor of safety. May the Devil himself also, with his followers and evil spells, incantations, bindings <term>ligationibus: a type of magic intended to "bind" or restrict a person's actions or health</term>, signs, and charms be far removed and separated from any place in which you are sprinkled, and from any creature who drinks from you, as far as the Heaven is distant from the earth, Light from darkness, truth from lies, justice from injustice, and sweet from bitter. In the name of the Father, † and of the Son, and of the Holy † Spirit.

*Let him immerse the cross, and say, signing the Water in the form of a cross.*

> And through Him I exorcise † adjure † bless † and sanctify † you, who through Moses His servant touched the sea with a rod: and wrapping the chariots and horsemen of all Pharaoh’s army in the midst of the waves, delivered the people of Israel.

*Let him immerse the Cross a second time, as above.*

> And through Him I exorcise † adjure † bless † and sanctify † you, who showed Moses the wood, which when he had cast it into the bitter waters, they were turned into sweetness. <note>This refers to the miracle at Marah described in Exodus 15:23-25.</note>

*Let him immerse the Cross a third time, as above.*

> And through Him I exorcise †, adjure †, bless †, and sanctify † you, who through Moses struck the rock of Horeb twice with a rod: and very abundant waters came out, and the people drank. THAT YOU MAY SHOW YOURSELF PURE, as above.

*Let him immerse the Paschal Candle <term>Cereum Paschale: a large candle representing the light of Christ, usually blessed during the Easter Vigil</term>, and say.*

> Through the immersion of this blessed candle, may the power of the Holy Spirit descend into you, O Water, and into your whole substance

I consecrate † you through Jesus Christ, who turned water into wine. I bless † you through Jesus Christ, who gives water springing up into eternal life. And like the loaves of those serving the City of Jerusalem. Speak, Lord God, to the heart of your handmaid N., that she may find...

<header>SECOND PART OF THE EXORCISTIC ART</header>

...[may I deliver this bread, which I bless and sanctify, and may it find] grace in your eyes, and comfort her, so that she may eat bread just like Ruth: that she may dip her morsel in oil, and her heart may be strengthened, so that her spirit may return and be refreshed. ->Let us pray.<-

Lord Jesus Christ, who are the bread born in Bethlehem <note>The name "Bethlehem" literally means "House of Bread" in Hebrew.</note>, the bread of life, the bread of Angels, and who daily descend from Heaven to satisfy and sanctify our souls, filling them with your holy blessing: So now may you deign to descend upon this bread, and bless † and sanctify † it, so that you may satisfy, sanctify, and fill the heart, the bowels, and all the limbs of your creature N. <note>The priest would insert the name of the afflicted person here.</note> with your essence. And I, through your most glorious and triumphant name of Jesus—so delightful, magnificent, and lovable—bless † this bread, and sanctify † it. 

And through that same Holy name—terrible, exalted, and raised above every name—I destroy all spells <term>maleficia</term>, incantations, bindings <term>ligationes</term>, occult signs <term>signaturas</term>, and charms <term>facturas</term> <note>These terms refer to various forms of folk magic and witchcraft believed to cause physical or spiritual harm.</note> made by diabolical art against your creatures who were redeemed by your most precious blood. Whatever is faithfully asked in your same holy name, you, being moved to mercy, always grant. 

Therefore, through that same holy name, which is to be preached everywhere and adored with all devotion, I bless † sanctify † and consecrate † this bread, and I infuse the power of your most holy name † to restore sight to the blind, hearing to the deaf, walking to the lame, health to the sick, and salvation to all believers. 

I bless † sanctify, † and consecrate † this bread, and may the Lord be with you, O blessed bread, so that you may extinguish poisons, suppress harmful things, drive away adversities, put demons to flight, and root out the most powerful tyranny of Hell <note>original: "Tartari," a classical term for the underworld used here for the demonic realm.</note> and of the Prince of the World <note>A title for Satan.</note> from the limbs of this creature N. and from all the creatures of God. And finally, for all who partake of you, may there come forth continuous health and protection, the forgiveness of sins, and the perpetual and longed-for enjoyment of the name of Jesus. Amen.

### EXORCISM PART TWO

...you, so that you may be like unleavened hearth-bread <note>original: "panis subcineritius azimus" — bread baked under the hot ashes of a fire, often associated with the haste and purity of the Exodus.</note>, sprinkled with oil: and like the bread of the presence <note>original: "panis propositionis" — also known as showbread, the twelve loaves kept in the Jewish Temple as an offering.</note> placed upon the table in the sight of God forever, to weaken every mark of Lucifer. I bless † you, so that you may be like the hearth-cake of barley belonging to Gideon, seen in a dream like a sword against Midian <note>Judges 7:13-14. In the Bible, a dream of a rolling barley loaf destroying a tent symbolized Gideon's victory over his enemies.</note>, to scatter every work of Satan; and like the loaf of bread offered to Christ, to put the enemy to flight from God’s creatures. I sanctify † you, so that you may be the bread sought by Jacob at Bethel, and the bread of Joseph given to Benjamin, to root out every evil deed of the Devil. I consecrate † you, so that you may be the rich bread of Asher <note>Genesis 49:20. Jacob's blessing on his son Asher promised that his food would be "rich" and fit for kings.</note>, providing delights to Kings, and the bread of Ephraim, which does not return, to extirpate every enchantment of the enemy. I bless † you, so that you may be the bread of the tears of David’s contrition, and the bread of Judith, used to decapitate Holofernes <note>Judith 10-13. Judith brought her own food into the enemy camp before killing the general Holofernes.</note>. I sanctify † you, so that you may be the bread in the delights of Paradise, the bread of wisdom, the bread of life proceeding from the word of God; bread from Heaven, containing all sweetness within itself, to break all the bonds of the Devil. I consecrate † you, so that you may be daily bread, bread not to be thrown to the dogs, bread set upon the tomb of the righteous <note>original: "super sepulturam justi" — a reference to the ancient practice of placing food at the graves of the holy, or the "bread of mourners."</note>, to destroy every work of the adversary. I sanctify † you, so that you may be like that loaf of unleavened bread handed into the hands of the Nazarene, and like the bread of the firstfruits: oiled bread, and the unleavened offering, and the sacrifice cooked in the oven, and the host for sin and for thanksgiving, to expel every infirmity, both natural and diabolical. And whoever eats from this bread, may they be like a woman, and like the merchant’s ship bringing her bread from afar <note>Proverbs 31:14. A metaphor for the virtuous woman providing for her household.</note>; and may they be healed and freed from all witchcraft <note>term: "maleficiis" — harmful magic or sorcery.</note>, incantations, bindings <note>term: "ligationibus" — a type of spell intended to "bind" a person's faculties or potency.</note>, marks, and spells made by diabolical art; and from every fever and sickness, and let Satan withdraw, confounded and driven back. Finally, I bless † you bread, I sanctify † and consecrate † in the name of Him whom the Disciples recognized in the breaking of the bread, who is the sweetest bread and fills the hungry with Heavenly goods; and through the Holy Virgin Mary, who alone among women deserved within her womb... <note>original: "visceribus" — literally "vitals" or "entrails," commonly used in a sacred context to mean the womb.</note>

### SECOND PART OF THE EXORCISM RITES.

...be driven out: For you planted it, Lord, and fenced it about from the beginning, and in the time of drought you irrigated it with the divine blood of your Son. Help us therefore, Lord, and bless † and sanctify † this wine: so that it may be the wine of grace, of knowledge, of fervent teaching, and of love, composed of many virtues, for the healing of whatever creatures taste and drink from it: May it nourish and enliven the soul toward devotion, constancy, and spiritual consolation, and may it refresh and strengthen the body: may it clarify the intellect, gladden the heart, and expel all pain and sadness: may it destroy and annihilate all harmful spells <term>maleficia — acts of sorcery or "black magic" intended to cause physical or spiritual harm</term> from your creatures who drink from it. Through the same Christ our Lord, Amen.

->Let us pray.<-

Almighty eternal God, cause us to pray for your mercy with all supplication over this wine, so that in blessing † you may bless it, and in sanctifying † you may sanctify it: so that it may be the wine set forth and blessed by Uncreated Wisdom <note>original: "Sapientia increata" — a theological title for Christ, personified as the Wisdom of God described in the Book of Proverbs.</note>, upon the table in her house: May it be as the wine reserved for the King to destroy every weariness caused by the Devil: May it be like the libations of wine which are to be poured out for each individual sacrifice: May it be abundant wine from the hand of the divine King to uproot harmful magic: May it be like the wine over the burial of the just: May it be the wine bought without silver and without any exchange <note>A reference to Isaiah 55:1, "come, buy wine and milk without money and without price."</note>, gladdening the hearts of those who drink: May it be the wine which you, Lord, promised to your servants, and let those drinking this wine sing psalms in joyfulness: Draw them, let them run after you; lead them into your wine cellar <note>original: "Cella vinaria" — an allusion to the Song of Solomon 2:4, symbolizing a place of divine intimacy and spiritual refreshment.</note>, and let them exult and be glad in you, having received health, and over the confusion of Satan. Through Christ our Lord, Amen.

->Let us pray.<-

Almighty God, have mercy on your handmaid <note>The Latin "famulæ tuæ" is specifically feminine, suggesting this prayer was used for a woman being treated for demonic possession or the effects of witchcraft.</note> who is vexed by the Devil, and grant her to breathe again favorably from the continuous torments of harmful spells: bless therefore † this wine, and sanctify † it, so that it may be like the wine which Christ gives to His devoted ones to drink in His spiritual banquet: May it be the wine which Wisdom has mixed, so that days may be multiplied...

## COMPLETION OF THE CRAFT

...and let years of life be added to all who drink from it, and let all power of the Devil be extinguished in them. Give, O Lord, to this wine increases of grace and blessings: and let a glorious crown protect those drinking it, and may they be delivered from all sorceries <term>maleficia — evil acts of magic meant to harm</term>, incantations, binding spells <term>ligationes — a type of magic intended to "bind" a person’s will, health, or fertility</term>, magical sigils <term>signaturis — occult characters or signs drawn to summon spirits</term>, and charms <term>facturis — physical objects or "fetishes" constructed for magical purposes</term>. Remember, O Lord God, this your handmaid **N.** <note>The letter "N." is a placeholder for the name (Nomen) of the person being exorcised.</note> and do not erase your mercies which you performed for her upon the cross: May she drink this excellent, blessed † and sanctified † wine, worthy for a beloved spouse to drink; Come, my beloved Jesus, let us go out into the vineyards, so that we may see that your vineyard of the Lord of Hosts <note>original: "Sabaoth" — a Hebrew title for God meaning "of the Armies" or "of Hosts"</note> has flourished to extinguish the stench of Lucifer: may these be words of blessing † and sanctification †, like clusters from the Gospel vineyard fattened by the Sacraments, the scent of which shall kill the ancient serpent: May the wine-presses of the vineyard of En Gedi <note>original: "Engabdi" — likely a variant of En Gedi, a place in the Judean wilderness famous in the Bible for its vineyards and springs</note> overflow, and may innumerable blessings drip from it, and most generous sanctifications and consecrations be multiplied over this wine, and over the creatures vexed by the Devil, so that those drinking from this wine may be healed and delivered from the bites of demons, whom the infernal spider has bitten.

Furthermore, good God, speak the word, and bless † and sanctify † the wine and your creature **N.**, just as after the wine was taken from Isaac you gave blessings to his son Jacob: and after the wine was taken from King Ahasuerus <note>original: "Assuero" — the King in the Book of Esther</note>, you blessed Queen Esther: Finally, I bless † and sanctify † this wine, so that **N.** may be inebriated in the praise of our God who has performed wonders with us, and that the creature herself may not be confounded forever: and through the blessed and sanctified wine, I destroy, annihilate, root out, confound, and eradicate all sorceries, incantations, bindings, sigils, and charms made by the diabolical art against all creatures, and especially against this handmaid **N.** Through Him who trod the winepress alone <note>A reference to Isaiah 63:3, interpreted by theologians as Christ enduring the Passion alone</note>, and washed his robe in wine, and who lives and reigns with God the Father and the Holy Spirit, forever and ever. Amen.

# THE BLESSING OF BREAD, WI-
# ne, and of all things intended
# for eating.

> Our help ✝ is in the name of the Lord,
> Who made Heaven and earth.

## THE EXORCISM

I exorcise you, creatures of bread and wine, and of every edible thing: In the name ✝ of him who blessed and multiplied the loaves in the desert, who turned water into wine, and who gave manna to the fathers in the desert, containing within itself every delight; so that you may be strength, health, and purification for this person and for all creatures of God partaking of you, to drive away all unclean spirits, and every wicked power of the Devil, and to exterminate all the forms and threats of Satan. Through him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire, Amen.

### Let us pray.

Almighty God, the living bread of eternal life and of the Angels, who made Elijah walk as far as Mount Horeb in the strength of bread baked under ashes <note>original: "subcinericii panis" — a reference to 1 Kings 19:6, where an angel feeds the prophet Elijah</note>, and who multiplied five loaves in the desert by your holy blessing, and transubstantiated <term>transubstantiasti — the miraculous changing of one substance into another</term> water into the finest wine. You who fed Moses and your people in the desert with the sweetest foods, may you deign to purify ✝ bless, ✝ and sanctify ✝ these creatures of food and drink, which in your holy name I purify ✝ bless, ✝ and sanctify, ✝ so that whoever eats and drinks of them, may drink and eat before the Lord with all joy through the love of the blessed Virgin Mary and of all the Saints; and let these foods be a defense and protection against sorceries <term>maleficia — harmful magic</term>, traps, and the diseases of the cunning enemy: and may the hearts, vitals, and all members of the bodies of those eating be sanctified through the grace of the Holy Spirit. All of which sorceries, incantations, binding spells <term>ligationes — magic intended to bind a person's will or body</term>, magical sigils <term>signaturas — occult symbols</term>, and charms <term>facturas — physical objects or "fetishes" used in witchcraft</term>, I destroy ✝ and annihilate ✝ through these edible and drinkable things, and from the [human] bodies...

# BLESSING

*For all medicines, ointments, baths, and perfumes—making the necessary changes for each.* <note>original: "mutatis, tamen mutandis" — a Latin phrase meaning the wording of the prayer should be adjusted to fit the specific item (e.g., saying "this ointment" instead of "this medicine")</note>

->Our help † is in the name of the Lord.<-

## EXORCISM.

I exorcise † thee, creature of medicine, by the Physician of eternal salvation, who restored the human race to health from eternal death by His own blood; by Him who, for the healing of mankind, bestowed medicinal virtue upon herbs, stones, and various creatures, so that there may be no communication between thee and the Apostate Angels. Instead, by the power of our Lord Jesus Christ, expel from thyself all the deceits of the enemy: and just as thou art a medicinal composition made from various materials to heal God's creatures—and especially those harassed by the Devil—so mayest thou retain thy natural power and receive supernatural power, being adjured in the power of God the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit, who destroys all evil crafts <term>maleficia — harmful magic, sorcery, or "black magic"</term> and reigns forever and ever. Amen.

<header>THE COMPLETION OF THE CRAFT</header>

# THE BLESSING OF THE OIL,
->*To be performed solemnly before the Altar.*<-

> Our help † is in the name of the Lord.
> Who made heaven and earth.

## EXORCISM.

I exorcise † you, creature <note>In a liturgical context, "creature" refers to any created thing—such as water, salt, or oil—being addressed before it is consecrated for holy use.</note> of oil, by † God the Father almighty, who created Heaven and earth and all things that are in them: by † His most wise son Jesus Christ, through whom all things were set in order with wisdom: by the † Holy Spirit, through whom we obtain goodness and mercy: by † Mary the Virgin, the vanquisher of Demons, by the † Holy Angels, the garrison of the Highest King: by the † Patriarchs of Israel: by the † Prophets, the oracles of the Holy Spirit: by the † holy Apostles, the judges of the world, by the Evangelists, the faithful Chancellors of Christ: by the † Innocents, the flowers of the Martyrs: by the † Holy Martyrs, fervent in love: by the Holy Confessors, the lights of the world: by the holy Doctors <note>The "Doctors" refers to the great teachers and theologians of the early Church.</note>, the trumpets of the Holy Spirit: by the † Monks, the seeds of blessing, by the † holy Hermits, who despise the world: by the † holy Priests, givers of Christ’s holy things <note>original: "dentes" — literally "teeth," a medieval metaphor for preachers who "chew" and prepare the Word for the people, though likely a scribal error for "dantes" (givers).</note>, by the holy Levites <note>A traditional term for Deacons.</note>, the keepers of the commandments: by the † holy Virgins, the brides of Christ: by the † holy Widows, the mirrors of conduct: so that all the power of the Devil, every army of the adversary, every assault, every swelling, and pain, and phantom of Satan and his ministers may be uprooted and flee from those who drink of you, or anoint themselves with you. May you destroy and consume all diabolical spells <term>maleficia — harmful magic or curses</term>, and may you be made an excellent and holy medicine, restoring health to mind and body; let the Demons not be able to hide themselves within those bodies, but by the power of the most mighty name of Jesus let them manifest themselves, and grant obedience to the Ministers of Jesus Christ, and depart along with all their evil spells. In the name of the most holy Trinity, of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit, and of all the saints, Amen.

<summary>This page contains a formal ritual for exorcising and blessing oil, calling upon a long list of heavenly figures to imbue the oil with the power to expel demons and heal illness. It specifies that the oil can be either ingested or applied topically to destroy "maleficia" (evil spells).</summary>

### Antiphon.

Then Samuel took a small flask of oil, and poured it over his head, and kissed him. <note>original: "Tulit autem Samuel lenticulam olei..." This refers to 1 Samuel 10:1, where the prophet Samuel anoints Saul as the first king of Israel, establishing the biblical precedent for using oil to set someone or something apart for a holy purpose.</note>

### The Song of Moses, Deuteronomy 32.

Hear, O Heavens, the things I speak: let the earth hear the words of my mouth.
Let my doctrine gather as the rain, let my speech flow as the dew.
As the shower upon the herb, and as the drops upon the grass: because I will invoke the name of the Lord.
Give magnificence to our God: the works of God are perfect, and all His ways are judgments.
God is faithful, and without any iniquity: He is just and upright. They have sinned against Him, and are no longer His children in their filthiness. <note>This refers to the Israelites' occasional falls into idolatry or sin, which the exorcist uses here to emphasize the need for purification.</note>
A crooked and perverse generation: is this how you repay the Lord, O foolish and unwise people?
Is He not your Father: who possessed you, and made you, and created you?
Remember the days of old: think upon every generation.
Ask your father, and he will declare it to you: ask your elders, and they will tell you.
When the Most High divided the nations: when He separated the sons of Adam.
He appointed the bounds of the peoples: according to the number of the children of Israel.
But the Lord's portion is His people: Jacob is the cord <note>original: "funiculus" — a measuring cord used to mark out an inheritance or plot of land.</note> of His inheritance.
He found him in a desert land: in a place of horror, and of vast solitude.
He led him about, and taught him: and He kept him as the apple of His eye. <note>original: "pupillam oculi sui" — literally "the pupil of his eye."</note>
As the eagle enticing her young to fly: and hovering over them.
He spread His wings, and took him: and carried him on His shoulders.
The Lord alone was his Leader: and there was no strange god with him.
He set him over the high land, that he might eat the fruits of the fields.

<header>COMPLEMENTUM ARTIS</header>
<meta>catchword: And I said</meta>

That he might suck honey from the rock: and oil from the hardest stone. <note>original: "Ut sugeret mel de petra." This imagery suggests that God provides sustenance and anointing even from the most unlikely or difficult sources.</note>

Butter from the herd, and milk from the sheep, with the fat of lambs, and of rams of the breed of Bashan. <note>Bashan was a region famous for its rich pastureland and sturdy livestock.</note>

And goats with the marrow of wheat: and that they might drink the purest blood of the grape. <note>original: "sanguinem vuæ... meraciſſimum." A poetic reference to wine, here used to symbolize the richness of God's gifts.</note>

The beloved grew fat, and kicked: he grew fat, thick, and bloated. <note>original: "Incraſſatus eſt dilectus." "The beloved" (Jeshurun) is a poetic name for the people of Israel, here described as becoming rebellious through over-indulgence and prosperity.</note>

He abandoned God his maker: and departed from God his savior.

They provoked Him with strange gods: and stirred Him to anger with abominations.

They sacrificed to demons, and not to God: to gods whom they did not know. <note>original: "Immolaverunt Dæmoniis." In a manual for blessing oils or performing exorcisms, this verse serves as a reminder of the spiritual warfare between divine grace and demonic influence.</note>

New and recent ones have come: whom their fathers did not worship.

You have abandoned the God who begot you, and have forgotten the Lord your creator.

The Lord saw it, and was moved to anger: because His sons and daughters provoked Him.

And He said, I will hide my face from them: and I will consider their final end.

For it is a perverse generation: and children without faith.

They have provoked me with that which was not God, and angered me with their vanities.

And I will provoke them with those who are not a people: and I will anger them with a foolish nation.

A fire is kindled in my wrath: and it shall burn even to the lowest depths of hell. <note>original: "uſque ad inferni noviſſima." In this biblical context, "hell" refers to the depths of the underworld or the grave (Sheol).</note>

And it shall devour the earth with its growth: and burn the foundations of the mountains.

I will heap evils upon them, and I will spend my arrows against them.

They shall be consumed with famine: and birds shall devour them with a most bitter bite.

I will send the teeth of beasts upon them: with the fury of those that crawl upon the earth, and of serpents.

Without, the sword shall lay them waste, and within, there shall be dread: the young man and the maiden alike, the suckling with the old man.

And I said, where are they? I will make the memory of them cease from among men.

But I delayed because of the wrath of the enemies: lest perhaps their foes should grow proud.

And say, "Our own high hand, and not the Lord, has done all these things."

They are a nation without counsel, and without wisdom: <note>original: "Gens absq; consilio est." This refers to those who have abandoned divine wisdom for the folly of false idols.</note> O, that they were wise, and understood, and would provide for their final end!

How could one man pursue a thousand: and two put ten thousand to flight?

Was it not because their God had sold them: and the Lord had shut them up?

For our God is not like their gods: even our enemies are the judges of this.

Their vine is of the vineyard of Sodom: and of the suburbs of Gomorrah. <note>Referencing the infamous cities of the plain, these metaphors signify total moral and spiritual corruption.</note>

Their grapes are grapes of gall: and their clusters most bitter.

Their wine is the gall of dragons: and the incurable venom of asps. <note>original: "Fel Draconum... venenum aspidum." In the context of exorcism, this describes the "poisonous" nature of demonic influence or sin, which only divine power can neutralize.</note>

Are not these things stored up with me: and sealed away in my treasures?

Vengeance is mine, and I will repay them in due time: so that their foot shall slide.

The day of destruction is at hand: and the times hasten to arrive.

The Lord will judge His people: and will have mercy on His servants.

He will see that their hand has grown weak: and those who were shut up have failed, and the remainder are consumed.

And He will say, "Where are their gods: in whom they placed their trust?"

"Of whose sacrifices they ate the fat: and drank the wine of their drink offerings?"

"Let them arise, and help you: and protect you in your time of need."

See now that I alone am: and there is no other God besides me. <note>original: "ego sim solus." This is a central declaration of monotheistic power, used in rites to command spirits to acknowledge the one true God.</note>

I will kill, and I will make to live; I will strike, and I will heal: and there is no one who can deliver out of my hand. <note>This verse emphasizes God's absolute authority over life, death, and spiritual affliction.</note>

### of Leviticus.

When a soul offers an offering of sacrifice to the Lord, his offering shall be of fine flour <term>fine flour: "simila," the highest quality of wheat flour, used specifically for sacred offerings</term>: and he shall pour oil upon it: and put frankincense <term>frankincense: "thus," an aromatic resin burned to create a "pleasing aroma" during rituals</term> on it, and bring it to the sons of Aaron the Priest: one of whom shall take a handful of the fine flour, and of the oil, with all the frankincense, and shall place it as a memorial <note>original: "memoriale" — the portion of the offering actually burned on the altar to represent the whole gift to God.</note> upon the Altar for a most sweet odor to the Lord: But that which remains of the sacrifice shall belong to Aaron and his sons, a holy of holies <note>original: "sanctum sanctorum" — the most sacred portion, reserved only for the priests.</note> from the offerings of the Lord. And when you offer a sacrifice cooked in an oven of fine flour, namely loaves without leaven <note>original: "absque fermento" — leaven or yeast was often excluded from sacrifices as a symbol of purity or haste.</note> mixed with oil, and unleavened wafers <term>wafers: "Lagana azyma," thin, flat breads</term> smeared with oil: If your offering be from a frying pan, of fine flour mixed with oil, and without leaven, you shall divide it into small pieces: and pour oil upon it. But if the sacrifice be from a grill, the fine flour shall likewise be mixed with oil,

## READING FROM THE EPISTLE OF SAINT
## James the Apostle.

Is any among you sick? Let him bring in the <term>priests</term> <note>original: "Presbyteros." While it literally means "elders," in this liturgical context it refers to the ordained clergy called to perform the rite of anointing.</note> of the Church, and let them pray over him, anointing him with oil in the name of the Lord: and the prayer of faith shall save the sick man, and the Lord shall raise him up; and if he be in sins, they shall be forgiven him. Confess therefore your sins to one another, and pray for one another, that you may be saved: for the continual prayer of a righteous man avails much. Elijah was a man subject to the same <term>passions</term> <note>original: "passibilis." This implies being subject to human suffering and emotions just as we are.</note> as we are, and he prayed a prayer that it might not rain upon the earth, and it did not rain for three years and six months. And he prayed again, and the heavens gave rain, and the earth gave its fruit.

But you, O Lord, have mercy on us.
Thanks be to God.

And that I may dwell in the house of the Lord for length of days.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: As it was in the beginning, is now and ever shall be, world without end. Amen.

## A READING FROM THE HOLY GOSPEL
## according to Luke.

At that time, Jesus taking it up, said: A certain man went down from Jerusalem to Jericho, and fell among robbers, who also stripped him, and having inflicted wounds upon him, went away leaving him half-dead. And it happened that a certain Priest went down the same way, and seeing him, passed by. Similarly, a Levite also, when he was near the place and saw him, passed by. But a certain <term>Samaritan</term> <note>Samaritans were traditionally outsiders to the Jewish community; here, he represents the healing mercy of Christ.</note> making a journey, came near him, and seeing him was moved with mercy. And approaching, he bound up his wounds, pouring on oil and wine: and setting him upon his own beast, he led him into an inn, and took care of him. And on the next day he took out two <term>denarii</term> <note>original: "denarios." A standard silver coin of the era, representing a fair wage for several days of care.</note> and gave them to the innkeeper, and said: "Take care of him, and whatever you spend over and above, I will repay you when I return."
But you, O Lord, have mercy on us.
Thanks be to God.

# BLESSING OF THE GARMENTS.

->Our help † is in the name of the Lord.<-
->Who made Heaven and earth.<-

## EXORCISM.

I exorcise † you, various creatures of clothing, by him † who prepared you for the beauty and adornment of the human race: by him † who clothed the birds of the sky, the beasts of the earth, and the fish of the sea: by him † who through Moses made holy garments for the glory and beauty of Aaron <note>A reference to the elaborate priestly vestments described in the Book of Exodus.</note>: and clothed Solomon’s temple with cedar and olive wood, and the purest gold, and adorned it everywhere with pictures and most precious ornaments: so that you may be pure garments, blessed † and clean from every stain, power, and spot of the most filthy Satan, and that you may in no way be able to retain the operations and strengths of the enemy himself: but be garments blessed and sanctified † by him, who, clothed in humanity <note>original: "cinctus humanitate" — literally "girded with humanity," referring to the Incarnation of Christ.</note> so that he might redeem the human race, subjected himself to death, and now, clothed in immortality and glory, lives and reigns forever and ever. Amen.

Almighty, eternal God, who among other wonders has decorated your creatures with various ornaments: and you made garments of skin for Adam and Eve: and you surround us with the garment of mercy in this world, and with the robe of glory as we enter into your Kingdom: We ask that you deign to purify, † cleanse, † and bless † these garments for the sake of the love of your name. I purify, † cleanse, † and bless † them in your name, so that you may deign to clothe this your creature, <note>The letter "N." serves as a placeholder where the priest would speak the name of the specific person receiving the blessing.</note> N., as if with a humble armor, in varied and subtle colors: to shoe them in hyacinth <note>original: "hyacinto" — likely referring to a blue or violet dyed fabric or leather, often associated with the high priest's garments in the Bible.</note>, and gird them with fine linen <note>original: "bysso" — a rare, high-quality ancient fabric made from silk-like flax or sea-silk.</note>: and to place bracelets on their hands, a necklace around their neck, rings in their ears, and a crown of beauty upon their head. Let them be adorned with gold and silver, and clothed in multi-colored embroidery: so that they may be exceedingly beautiful before you, their best and greatest God. And when you sense the fragrance of these garments, give to this your servant N., your daughter, the kiss of peace, consolation, and blessing. Grant, Lord, that the scent of these garments may be like the scent of incense: and may those who carry and wear them be able to receive the fullness of heavenly grace and the protection of your blessing within them, so that—defended and unharmed from all the enemies' spells, incantations, bindings, <term>signatures</term> <note>Magical symbols or "characters" believed to have been inscribed by sorcerers to cause harm.</note>, and manufactured charms and snares—they may be able to glorify you forever. Through Christ our Lord, who lives and reigns with you, etc.

# BLESSING
### *of Herbs.*

->Our help † is in the name of the Lord.<-

## EXORCISM,

Almighty God, who in the creation of the universe said among your wonderful works: "Let the earth bring forth the green herb that produces seed, and the fruit-bearing tree making fruit according to its kind, whose seed is in itself upon the earth," and you gave them to us for food and use because they were very good: So, for the sake of your holy, laudable, and blessed name—great and glorified in Heaven and on earth—place your right hand...

...full with a generous blessing † upon these herbs, [Name], and fill them with all blessings, just as I, in your holy name, Jehovah, exorcize † bless, † and sanctify them, so that wherever they may be placed, whether in wool, or feathers, or straw, or beds, or in houses, chambers, dwellings, and in any places whatsoever, or if someone has them upon themselves, or carries them, or if anyone has been fumigated <note>The practice of "fumigation" involved burning herbs so the patient could inhale the smoke or let it pass over their skin for spiritual or physical cleansing.</note> by them, or washed in a bath with them, through your omnipotence and the power of the most bitter passion of our Lord Jesus Christ your son, let every evil be distanced from them. Let every danger, all powers and works of Satan be removed, just as the Heaven is distanced from the earth: Light from darkness: and truth from lies. 

Nor shall our adversaries themselves <note>The "adversaries" refers to demons or malevolent spirits.</note> have the power of hiding, of harming, of suppressing the truth <note>original: "veritatem tacendi," literally "keeping silent about the truth."</note>, nor of remaining in these creatures and places: and therefore, through Him who gives the herb for the service of men <note>A reference to Psalm 104:14: "He causeth the grass to grow for the cattle, and herb for the service of man."</note>, and through the power of His most holy name, the Tetragrammaton <term>Tetragrammaton: The four-letter Hebrew name of God (YHWH), considered in the Western magical tradition to be a word of supreme power.</term>, and through His own voice saying: *Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do,* I bless these herbs [Name] † and sanctify † them to drive away demons. 

I do this to destroy, annihilate, extirpate, and eradicate every evil spell <term>maleficium: Harmful magic or sorcery intended to cause injury or illness.</term>, incantation, bindings <term>ligationes: Magical "knots" or ligatures believed to physically restrain a person or cause impotence.</term>, signatures <term>signaturas: Occult marks, characters, or "seals" used by sorcerers to claim authority over a person or object.</term>, and fabrications <term>facturas: Physical objects or charms "fashioned" by a witch or sorcerer to hold a curse.</term> from human bodies, from other creatures, and from any places whatsoever. Let every diabolical work already done, or yet to be done, be undone, so that no dwelling and no power of the enemy may remain there. 

Instead, in the name of Jesus—whose most holy name, virtue, and power I invoke over these herbs—let the cursed ones recede, and let them go headlong in confusion into the abyss <note>original: "barathro" — a Greek-derived term for a deep pit or the underworld.</note>, into Tartarus <note>A mythological abyss used in late Latin to describe the deepest regions of Hell.</note>, into Gehenna <note>A biblical term for the place of fiery punishment.</note>, and into hell with all their heretical works. And let the most holy Angels of God dwell there instead, so that they may receive health. 

And just as the herb sprouts from the earth by rains, so may the dew from heaven and the power of the Holy Spirit rain upon these herbs; and through His holy blessing † may they become green herbs and rich pastures upon the mountains of Israel. Seek, eternal God, that which was lost: bring back that which was cast away, bind up what was broken, and strengthen what is weak: Guard what is strong and fat, and feed your sheep, and make them lie down in the bosom of Abra[ham]...

<header>COMPLEMENT OF THE ART</header>

# BLESSING OF THE FIRE, FOR *performing fumigations, and for burning the names of Demons, as well as their images and the tools of their witchcraft.*

->Our help † is in the name of the Lord.<-
->Who made Heaven and earth.<-

## EXORCISM.

> I exorcise † you, creature of fire, by your God † and the Creator of the entire universe, who rained down sulfur and fire upon Sodom and Gomorrah and overthrew those cities and all their inhabitants: by Him from whom fire went forth and devoured Nadab and Abihu <note>The sons of Aaron who were consumed by divine fire for offering "strange fire" in the Tabernacle (Leviticus 10:1-2)</note>: and again [by Him who] devoured the murmuring people at the furthest part of the camp: by Him † who established you in Hell to torture sinners, upon whom He rained snares, fire, sulfur, and scorching winds, so that you may become a creature purged and blessed from every stain of the enemy; and that you may retain within yourself all the power and virtue of burning,

## IF FOR FUMIGATION, SAY:

> To restrain the pride of the cursed Demons, so that through this fumigation <note>The act of creating ritual smoke by burning blessed substances</note>, you may emit a smoke so horrible and dark that, with all its force and fury, there may be upon the Demons vexing the servant of God N. <note>The letter "N." serves as a placeholder where the name of the person being exorcised is spoken</note> every possible confusion, bitterness of pain, stenches, the height of all iniquities and torments, calamities, distresses, shatterings, sorrows, floods of every crime, swords, spears, arrows, and striking lightning bolts, inextinguishable blazes, fire, sulfur, and the wrath of God; so that, remembering the most intolerable tortures and pains of Hell itself, they may offer obedience to me, the Exorcist, by divine power: and finally, confused, they may prepare to flee from the fumigated places and from the possessed bodies. Through Him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

<note>Vocabulary: Blessing of the fire, Exorcism, fumigation, Demon, Sodom, Gomorrah, Nadab, Abihu, Hell, Exorcist</note>

<summary>This page contains a formal ritual to bless and exorcise fire, empowering it to either burn demonic objects or produce a "horrible smoke" intended to torment and drive away possessing spirits. It utilizes numerous biblical precedents of divine fire—both as a tool of purification and a weapon of judgment—to command the fire's spiritual efficacy.</summary>
<keywords>exorcism of fire, ritual fumigation, spiritual warfare, demonic names, Sodom and Gomorrah, Nadab and Abihu, hellfire, liturgical prayer, cleansing fire</keywords>

# FOR THE BURNING
of the names and images of Demons, say:

> That you <note>Addressing the blessed fire</note> may so twist, torture, and burn the figures and names of Demons thrown into you and to be consumed by you, as if their very substances were being tormented and tortured among the horrors and confusions of Gehenna <note>original: "gehennæ"; a term for Hell derived from the Valley of Hinnom, a place of perpetual fire</note>. Through Him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

# FOR BURNING THE INSTRUMENTS
of witchcraft <note>original: "maleficialibus"; refers to physical objects believed to be enchanted or used to cast spells</note>, have the instrument of witchcraft and a sword prepared before the Altar, clothed at least in a priestly stole; destroy it in this manner, as follows.

> Judas, when he had received a band of soldiers, and officials from the High Priests and Pharisees, came there with lanterns and torches and weapons. Jesus therefore, knowing all things that were to come upon him, went forward and said to them: "Whom do you seek?" They answered him: "Jesus of Nazareth." Jesus said to them: "I am he." And Judas also, who betrayed him, stood with them. As soon then as he said to them, "I am he," they went backward and fell to the ground.

->the instrument of witchcraft once with a<-
->hammer, then continue.<-

> Then Simon Peter, having a sword, drew it and struck the servant of the High Priest, and cut off his right ear. Now the servant’s name was Malchus.

## THE EXORCIST SHALL STRIKE TWICE
->the instrument of witchcraft, and continue.<-

> Then Judas, who betrayed him, seeing that he was condemned, repented and brought back the thirty pieces of silver to the chief priests and the elders, saying: "I have sinned in betraying innocent blood." But they said: "What is that to us? See you to it." And throwing down the pieces of silver in the temple, he departed; and going away, he hanged himself with a noose.

# HE SHALL STRIKE THREE TIMES, AS ABOVE.
*Then let him say:*

But Jesus, crying out again with a loud voice, yielded up his spirit. And behold, the veil of the temple was torn in two parts, from the top even to the bottom: and the earth shook, and the rocks were split. <note>original: Matthew 27:50-51; the tearing of the veil and splitting of rocks are used here as a liturgical metaphor for the breaking of a spell's power.</note>

### HERE THE EXORCIST SHALL CUT INTO THE INSTRUMENT
of witchcraft itself <note>original: "instrumentum ipsum maleficii"; the physical object, such as a charm, doll, or written curse, believed to be the source of the affliction.</note>, then deliver it to the blessed fire, observing the manner and form in the twelfth teaching.

And just as you are now about to turn these heretical and witchcraft instruments—which vex God’s creatures—into smoke and reduce them to nothing, so in the power of the Lord of hosts, may their operations and diabolical harassments vanish; and may all witchcraft, incantations, bindings <note>original: "ligaturæ"; magical knots or spells intended to "bind" a person's will or health</note>, signatures <note>original: "signaturæ"; occult symbols or demonic marks</note>, and manufactured charms <note>original: "facturæ"; physical objects "made" or constructed for a specific magical purpose</note> be eradicated from all the members of those bewitched, be broken, and be annihilated, yet without spiritual or physical injury to them. Through Him who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire. Amen.

->Let us pray.<-

And finally, being wrapped in most atrocious pains, terrified, and confused, let them flee from our face, and go headlong to the places appointed for them, or descend into the lowest depths of hell, having been overcome, according to the good pleasure of your divine Majesty. ### Psalm 67 <note>In the Catholic Vulgate Bible used by the author, this is Psalm 67; in modern English Bibles like the King James or NRSV, it is numbered as Psalm 68. It was chosen here for its themes of God scattering His enemies.</note>

Let God arise, and let his enemies be scattered: and let them that hate him flee from before his face.

As smoke vanishes, let them vanish: as wax melts before the fire, so let the wicked perish at the presence of God.

And let the just feast, and rejoice before God: and be delighted with gladness.

Sing to God, sing a psalm to his name: make a way for him who ascends upon the west: the Lord is his name.

Rejoice in his presence, they shall be troubled before his face: the father of orphans, and the Judge of widows.

God is in his holy place: God, who makes those of one manner dwell in a house. <note>original: "unius moris"; this implies people of one mind or character dwelling in harmony.</note>

Who leads out the prisoners in strength, and likewise those who provoke him, who dwell in the tombs.

O God, when you went out in the sight of your people, when you passed through the desert.

The earth was moved, for the heavens dropped dew before the face of the God of Sinai: before the face of the God of Israel.

You shall set aside for your inheritance a free rain, O God: and it was weakened, but you have made it perfect.

Your creatures <note>original: "Animalia"; refers here to the "flock" or "living things" of God's people.</note> shall dwell in it: in your sweetness, O God, you have provided for the poor.

The Lord shall give the word to them that preach the gospel with great power.

The King of the powers of the beloved, of the beloved: and for the beauty of the house to divide the spoils.

If you sleep among the midst of the lots <note>original: "inter medios cleros"; a famously difficult phrase in the Latin Vulgate, often interpreted as "sheepfolds" or "boundaries of inheritance."</note>, the wings of the dove are covered with silver, and the hinder parts of her back with the paleness of gold.

While the heavenly King distinguishes kings over her, they shall be made white with snow in Salmon <note>original: "Selmon"; a mountain mentioned in Judges 9:48, used here as a metaphor for spiritual purity or victory.</note>: the mountain of God, a fat mountain.

# EXORCISM.

This is a placeholder for the name of the specific substance being used, such as frankincense, sulfur, or rue.</note>, by him who touches the mountains and they smoke: by him who, descending upon the smoking Mount Sinai, terrified and struck the people with fear: so that you may not have the power to retain the strength of the Devil within you. Rather, may you be made a strong creature and a powerful fumigation for the burning, torturing, and driving away of demons who vex God's creatures. In the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit. Amen.

Almighty eternal God, we humbly pray to you, that you might deign to strengthen this creature of fumigation [Name of Substance], which in your holy name I purify, † cleanse, † bless, † and sanctify † with your holy power: And just as when Mount Sinai was smoking the people were terrified and shaken; and as by the liver of the fish through the Archangel Raphael and Tobias—when placed upon living coals and smoking—the demon was banished into the desert of upper Egypt. <note>This refers to a story from the Book of Tobit where the Archangel Raphael instructs Tobias to burn a fish's heart and liver to drive away the demon Asmodeus.</note>

O God of Abraham, God of Isaac, God of Jacob, who willed that the triumphal Title of the holy Cross—"Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews"—be written upon it, <note>original: "Titulus." This refers to the "INRI" sign placed above Jesus during the Crucifixion, which was often viewed in the Middle Ages and Renaissance as a powerful protective charm.</note> and who wrote the name of the same Jesus in golden letters in the heart of Saint Ignatius: <note>referring to St. Ignatius of Antioch, of whom legend says that after his martyrdom, the name of Jesus was found inscribed in gold upon his heart.</note> we beseech You: that You deign to bless † and sanctify † this creature <note>term: "creature" — in a liturgical context, this refers to any created thing (like water, salt, or here, paper) being addressed in its natural state before it is consecrated for holy use.</note> and ink. May anyone who devoutly carries upon them prayers or holy names written with these materials, or if they are placed among feathers, wool, and straw, <note>It was a common folk belief that "hexes" or "charms" were hidden by witches inside the stuffing of pillows and mattresses (feathers/straw) to cause illness or nightmares.</note> or in beds, houses, and garments, or wherever they may be situated, may the Demons be unable to lurk there. Instead, let them be forced to give a real sign of their presence, and let these materials be a holy and blessed armor against Demons, powerful in the destruction of all witchcrafts, incantations, bindings, <note>term: "ligationum" (bindings) — a type of magic intended to "bind" a person’s health, virility, or luck.</note> magical signs, <note>original: "signaturarum."</note> hexes, <note>original: "facturarum."</note> fascinations, <note>term: "fascinationum" — the "evil eye" or a spell cast by a look.</note> and all invisible enemies. May they have no power to disquiet or disturb Your creatures redeemed by the most precious blood of Your Son, so that they may [be able]...

## BLESSING OF THE STOLE FOR
### *binding Demons.*

-> Our help † is in the name of the Lord. <-
-> Who made Heaven and earth. <-

-> L E T  U S  P R A Y. <-

Almighty eternal God, who gave us power over serpents and scorpions <note>A reference to Luke 10:19, where Jesus gives his disciples authority over spiritual enemies</note>, we invoke your most powerful, terrible, and formidable holy name upon this Stole, which you may deign to bless † consecrate, † and infuse with the strength of your power †: You who are the Lord of hosts, the Lord strong and mighty in battle: and just as you sent the holy Angel from Heaven, who with a great chain seized the great ancient Dragon, who is the Devil and Satan, and bound him for a thousand years so that he might not seduce the nations; so may you deign to send Michael the Archangel, Prince of the heavenly host, so that he may bind the demons harassing those creatures redeemed by the precious blood of Jesus Christ at the touch of this sacred stole. Let it be done so that their hands and feet are weighed down with shackles, so that they may reveal their presence to your ministers, offer obedience, obey their commands, and be forced to stand in the sight of the Exorcists <note>The term "Exorcistae" refers to the specific minor order or office of those authorized to cast out demons</note> with trembling, groaning, sorrow, fear, dread, and terror in His holy name. Through the same Jesus Christ our Lord. Amen.

I bless you † therefore, O sacred Stole, I consecrate you † and I invoke divine power upon you, † so that you may be a Stole of weeping, a Stole of trampling <note>original: "conculcationis," referring to the act of treading underfoot or crushing an enemy</note>, † a Stole of terror, a stole of pressure, a stole of sorrow, a stole of dread, a stole of groaning and pain for all demons harassing human bodies.

<header>SECOND PART OF THE EXORCISMS.</header>

So that, just as Jesus Christ on the cross put a bridle <note>original: "frœnum"; a piece of tack used to control a horse, here used metaphorically for spiritual restraint</note> on Lucifer and his followers: So, O sacred Stole, may you be a bridle to all demons—surpassing the bridle of the horse, the mule, the ass, and all beasts of burden—so that at your touch, they may immediately reveal their presence, offer obedience to the Ministers of God, and not be able to kick back <note>original: "recalcitrare"; to resist or refuse to move, like a stubborn animal</note> in any way or manner. May you, O blessed Stole, be a most powerful bridle and a bit <note>original: "orisipula"; a specific technical term for the part of a bridle that enters the mouth</note> in their mouths, and on their lips and jaws: powerful, terrible, and restraining the proud, so that they may fall in our sight when the name of Almighty God is invoked, just as the walls of Jericho fell. May you be a chain woven with wondrous works and most powerful bonds, acting like the powerful and strong hand of the Lord of Hosts over their pride and arrogance. May you be a single chain through the merits of the one holy † cross of our Lord Jesus Christ; may you be a triple chain through the power of the holy nails of Christ; may you be a fivefold chain through the merits of the five wounds of Christ; may you be a sevenfold chain through the seven words spoken by Christ on the cross; may you be a manifold chain through the lashings given to Christ. May you be a bridle of the blood coming forth from the winepress <note>original: "lacu"; literally a lake or vat, often referring to the "winepress of God's wrath" or the vessel of Christ's sacrifice</note> to all unclean spirits, so that they may stand trembling in the presence of the exorcists of the Holy Church; and at the touch or binding of this sacred Stole, they may immediately reveal themselves and be bound to obey the exorcists' commands; and let there be no one who offers them help: Through Christ our Lord.

I bless † you, and I consecrate † you by him who ordered golden chains of the purest gold to be made for the breastplate <note>original: "rationali"; referring to the "Breastplate of Judgment" worn by the High Priest in the Old Testament, signifying divine authority</note>: By † him who broke the chains on the necks of our enemies. By † him who constrains the jaws of demons with a muzzle <note>original: "chamo"</note> and bridle, so that they may not be able to harm anyone. By † him who walks upon the Asp and the Basilisk <note>Names of venomous serpents or legendary monsters used in the Bible to represent the powers of evil</note>, and tramples the Lion and the Dragon. By † him who scatters the nations that desire wars. By † him who ordered the man not having a wedding garment to be bound and cast into the outer darkness. By † him who alone does great wonders. By † him who was captured by the Jews, bound, scourged, spat upon, crowned with thorns, and killed on the cross. By † all things which he did, said...
