...and Christ fulfilled it, so that you may be a blessed † and consecrated † stole <note>original: "stola"; the primary vestment signifying priestly authority</note> to tame the pride and arrogance of stubborn demons: So that at your touch, they may prostrate themselves, manifest their presence, offer obedience to the [exorcists], <note>original: "exordestis"; likely a printer's error for "exorcistis"</note> confess the truth, and finally flee, to the praise of Almighty God, the salvation of men, and the confusion of all demons. In the name of the Father † and of the Son † and of the Holy † Spirit, Amen.

Again, I invoke upon you the power of the double chain of Saint Peter, prince of the Apostles: to whom, and to his colleagues, was most powerfully given by the Lord of virtues the power of treading upon serpents and scorpions and of casting out demons, and the free faculty and authority of binding and loosing the souls of the faithful from the bonds of sins: and by the chains and bonds with which the holy martyrs of God were bound while, bearing witness to Jesus Christ, they bravely endured lashings, punishments from tyrants, torments, passions, and death, so that they might become heirs in the house of God, that you may bind the demon or demons vexing N. <note>The letter "N." serves as a placeholder where the priest would speak the name of the person being exorcised</note>, this servant of God, in the name of God the Fa † ther Almighty, in the name of Je † sus † Christ † our Savior and the Ho † ly † Spirit the Paraclete: <term>Paraclete|A title for the Holy Spirit derived from Greek, meaning "Advocate" or "Helper"</term> and that you may completely absolve <note>original: "absolvas"; here meaning to set free or release</note> this creature of God from all the snares of evil spirits, and their bindings and all their chains, through the power of the holy † Cross. For you are a priestly ornament: you are like the sling of David against Goliath, you are the breastplate <note>original: "lorica"; a piece of armor covering the chest</note> of the holy ministers of God, you are the golden torque and necklace of the golden-fleeced lamb of the King of ages; you are, finally, that without which the priest blesses nothing, nor consecrates anything: you are that with which every minister of Christ, when clothed, blesses what is to be blessed, consecrates what is to be consecrated, curses what is to be cursed, exorcises what is to be exorcised, adjures what is to be adjured, and finally completes all ecclesiastical ministries. Therefore, when you touch the vexed creatures of God, may the confused evil spirits depart immediately.

Through the name of Jesus Christ the Nazarene. And may you be to us a stole of joy, a pure stole, a white stole, a stole of innocence, a stole of immortality, Amen.

# PRAYER.

> O God, whose nature it is always to have mercy and to spare, receive our prayer; so that your compassionate kindness may mercifully set free this creature of yours who is obsessed by a demon, as well as ourselves and all your servants who are bound by the chain of our sins. Through our Lord Jesus Christ, Who lives and reigns with you forever and ever, Amen.

# BLESSING
## Of the Rods for Scourging Demons. <note>original: "Virgarum ad flagellandum Dæmones"; these were physical switches or branches used by the exorcist to symbolically "strike" the spirit during the rite.</note>

-> Our help is in the name of the Lord. <-
-> Who made heaven and earth. <-

# EXORCISM.

> I exorcise you, creature of rods, by him † who created the green herb that produces seed, and the fruit-bearing wood that produces fruit according to its kind, so that you may have no communion with the apostate angels. Instead, may you be exorcised † and blessed † for the purpose of bruising and scourging Satan beneath our feet: so that every unclean spirit existing in any diabolical form, or within a body, shall be lashed by you; may it manifest itself immediately, obey the commanding Priest, and finally flee in confusion and depart from human bodies: Through Jesus Christ, who upon the wood of the holy Cross struck down Lucifer and all his followers. In the name of the Father † and of the Son † and of the Holy † Spirit, Amen.

# Let us pray.

> Almighty and eternal God, who in your bodily power subjects all bodies to yourself, and who transforms natural powers and wonderfully pours supernatural graces into your creatures according to the good pleasure of your divine majesty, so that we may know you alone as the omnipotent God who works wonders; I humbly beseech your Majesty that you might deign to bless † these exorcised rods. Bless them just as you blessed the rod of the Angel when he touched the meat and the unleavened bread: <note>A reference to Judges 6:21, where an angel consumed an offering with fire by touching it with a staff.</note> Sanctify them † just as you sanctified the rod

Thus, if anyone uses these rods in your holy name and lashes whatever forms of Demons or the harassed bodies themselves, may that scourging and striking—through the holy name <term>Tetragrammaton: The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH, considered the most powerful name in Judeo-Christian magic and exorcism</term>—be as if the renewal of all pains and infernal punishments were upon the very substances of the Demons: even as fire, sulfur, wrath, and the fury of the Lord.

And let them be rods punishing their iniquities.

### THE SECOND PART OF EXORCISTICS.

->Kkk 4<-

Your sorrow shall be turned back upon your own head: and your iniquity shall descend upon your own crown. <note>original: "in verticem tuam." Based on Psalm 7:16. The priest commands that the demon's malice fall back onto its own head.</note>

Destroy, O Lord, your enemy and the avenger: in the severity of your justice.

You have rebuked them, and the wicked one has perished: you have blotted out their name forever, and for age upon age.

Add to these your swords: and destroy their cities. <note>original: "frameas." This term refers to a spear or sword. In this context, it asks God to use divine weaponry against the "fortified places" or strongholds of the demons.</note>

Your memory has perished with a great sound, and the Lord remains forever.

Your own foot is caught in the snare that you have hidden.

Let sinners be turned back into hell: all those who forget God.

Whose mouth is full of cursing, and bitterness, and deceit: under their tongue is labor and sorrow.

You sit in ambush, that you may kill the innocent; you lie in wait in secret, like a lion in his den. <note>A common metaphor in exorcism where the demon is compared to a stalking predator waiting to strike at the soul.</note>

Break, O Lord, the arm of the sinner and the malicious one: in his own snare.

For he has bent his bow, he has prepared his arrows in the quiver: that he may shoot in the dark at those who are upright of heart.

He shall rain down snares upon sinners: fire, brimstone, and the spirit of storms shall be the portion of their cup. <note>original: "spiritus procellarum." This refers to a "scorching wind" or "tempestuous spirit," a traditional image of divine judgment.</note>

They have spoken false things, each one to his neighbor: they have spoken with deceitful lips in their mouths and hearts.

May the Lord destroy all your deceitful lips: and the tongue that speaks great things.

Their throat is an open grave: they have acted deceitfully with their tongues: the poison of asps is under their lips. <note>An "asp" is a venomous snake; the text compares the demon's lies to deadly poison.</note>

Whose mouth is full of cursing and bitterness: their feet are swift to shed innocent blood.

You have been brought to nothing in his sight, you malicious one: but he glorifies those who fear the Lord.

You have invaded this house: like a lion ready for its prey.

But arise, O Lord, rescue it from the wicked one, so that he may no longer trouble it. <note>In the original Latin, the house (domus) is referred to with feminine pronouns ("eam"). The priest treats the home as a vulnerable entity needing protection.</note>

<summary>This page contains a series of aggressive imprecations derived from the Psalms, used by the exorcist to attack the presence of a demon within a home. The ritual uses the demon's own described malice (lies, ambushes, and "poison") as the grounds for its divine eviction.</summary>

Surround these with the sorrows of death, and trouble them with the terrors of iniquity. <note>original: "Circunda hos doloribus mortis." Drawing from Psalm 18:4, the exorcist calls for the same spiritual agony to afflict the demon that it attempts to inflict on the human soul.</note>

The sorrows of hell have surrounded you; the snares of death have overtaken you.

The earth was moved and it trembled; the foundations of the mountains were troubled and shaken: because He is angry with you.

The Most High thundered from Heaven: and hail and coals of fire fell upon you. <note>original: "grando, & carbones ignis." A recurring image in these rites where divine judgment takes the form of a celestial storm.</note>

He sent forth His arrows and scattered you; He multiplied lightnings and troubled you.

The Lord will repay you according to your iniquity: and He will humble the eyes of the proud.

Pursue these our enemies, O Lord: and I shall overtake them.

Shatter these, O Jesus Christ: and in your name let them fall under my feet. <note>The text shifts here from quoting scripture to a direct command in the name of Christ, positioning the exorcist as a victorious soldier standing over a defeated foe.</note>

And destroy them; I shall not turn back until they are consumed.

And crush them like dust before the face of the wind: like the mud of the streets, blot them out.

Let your hand be found upon all our enemies: let your right hand find all those who hate us.

Make them as a furnace of fire in the time of your presence: and in your wrath trouble them, and let fire devour them.

Let the abyss open its mouth, and roaring, let it seize you. <note>original: "barathrum." A Greek-derived term for a deep pit or the underworld, used here to describe the mouth of Hell reaching up for the demon.</note>

Let your heart be like melting wax: in your iniquity and your pride.

Lift up your gates, and let the eternal gates be raised: because the King of Glory has entered. <note>original: "Rex gloriæ." This verse, traditionally used for Christ’s entry into Jerusalem or Heaven, is used here to command the demon to "open up" and leave the person because God has arrived.</note>

He has crushed you like a calf of Lebanon: and the Beloved like the son of unicorns. <note>original: "filius unicornium." In historical Latin Bibles, this refers to a powerful, wild horned animal (likely a wild ox), symbolizing irresistible divine strength.</note>

Be ashamed, O impious demons, and let yourselves be led down into hell: let your deceitful lips be made silent.

With bit and bridle, shatter the jaws of these demons: so that they do not come near us. <note>original: "In chamo, & fræno." A metaphor for total restraint and control, treating the demon like a wild animal that must be forcibly reigned in.</note>

The death of sinners is the worst: and those who hate the just man shall fail.

### THE SECOND PART OF THE EXORCISM. 889

Let the **Demons** be confounded: those who seek after our souls.
Let them be turned backward and put to shame: those who devise evils against us.
Let them become like dust before the face of the wind, and let the **Angel of the Lord** <note>original: "Angelus Domini." In this context, the Angel is invoked as a divine warrior driving the demons away.</note> drive them into a narrow place.
Let your way be darkness and slipperiness: and the **Angel of the Lord** pursuing you.
You have unsheathed the sword, **Demons**: you have bent your bow.
But let your sword enter into your own hearts: and let your bow be shattered.
You unjust ones shall be punished, and the offspring of the ungodly shall perish.
In you, O Lord, we shall toss our enemies with the <term>horn</term> <note>original: "cornu." A biblical metaphor for strength or power; to "toss with the horn" implies driving back an enemy like a powerful ox.</note>: and in your name we shall despise those rising up against us.
Shatter the bow, and break the weapons: and burn the shields with fire.
Why do you glory in malice, you who are powerful in iniquity?
All day long your tongue has devised injustice; like a sharp **razor** you have practiced deceit.
You have loved malice above kindness: iniquity more than speaking what is right.
You have loved all words of ruin: a deceitful tongue.
Therefore God will destroy you in the end; He will uproot you and lead you out of your <term>tabernacle</term> <note>original: "tabernaculo." Referring to the demon's temporary dwelling or "stronghold" within the possessed person.</note>: and your root from the land of the living.
Cast these down, and divide their tongues: because iniquity and injustice are in their midst.
Let death come upon you: and may you descend into **hell** while still living.
Hear, O God, and scatter those who contaminate your house: and lead them down into the pit of destruction.
They have prepared a snare for our feet: but you have given those lying in wait for us into reproach.

It concludes with a series of historical-biblical archetypes of defeated enemies (Midian, Sisera, etc.) to serve as precedents for the demon's own expulsion.</meta>

->### FOR THE COMPLETION OF ARTICLE XI.<-

As wax that melts, you shall be taken away; fire has fallen upon you, and you have not seen the Sun; so in His wrath, He shall swallow you up. <note>original: "Sicut cera, quæ fluit... sic in ira absorbet vos." Drawing from Psalm 58, this imagery suggests that the demon's "substance" is being liquified and consumed by divine judgment.</note>
The just man shall rejoice when he sees the vengeance: he shall wash his hands in your blood.
And You, O Lord, shall laugh them to scorn, and You shall bring them to nothing.
Scatter them by Your power: and cast them down, O Lord my protector.
Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered: and let those who hate Him flee from our face. <note>original: "à facie nostra." While the standard Psalm 68:1 says "from His face," the exorcist here adapts the liturgy to emphasize the demons fleeing from the presence of the gathered faithful.</note>
As smoke vanishes, let them vanish; as wax melts before the face of fire, so let the sinners <note>In this ritual context, "sinners" refers specifically to the fallen angels or demons.</note> perish before the face of the Lord.
You have ascended on high, O Lord: You have led captivity captive: You have received gifts among men.
Nonetheless, God shall shatter the heads of His enemies: the hairy crown of those who walk in their sins.
Scatter those who desire wars: they shall come bound from Egypt: Ethiopia shall stretch out her hands to God.
Pour out Your wrath upon them, and let the fury of Your anger overtake them.
Because they persecute us, whom You have struck: therefore, add pain to their wounds.
Add iniquity to their iniquity: so that they do not remain in this dwelling. <term>dwelling</term> <note>original: "habitaculo isto." This refers specifically to the body or soul of the possessed person which the demon is currently inhabiting.</note>
Let those who seek after our souls be confounded: and let them be caught in their own pride.
You who have shattered their powers—the bow, the shield, the sword, and the war—suppress them.
They have devised a malicious counsel against us: and they have conspired against Your <term>creature</term>. <note>original: "creaturam tuam." A standard term for a human being, emphasizing that the person is God's handiwork currently being marred by the demon.</note>
Therefore, do to them as You did to **Midian** and **Sisera**: as to **Jabin** at the brook of **Kishon**. <note>These refer to famous biblical victories in the Book of Judges where God intervened to destroy the oppressors of Israel.</note>
Make them like **Oreb**, and **Zeeb**, and **Zebah**, and **Zalmunna**.
O my God, make them like a **wheel**: and like stubble before the face of the wind. <note>original: "ut rotam." In biblical Hebrew imagery, this refers to "whirling dust" or "tumbleweeds" that are easily blown away.</note>

<meta>catchword: Sicut (As)</meta>

<summary>This page concludes Article XI of the exorcism rite, using a series of aggressive biblical imprecations from the Psalms to command the demon's dissolution. It invokes historical examples of defeated biblical enemies to symbolize the inevitable downfall of the possessing spirit.</summary>
<keywords>imprecations, Psalm 68, Sisera, Midian, spiritual warfare, divine wrath, dissolution, protection</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the liturgical assault against demonic entities, shifting from the general imagery of dissolution (wax and smoke) found on page 890 to specific biblical imprecations of fire, physical destruction, and confinement to the abyss. The text draws heavily from the "Imprecatory Psalms" to provide the exorcist with divine precedent for the crushing of enemies.</meta>

# EXORCISM PART TWO.

As the fire that burns the forest, and as the flame that sets the mountains ablaze. <note>original: "Sicut ignis, qui comburit Sylvam..." Drawn from Psalm 83, this imagery invokes a total, sweeping purification that leaves no refuge for the spirit.</note>
So shall You pursue and trouble them in Your wrath and in Your tempest.
Let them be confounded and trampled down forever and ever.
Place them in the lowest pit: in the darkness and the shadow of death. <note>original: "lacu inferiori." This refers to the *Abyssus* or the deep pit of hell, where the demon is commanded to return.</note>
You have humbled the proud one like one wounded; with the arm of Your strength, You have scattered Your enemies.
You shall walk upon the asp <term>asp: a venomous snake, often symbolizing hidden or "sneaky" demonic influence</term> and the basilisk, and You shall trample the lion and the dragon. <note>original: "Super aspidem ambulabis..." A core text of exorcism (Psalm 91), asserting the practitioner's authority over various forms of evil.</note>
Behold, you our enemies shall perish, and you shall be scattered, you who work iniquity.
Repay them for their iniquity; and in their malice, destroy them; destroy them, O Lord our God.
Deliver us from the arrow that flies by day, from the plague that walks in darkness, from the assault and the **Noonday Demon.** <note>original: "Daemonio meridiano." Historically interpreted by monks as the demon of acedia (spiritual apathy), in this context it refers to any overt demonic manifestation during the light of day.</note>
Fire shall go before You and shall set our enemies on fire round about.
He who acts proudly shall not dwell within my house; he who speaks wicked things has not prospered before my eyes. <note>The "house" here serves as a metaphor for both the physical location of the exorcism and the body of the afflicted person.</note>
In the morning, I slew all the sinners of the land, that I might destroy all workers of iniquity from the City of the Lord.
The Lord has shattered the gates of brass and broken the iron bars asunder.
He has rebuked the proud; cursed are they who turn aside from Your commandments.
The just Lord shall cut the necks of sinners; let all those who hate Zion <note>Zion here represents the community of the faithful or the sanctified soul.</note> be confounded and turned back.
Let them be as grass on the rooftops, which withers before it is plucked up.
Let burning coals fall upon you; may You cast them into the fire.
Destroy all those who trouble this house, O Lord, for we are Your servants.

It emphasizes the absolute authority of the divine over the "proud" spirits occupying the "house" of the afflicted.</summary>

### THE COMPLETION OF THE ART

Let their table become a snare before them, a trap, a stumbling block, and a retribution. <note>original: "Fiat mensa horum..." Derived from Psalm 69, this prayer asks that the very comforts and sustenance of the enemy—or the offerings given to a spirit—become the means of their downfall.</note>

Let their eyes be darkened so that they may not see, and keep their backs always bent. <note>To "bend the back" signifies permanent subjugation and the loss of the spirit's pride and strength.</note>

The wicked shall be destroyed from the earth, and the way of the ungodly shall be dark.

May the Lord set His face against you, so that you flee, even when no one pursues. <note>This invokes a state of divine terror and paranoia, suggesting the spirit will find no rest even in the absence of a direct attack.</note>

God has withered the roots of proud leaders and planted the humble in their place.

The Lord has overturned your lands and destroyed them even to the foundation.

He has withered and dispersed you; your memory has ceased in Heaven and on Earth.

The inheritance of the children of sinners shall perish, and with their seed, a persistence of reproach.

Strike the backs of these enemies, O Lord, and let those who hate You not rise up again.

Because of your sin, you have fallen into a curse, and your portion shall forever be in that curse.

The mourning of the damned is within their spirit, and the name of the wicked shall be blotted out.

The Lord has broken the staff of the wicked—the scepter of rulers—with indignation and an incurable wound. <note>original: "Contrivit Dominus baculum impiorum..." This draws from Isaiah 14:5, celebrating the fall of a tyrannical power, here applied to the demonic hierarchy.</note>

Let those who persecute me be confounded, but let me not be confounded; let them be terrified, but let me not be terrified. Bring upon them the day of affliction, and crush them, O Lord.

The Lord shall cast down great stones from above, rushing upon you until your total destruction.

The fury of the Lord is kindled against sinners, and He has stretched out His hand over you.

The sinners in Zion are terrified; fear has seized the **hypocrites.** <term>hypocrites: in this ritual context, it refers to those who pretend to holiness or hide their true, malicious nature under a false appearance.</term>

The mountains are troubled, and their corpses are made like dung in the middle of the streets.

The arrows of the Lord are sharp, and all His bows are drawn against you.

You have forgotten God your creator, and you have not remembered your strong helper.

<meta>This page continues a sequence of ritual maledictions and scriptural rebukes used during an exorcism. While the previous page focused on the physical subjugation of the enemy—breaking their staff and darkening their path—this section emphasizes the spirit's inevitable retreat, the hollowness of its pride, and the self-destructive nature of its own sins. It transitions from general warnings to specific biblical parallels of downfall, such as the fall of Babylon.</meta>

### THE SECOND PART OF THE ART OF EXORCISM

Flee far away, just as the dust of the mountains is carried off by the winds, and like a whirlwind before the storm. <note>This imagery emphasizes that the spirit has no more substance or weight than dust when confronted by divine power.</note>

Fallen, fallen is Babylon, and all the carved images of its gods have been crushed. <note>original: "Cecidit, cecidit Babylon." A common biblical reference (Isaiah 21:9) used here to signify the total collapse of the demon's authority and idols.</note>

Flee from the infernal fury: from the edge of His swords, from the edge of the imminent sword, from the face of the drawn bow, and from the heat of the heavy battle.

You shall eat the fruit of your own way, and you shall be filled to satiety with your own counsels. <note>A poetic way of saying the spirit will be forced to suffer the consequences of its own malicious advice and schemes.</note>

Your own iniquities shall seize the wicked one, and you shall be bound with the ropes of your sins.

You shall die because you lacked discipline, and in the greatness of your folly, you shall be deceived.

The treasures of your impiety are of no use; you have been made a foolish creature by me, and your name shall rot. <note>original: "creatura stulta à me facta es." This likely refers to the exorcist's ritual authority to redefine the spirit's status from a powerful entity to a "foolish creature" subject to the command of the practitioner.</note>

Among you proud ones there is always quarreling, and you shall be extinguished, for you are the lamps of the wicked.

Your way is an abomination to God; in your fruits there is only confusion and perdition.

The Lord will demolish the house of the proud, and the wicked one shall not be able to rejoice in the ruin of another.

When you go into the depth of your sins, all your wicked deeds shall follow you.

You have been scourged, O wicked witness, and because you speak lies, you shall die the death once again. <note>This refers to the "second death" or eternal damnation, suggesting that the spirit's current state is merely the beginning of its punishment.</note>

Mockery is prepared for those who are cursed, and hammers for striking down your pride. <note>The "hammer" is a frequent symbol in exorcism literature for the crushing weight of divine justice.</note>

Christ will scatter you with His gaze when He sits upon the throne of His judgment.

Your inheritance lacks any blessing, because you have returned evil for good.

You are called proud and arrogant, but there is no counsel that can stand against the Lord.

You who sow iniquity shall reap evils, and the rod of your anger shall be consumed.

A deep pit is your path, full of swords, and the Lord will cast you down into it.

You lie in wait like a thief on the road, but nettles shall fill you and thorns shall cover you.

### THE COMPLETION OF THE ART

In a good day you shall enjoy evils, and you shall not be able to flee the evil day. <note>This is a ritual reversal, suggesting that even when things seem "good," the spirit will find only suffering.</note>

You shall live in your malice, and in your evil work: so that you may be tormented forever.

Your strength shall be like the ash of flax, and your work like a spark: and both shall be set on fire together, and there will be no one to extinguish them. <note>original: "favilla stupæ." A reference to Isaiah 1:31, implying that the demon's power is highly flammable and easily destroyed by divine fire.</note>

Hell shall enlarge its appetite, and open its mouth in a moment, and you strong ones shall descend into it, along with your followers, and the lofty and the glorious ones. <note>original: "Dilatabit infernus animam suam." Based on Isaiah 5:14, describing the abyss expanding to swallow the prideful spirits.</note>

Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen.

The Lord watches over you, the Lord is your protection: upon your right hand.

SECOND PART OF THE EXORCISTIC ART, 899
<note>Latin: *Exorcisticæ Pars Secunda*. This section of the manual focuses on the secondary rituals of spiritual cleansing, specifically for domestic environments.</note>
Lll 2
<meta>Catchword: Wife (original: *Uxor*)</meta>

### Psalm 125.
<note>In modern English Bibles, this is Psalm 126. It is a song of thanksgiving for the return of captives to Zion and a prayer for continued restoration.</note>

When the Lord turned back the captivity of Zion, we became like those who are comforted.
Then our mouth was filled with joy, and our tongue with exultation.
Then they shall say among the nations: The Lord has done great things for them.
The Lord has done great things for us: we have become joyful.
Turn again our captivity, O Lord, like a stream in the South.
Those who sow in tears shall reap in exultation.
Going out, they went and wept, carrying their seeds.
But coming, they shall come with exultation, carrying their sheaves.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.
As it was in the beginning, and now, and always, and unto the ages of ages.
Amen.

### Psalm 126.
<note>In modern Bibles, this is Psalm 127. It emphasizes that human effort is futile without God's blessing, particularly regarding the building of a house and the raising of a family.</note>

Unless the Lord builds the house, those who build it have labored in vain.
Unless the Lord guards the city, he who guards it watches in vain.
It is vain for you to rise before the light: rise after you have sat down, you who eat the bread of sorrow.
Since he has given sleep to his beloved ones: behold, the inheritance of the Lord is children, a reward, the fruit of the womb.
Like arrows in the hand of the powerful, so are the children of the shaken. <note>original: *excussorum*. This refers to the "children of one's youth," metaphorically described as being sent out like arrows.</note>
Blessed is the man who has fulfilled his desire from them: he shall not be put to shame when he speaks to his enemies at the gate.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.
As it was in the beginning, and now, and always, and unto the ages of ages.
Amen.

Blessed are all who fear the Lord: who walk in his ways.
Because you shall eat the labors of your hands: you are blessed, and it shall be well with you.

### Psalm 128.
<note>In modern English Bibles, this is Psalm 129. It is a prayer for deliverance from enemies and a plea for the failure of those who oppose the faithful.</note>

Often they have fought against me from my youth, let Israel now say.
Often they have fought against me from my youth, yet they could not prevail against me.
Sinners have built upon my back: they have prolonged their iniquity.
The Lord is just and will cut the necks of sinners: let all who hate Zion be confounded and turned backward.
Let them be as grass upon the rooftops: which withers before it is plucked up.
Wherewith the mower has not filled his hand: nor he who gathers sheaves his bosom.

The blessing of the Lord be upon you: we bless you in the name of the Lord.

Amen.

Aid us, O Christ, Son of God,

### Psalm 130 <note>In modern Bibles, this is typically Psalm 131. It is a "Song of Ascents" attributed to David, focusing on childlike humility before God.</note>

O Lord, my heart is not lifted up, nor are my eyes lofty.
Neither have I walked in great matters, nor in wonderful things that are above me.
If I was not humble in my thoughts, but lifted up my soul:
As a child that is weaned from his mother, so is the recompense <note>original: *rebutio*, a likely typo for *retributio*. The verse compares the soul's contentment to a child resting with its mother.</note> in my soul.
Let Israel hope in the Lord, from this time forth and forevermore.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen.

### Psalm 131 <note>In modern Bibles, this is Psalm 132. It commemorates David’s desire to build a permanent temple (tabernacle) for God.</note>

O Lord, remember David, and all his meekness.
How he swore to the Lord, and vowed a vow to the God of Jacob.
If I shall enter into the tabernacle <term>Tabernaculum: literally a tent or dwelling place; here referring to David's own home</term> of my house: if I shall go up into the bed where I lie.
If I shall give sleep to my eyes, and slumber to my eyelids,
And rest to my temples: until I find a place for the Lord, a Tabernacle for the God of Jacob.
Behold, we heard of it in Ephrata <note>original: *Ephrata*. An ancient name for Bethlehem or the surrounding region where the Ark of the Covenant was once sought.</note>: we found it in the fields of the woods.
We will go into His tabernacle: we will adore in the place where His feet stood.
Arise, O Lord, into Your resting place: You, and the ark of Your sanctification <note>original: *arca sanctificationis tuæ*. This refers to the Ark of the Covenant, the most sacred object of the Israelites, representing God's presence.</note>.

# SECOND PART OF THE EXORCISMAL RITES

Let Your priests be clothed with justice: and let Your Saints rejoice.
For the sake of David Your servant: turn not away the face of Your Anointed <note>original: *Christi tui*. While "Christ" is a proper name, in this Old Testament context, it refers to the "Anointed One," the King of Israel who represents God's authority on earth.</note>.
The Lord has sworn the truth to David, and He will not disappoint him: "Of the fruit of your body I will set upon your throne."
If your children will keep My covenant: and these My testimonies which I shall teach them.
Then their children also shall sit upon your throne forevermore.
For the Lord has chosen Zion <note>Syon: The hill in Jerusalem that served as the spiritual and royal heart of Israel.</note>: He has chosen it for His dwelling.
"This is My rest forever and ever: here will I dwell, for I have chosen it."
Blessing, I will bless her widow <note>The Latin *viduam* (widow) is used in the Vulgate, though other translations suggest "provisions." In a spiritual sense, it implies God's care for the most vulnerable in the household.</note>: her poor I will satisfy with bread.
I will clothe her priests with salvation: and her Saints shall rejoice with exceeding joy.
There I will bring forth the horn <term>Horn: a common biblical symbol for strength and power</term> of David: I have prepared a lamp for My Anointed.
His enemies I will clothe with confusion: but upon him shall my sanctification flourish.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen.

->Psalm 132<- <note>In modern Bibles, this is Psalm 133, a brief "Song of Ascents" celebrating unity.</note>

Behold how good and how pleasant it is: for brothers to dwell together in unity.
It is like the ointment upon the head: that ran down upon the beard, the beard of Aaron <note>Aaron was the first High Priest; the oil signifies a holy consecration that flows from the head of the community down to its members.</note>.
Which ran down to the hem of his garment.
Like the dew of Hermon <note>Mount Hermon is known for its heavy dew, which brought life to the surrounding vegetation; here it symbolizes God's refreshing grace.</note>: which descends upon Mount Zion.
For there the Lord has commanded blessing: and life forevermore.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen.

> Bless † O Lord, we beseech You, this cross, through which You snatched the world from the power of Demons, and overcame the instigator of sin <note>original: *suggestorem peccati*</note>, who rejoiced in the transgression of the first man through the presumption of the forbidden tree. Sanctify † O Lord, this seal of Your passion, that it may be an obstacle to Your enemies and a perpetual banner for those who believe in You. May it be that in whatever place it is situated, there shall be no dwelling of Demons, but rather let them flee in confusion and be driven far from that place and those dwelling in it, as far as the Heaven is removed from the earth. Instead, let the holy warrior angels, most brave, inhabit the place, protecting both the location and its inhabitants. You who live and reign forever and ever. Amen.

# THE BEGINNING OF THE HOLY GOSPEL,
## According to John.

->Glory to You, O Lord.<-

In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God. He was in the beginning with God. All things were made through Him, and without Him nothing was made. What was made: in Him was life, and the life was the light of men, and the light shines in the darkness, and the darkness did not comprehend it. There was a man sent from God, whose name was John. He came for a testimony, to bear witness to the light, so that all might believe through him. He was not the light, but was to bear witness to the light. That was the true light, which enlightens every man coming into this world. He was in the world, and the world was made through Him, and the world did not know Him. He came unto His own, and His own did not receive Him. But as many as did receive Him, to them He gave the power to become children of God, to those who believe in His name, who were born not of blood, nor of the will of the flesh, nor of the will of man, but of God. And the Word was made flesh, and dwelt among us, and we saw His glory, glory as of the only-begotten of the Father, full of grace and truth.

->Thanks be to God.<-

# THE CONTINUATION OF THE HOLY GOSPEL
## According to Matthew.
->Glory to You, O Lord.<-

When Jesus therefore was born in Bethlehem of Judea, in the days of King Herod, behold, Magi <note>The "Wise Men" from the East</note> came from the East to Jerusalem, saying: where is he who is born King of the Jews? For we have seen his Star <note>original: *Stellam*</note> in the East, and have come to adore him. But King Herod, hearing this, was troubled, and all Jerusalem with him: and assembling all the Chief Priests and the Scribes of the people, he inquired of them where the Christ was to be born. And they said to him, in Bethlehem of Judea: for thus it is written by the Prophet <note>referring to Micah 5:2</note>. And you, Bethlehem, land of Judah, are by no means the least among the princes of Judah: for out of you shall come forth a Leader who shall rule my people Israel. Then Herod, having privately called the Magi, diligently learned from them the time of the Star which appeared to them, and sending them to Bethlehem, said: Go, and inquire diligently about the child, and when you have found him, report back to me, so that I also, coming, may adore him. When they had heard the King, they went away; and behold, the Star, which they had seen in the East, went before them, until it came and stood over where the child was. And seeing the Star, they rejoiced with very great joy. And entering the house, they found the child with Mary his mother, and falling down, they adored him <note>The Latin ends here; the ritual likely continues on the next page with the Magi offering their gifts of gold, frankincense, and myrrh</note>.

## THE CONTINUATION OF THE HOLY GOSPEL,
### According to Luke.

->*Glory to You, O Lord.*<-

The Angel Gabriel was sent by God to a city of Galilee, named Nazareth, to a Virgin betrothed to a man whose name was Joseph, of the house of David, and the virgin’s name was Mary. And the Angel, having entered to her, said: "Hail, full of grace, the Lord is with you, blessed are you among women." When she had heard this, she was troubled by his word, and pondered what kind of greeting this might be. And the Angel said to her: "Do not fear, Mary, for you have found grace with God; behold, you shall conceive in your womb and bear a son, and you shall call his name Jesus. He shall be great, and shall be called the son of the Most High <term>Altissimus: a title for God emphasizing his supreme power</term>; and the Lord shall give to him the throne of his father David, and he shall reign in the house of Jacob forever, and of his Kingdom there shall be no end." But Mary said to the Angel: "How shall this be, since I do not know a man?" <note>A formal way of saying she was a virgin</note> And the Angel, answering, said to her: "The Holy Spirit shall come upon you, and the power of the Most High shall overshadow you. And therefore also that Holy One which shall be born of you shall be called the Son of God."

->*Thanks be to God.*<-

<header>THE SECOND PART OF THE EXORCISTIC ART</header>

# IF OTHER PLACES REMAIN,
*let him resume the first Gospel and follow the manner and form as described above, until he has completed the circuit.*

*When all these things are finished, let him bless all the inhabitants of the house, as they kneel, in this manner:*

Dearest Brothers. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; Thanks be to God.

Remain at peace in your house: and let your heart be joyful. May the Lord grant us rest, and peace, and consolation on every side from all your enemies. May the Lord open His finest treasury, Heaven, to grant rain to the land in its proper season: and may the earth itself be blessed to yield its fruits, and for an abundance of all things which you use. May He make you abound in all good things, just as He made the people of Israel <note>A reference to the biblical promise of prosperity for the Israelites if they followed divine law, specifically echoing Deuteronomy 28.</note> abound while they persevered in His commandments. May God bless† you from His Throne, whether standing, walking, eating, or sleeping, so that you may be among the number of the predestined and the chosen; may your house flourish even unto the third and fourth generation, and be preserved in happiness forever. And may He strengthen every intention of your heart by His power, so that holy Jerusalem may glory in you, and your names may be in the number of the Saints and the righteous, and your house remain in a state of sanctification: and finally, may the blessing† of God descend upon you, and remain with you always. Amen.

->Let Holy Water be sprinkled.<-

# B L E S S I N G
## for whatever thing you wish.

Bless†, O Lord, this creature <note>original: *creaturam*. In liturgical Latin, this refers to any "created thing"—such as water, salt, or a physical object—being set aside for sacred use.</note> [Name of object], that it may be a saving remedy for Your creation, which You redeemed with Your precious blood; and grant through the invocation of Your holy name that whoever uses it may receive blessing and health, and that it may be a protection against all diseases and all the snares of the enemy. Through Christ our Lord, Amen.

->Let Holy Water be sprinkled.<-

# COMPLEMENT OF THE EXORCISTIC ART,
## PART THREE.
### WHICH IS CALLED THE EXORCISMAL PART.

<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut decorative initial 'E' featuring scrolling foliage and a small mask or face at the bottom.</image-desc>The Exorcist should approach this Divine work with firm faith and perfect charity, and with all humility, not trusting in his own strength—for that would be empty and the labor would be in vain—since the driving out of demons from possessed bodies belongs to God alone. Rather, he should approach as a simple and unworthy Minister, dressed in a <term>Surplice</term><note>Latin: *Cotta*; a knee-length white liturgical vestment with wide sleeves, worn over a cassock.</note> and <term>Stole</term><note>Latin: *Stola*; a long, narrow strip of fabric worn around the neck, signifying the authority of the priesthood.</note> in the Church before the Altar for the sake of greater devotion—and even more so if the Most Holy Body of Christ <note>The consecrated Host or Eucharist, believed to be the literal presence of Christ.</note> is reserved there. He should sprinkle the place in a circle with holy water and say:

> You shall sprinkle me, O Lord, with hyssop <note>original: "Asperges me Domine hyssopo"; Hyssop is a plant used in biblical times for ritual cleansing.</note>, and I shall be cleansed: you shall wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow. Have mercy on me, O God, according to your great mercy. Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen.

->THEN LET HIM SPRINKLE THE POSSESSED<-
->creature, himself, and those standing by,<-
->saying:<-

Through this holy water, may every unclean spirit and every assault of Satan be separated from you, and flee from us: In the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy Spirit. Amen.

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the Third Part of the manual, focusing on the specific ritual for exorcising a possessed person. It emphasizes the priest's humility and reliance on God, detailing the vestments to be worn and the initial purification of the space and participants with holy water.</summary>
<keywords>exorcism ritual, surplice, stole, holy water, Asperges me, purification, spiritual humility, possession</keywords>

Therefore I beseech blessed Mary ever Virgin, blessed Michael the Archangel, blessed John the Baptist, the holy Apostles Peter and Paul, and blessed N., all the Saints, and you brothers, to pray for me to the Lord our God. ## PREPARATION OF THE EXORCIST to be spoken with great constancy, with eyes raised to the Crucifix.

I see, good Jesus, these dogs and ravenous wolves surrounding me and prepared against me; let your power go before me: so that I may complete my course and the ministry which I have received from the Holy Church. I say I do not fear the multitude of demons coming against me: for I am ready not only to be afflicted, but to die for your love and your Holy law. Grant therefore help from Heaven, sweetest Jesus: for these trust in their pride and likewise in their malice; but I trust in you, O Lord, who are powerful, and hope for victory to destroy them with a single nod. <note>original: "uno nutu"; implying God's will is so powerful that a mere gesture or thought can defeat the enemy.</note>

Root me † entirely in charity, and dwell in me through faith, so that in this way, and together with the intercession of all the Saints, I may be able to tame the ferocity and pride of this Beast.

->Let us pray.<-

> Almighty Creator, I have put on the breastplate <note>original: "lorica"; continuing the metaphor of Roman body armor for spiritual defense</note>, and am girded with your weapons of war to fight against these rebels of yours. But how will I be able to do so, Lord, unless you help me? Indeed, I undertake a harsh war, and the adversaries are many, who trust in their own pride, ferocity, and malice: Help me therefore, Lord, because the victory of war lies not in a multitude of iniquity or an army, but strength comes from Heaven. And do not, I beseech you, allow these beings to despise your dignity, nor your unworthy servant crying out in prayer: But let my cry ascend like a trumpet to your Throne: and so, through the intercession of all the Saints, may my prayer be heard to the confusion of those who seek our souls. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

## PREPARATION FOR ARMING
->The possessed person: to be said with piety and the sorrow of tears <note>original: "lachrymarum compunctione"; 'compunction' here refers to a deep, transformative sense of remorse or spiritual grief</note>.<-

For your sadness, by God’s mercy, shall be turned into joy, and you shall obtain the Kingdom of Heaven.

May Jesus † Christ deliver you from this Dragon, He who by His own power rose from the dead for you. And I strengthen your heart † in the arm of the Holy Spirit, lest the tyrants circumvent you. May Jesus Christ, who sitting at the right hand of the Father has been made an advocate for you, deliver you † from the works of Satan and his ministers; and I seal your innermost parts <note>original: "viscera"; literally 'internal organs,' here referring to the core of the person's being</note> † in the Throne of the Most Holy Trinity, lest you be tortured by these Cursed ones.

# EXORCISM, PART THREE.

The Choir <note>Refers to the Heavenly Host or the "Choir of Saints"</note>, so that it may destroy you, most proud ones, holds double-edged and sharpened swords already drawn; and the Judge, with the council of the Apostles, already sits prepared to condemn your lips and your lying operations. Rise, therefore, to the tongue, so that you may hear the precious words of the Lord, and answer according to the truth to those things which are asked of you.

->WHEN THE DEMON HAS ARRIVED AT THE TONGUE<-
->let the exorcist place the Stole <note>The stole is a long silk band worn by priests, representing his spiritual authority</note> around the neck of the<-
->possessed person, and making three<-
->knots, let him say:<-

> Just as our Lord Jesus Christ, descending from Heaven, seized the Dragon, the ancient Serpent, and bound him with a great chain, and casting him into the Abyss, closed it so that he might not deceive the nations; and after death descending to the lower regions, He shattered the gates of brass and destroyed the most proud Lucifer and his followers; so do I bind you with this sacred Stole, which, like a ring in your nostrils and a rope upon your tongue, is wound around by my hands, so that by the power of the Passion and the Cross of the Lord, you may obey all my commands. ### COMMAND TO THE DEMONS
->who are causing torment.<-

To you demons, who with rash daring torture this creature of God <note>The Latin 'creatura' is grammatically feminine; the ritual refers to the person as a 'creature of God' regardless of their gender</note>, I command you under these same penalties that you say nothing at all, nor do anything, except as much as shall be specifically granted by God and by me; and departing from this person, give the sign imposed on you by the Exorcist, and leave this person and every creature unharmed, so that they may give praise to God their Redeemer. To whom be praise, honor, and glory for ever and ever.

### COMMAND TO LUCIFER,
->and the other Princes.<-

I also command you, Lucifer, and all you Princes of the Demons: and especially the enemies of these infernal swine <note>original: "infernalium porcorum" – a common derogatory term for demons in these texts, referencing the biblical story of the Gadarene swine</note>, in the name of the Most Holy Trinity, of the Father † and of the Son † and of the Holy Spirit † under the same penalties to be incurred immediately, that like ministers of Divine justice, you come with all force and wrath in the event of disobedience upon all these spirits, as if they were wood-cutters <note>original: "quasi caedentes ligna" – a metaphorical reference to a violent or crushing labor of destruction</note>, and compel them to the confusion of you all to obey my commands. Otherwise, by Divine power, you shall be tortured immediately in the Abyss <term>Barathrum: a Greek-derived term used in Latin to describe the deepest, most bottomless pit of the underworld</term>. Amen.

->Lord, hear my prayer.<-
->Response: And let my cry come unto thee.<-
->The Lord be with you.<-
->Response: And with your spirit.<-
->Let us pray.<-

Almighty eternal God, who by your word confound the proud and the disobedient alike, and from whom no secret is hidden: We humbly beseech your omnipotence that you deign to approve all things done by me in your name: and as for these rebels all held under these commands, in the case of their disobedience, may you by no means release them: But in the rigor of your Justice, torture them. Through Jesus Christ your son, our Lord. Amen.

<header>EXORCISM PART THREE.</header>

...friendship, through which in mutual love we bear one another’s burdens, so that we may fulfill the law of Christ, who, though he was in the form of God, did not deem it robbery to be equal to God: But he emptied himself, taking the form of a servant, being made in the likeness of men, and found in human appearance, he humbled himself, becoming obedient even unto death, even the death of the Cross <note>This is a direct translation of Philippians 2:6-8, a foundational hymn about Christ's humility</note>: he himself bore our sins, so that he might reconcile us to God the Father in friendship. I adjure† you <note>original: 'Conjuro vos' — the '†' indicates the exorcist should make the sign of the cross</note>, by the holy purity of conscience, which shuts out the worm of conscience <note>A traditional metaphor for the gnawing pain of guilt or remorse</note>, and frees the soul from the prison of pain, and purges the mind of all uncleanness; for this is called the holy mind, a Paradise of delights planted with various branches of honorable works, and decorated with the flowers of virtues, and sweetly watered by Heavenly grace: the Paradise in which is planted the tree of life and of Heavenly wisdom, the banquet of souls: the Bridal Chamber of God: the Palace of Christ: the dwelling place of the Holy Spirit: the Throne of Solomon: the bed of the Heavenly Spouse, in which a good conscience itself is most greatly delighted, and rests with her Spouse. I adjure† you, by holy hospitality, the friend of Angels: the sister of Charity, and the crown of Humility: through which we deserve to receive not only Angels, but even God himself at times <note>A reference to Abraham and Lot, who unknowingly hosted God or his messengers (Genesis 18-19)</note>. I adjure† you, by holy piety, through which, dismissing vices and abandoning all things for the love of Christ, we shall arrive at his perfection through humility, and fasting, alms-giving, and patience, and charity, and through perseverance in good works. I adjure† you, by the holy Apostolic Religion <note>original: 'Religionẽ Apostolicã' — here referring to the collective faith and disciplined life of the Church</note> separated from this world in heart and in work, which strikes the Leaders of Moab, kills the Governors, and the Tyrants of Jerusalem, and wounds the Hypocrites of the Egyptians unto death. I adjure† you, by holy obedience, with which all things are found full of charity, for this is that venerable virtue which is preferred over sacrifice <note>Reference to 1 Samuel 15:22, 'to obey is better than sacrifice'</note>; it is the Ladder of Heaven and the locking of Hell; it preserves harmony among the Angels: it nurtures humility and peace: it proves patience and stirs up gentleness, for the sake of which Families are ruled, and Commonwealths are preserved—

I adjure† you, by Holy contemplation, through which the eternal life is designated as secure and true: Because our happiness is nothing else than to see God, to have God, and to contemplate God. ### THE THIRD PART OF THE EXORCISTIC ART.
<sig>Nnn 4</sig>

...[solitude], <note>Continuing from the previous page: "I adjure you, by the Holy solitude..."</note> always breaking forth into eloquent speech. I adjure† you <note>The '†' symbol indicates the exorcist should make the sign of the cross at this moment</note>, by holy perseverance, by which Martyrs are wreathed with laurel, Virgins are crowned, and Confessors and Priests are exalted: This is that which gives form to merit; it gives color to the purpose of the just; it rewards the one who runs the race, Crowns the one who fights lawfully, and leads to the prize; and it guides all to the harbor. This, I say, is the full-length robe <note>original: 'tunica talaris' — a garment reaching to the ankles</note> of Saint Joseph reaching down to the Earth, the priestly tunic reaching to the feet: The heel of good work, which we must fortify against the bite of the Serpent. 

I adjure† you, by the holy remorse <note>original: 'compunctionem' — a spiritual "pricking" of the heart that leads to tears of repentance</note> of tears, which makes us, who were exiles, Citizens of the Heavenly Fatherland: and having set aside our corruptible clothing, as we enter the Heavenly Jerusalem, it associates us with the Holy Angels: of which the Lord spoke, saying: *Blessed are they who mourn, for they shall be comforted* <note>Matthew 5:4</note>. For in true contrition and humiliation of heart, the hope of pardon is born, the troubled conscience is reconciled, lost grace is repaired, man is made safe from the coming wrath, and God and the repentant Soul meet each other in a Holy kiss. This is the health of Souls, the remission of sins, the spiritual sacrifice through which the scourge of God is changed. This [remorse] fortifies the mind, makes the intention fruitful, waters confession, extinguishes illicit movements, and makes one despise the world in all things. 

This is the vital small Ship <note>original: 'Navicula'</note>, through which one returns from shipwrecks to the harbor of Salvation: the saving water, through which every sin is destroyed: The happy bath of penance, which is as effective for cleansing as often as the human heart needs purification: it is the herb of that Heavenly launderer <note>original: 'fullonis' — a fuller or cloth-cleaner; a reference to Malachi 3:2</note>, with which he cleanses daily the defiled garments of his servants from filth: and the heavenly soda <note>original: 'Nitrum' — an alkaline cleaning agent</note> which, made from the dew of divine grace, wipes away the stains of sins: and finally it is the sweet tear, made a companion to the passion of Jesus Christ. 

I adjure† you, by holy Vigilance, concerning which the Prince of the Apostles <note>Saint Peter</note>, solicitous for our salvation, sweetly warns us, saying: *Watch, brothers, because your Adversary the devil seeks someone to devour* <note>1 Peter 5:8</note>: and through which, idleness having been despised, we are adorned with all good things: and finally we are refreshed in the Heavenly Fatherland. I adjure† you, by Holy Justice, which is not inflamed by cruelty, nor softened by the love of money, nor relaxed by fear; neither softening judgment toward the right hand, nor exasperating punishment toward the left, but in the name of Christ and the certainty of truth, she renders to each what is their own. Through her, chastity reigns, peace and security triumph: dignity flourishes and brings forth fruit in patience; she is at last the medicine for vices and the antidote for sins. 

I adjure† you, by Holy Peace, the guardian of all virtues, which the Angels announced at the birth of Jesus Christ, and He himself asked for while hanging on the Cross, and left as a testament to His Apostles as the highest good. And finally, blessed is he who sheds tears in her. Because she is a holy and immaculate burnt offering <note>original: 'holocaustum'</note> to God, so that they may be made worthy of his grace.

...[Holy] <note>The text begins with the suffix '-ctum', likely completing 'Sanctum' (Holy) from the previous page</note> **Fasting**, which is the necessary satisfaction for all the members of the body: a Heavenly work: the Gate of Paradise, and the pattern of the world to come: through which vices are cast down, virtues are increased, the flesh is humbled, Demons are cast out, and the snares of the devil are overcome: Divine Mysteries are unfolded, memories of the past are made, present things are set in order, and future things are foreseen. 

I adjure† <note>The ‘†’ symbol indicates the exorcist should make the sign of the cross to empower the command</note> you, by **Holy Almsgiving**, which cleanses sins, treasures up a good foundation, and intercedes with the Lord for us: and that which is given to the poor is preserved in its entirety: Indeed, it is possessed a hundredfold in the true and eternal life. 

I adjure† you, by the **Holy Faith** of Christians, through which, renouncing the devil and his displays <note>original: 'pompis' — referring to the "pomps" or empty allurements of the devil rejected at baptism</note>, we are made participants in the Passion, believing in God as Three and One, who is not diminished in the individuals nor increased in the three, from whom all things, through whom all things, and in whom all things are: and dying in the bosom of our Holy Mother Church, we are saved, while you are confounded. 

I adjure† you, by **Holy Hope**, which pours grace into those who wait, and both illuminates and refreshes them: in charity, it extends its breadth even to the enemy: and it sets a height so that we may persevere in it until death: through this, the wailing wards and orphans hope to laugh, the poor trust they will rejoice, and pilgrims hope to reach the destination and the end of their labor. 

I adjure† you, by **Holy Charity**, the bond of perfection, the end of the commandments, and the sweetness of all good things: by which we love God above all things, and our neighbor as ourselves: and because of which, from many souls one soul is made, and one heart in Christ Jesus our Lord, fashioned into a seamless garment <note>A reference to the 'tunica inconsutilis' (John 19:23), symbolizing the indivisible unity of the Church</note>, in which, finally being rooted, we comprehend with all the saints what is the breadth, length, and depth. 

I adjure† you, by **Holy Truth**, the shining light, which promises us very great rewards and promises, because she makes us disciples of the Lord and free from the errors of Demons. To the confusion of all of you and to your punishment, O wicked and lying Demons, nursing-children of lies: Indeed, the light of the Lord’s face is sealed upon us, to contemplate Truth itself...

<summary>This page lists a series of adjurations that use Christian virtues—Fasting, Almsgiving, Faith, Hope, Charity, and Truth—as weapons to torment and expel demons during an exorcism. Each virtue is described by its spiritual benefits to the human soul and its destructive effect on the power of the devil.</summary>

> Therefore, accursed Satan, adjured by the name of the eternal God, and our Savior Jesus Christ His son, depart—trembling and groaning—vanquished by your own envy; let there be nothing in common between you and this creature of God <note>original: 'creatura Dei' — in this context, the person undergoing the exorcism</note>, who now contemplates Heavenly things: Give honor, therefore, to the approaching Holy Spirit, who, descending from the highest citadel of Heaven, having disrupted your deceits, has perfected this His Temple and dwelling place, sanctified by the divine fountain <note>A reference to the waters of Baptism</note>, so that this faithful creature, liberated from all harmful things within, may return thanks to the everlasting God and always bless His Holy name forever and ever, Amen.

## EXORCISM II.
### Of the Holy Fathers of the Old Testament.

God, † come to my assistance.
O Lord, † make haste to help me.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:
As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen. Alleluia.
O Lord, hear my prayer,
And let my cry come unto Thee.
The Lord be with you.
And with your spirit.

## HERE, ABOVE THE HEAD OF THE SICK PERSON
->let him lay his hands, and say:<-
### EXORCISM.

I exorcise you, [Name] <note>The letter 'N.' stands for *Nomen* (Name), where the exorcist inserts the name of the afflicted person</note>, a sick creature of God, by God † <note>The ‘†’ symbol indicates the exorcist should make the sign of the cross over the person</note> the creator of all goodness, by the charity † of our Lord Jesus Christ, and by the mercy † of the Holy Spirit: so that you may be made a Temple of the living God, and a dwelling place of the Holy Spirit, and a perfect medicine † sanctified † and blessed † remaining in your inner being, to root out every power of the Adversary, to put to flight every incursion of Satan, and to drive out every adjured phantom, through Him who with the Father, † and the Holy Spirit † lives and reigns forever and ever. Amen.

->Let us pray.<-

Holy Lord, Father Almighty, Eternal God, who by pouring out the grace of Your blessing onto sick bodies, guards Your creation with manifold piety: graciously assist us at the invocation of Your Most Holy Name, and raise up [Name] Your handmaid <note>The original uses 'famulam' (female servant/handmaid), suggesting this specific prayer was written with a female subject in mind</note> who has been granted health by Your right hand, confirm her with strength, protect her with power, and restore her to Your Holy Church with all desired prosperity. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

## HERE, HAVING SPRINKLED HOLY WATER,
->with eyes raised and having grasped the hair<-
->of the possessed person’s head, let him say:<-

Strengthen me, God of Israel, and look upon the works of my hands, this dragon <note>original: 'draco' — a common metaphorical term for the devil or a demon in late medieval and early modern exorcism manuals</note>: so that, confounded by Your word, he may withdraw.

### CONJURATION.

Flee, most foul Satan, in the name of God the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit: For I conjure you, † on account of your cursed homicide <note>A reference to the devil being a "murderer from the beginning" (John 8:44)</note>, so that without injury to this Creature, and to all the faithful, you may flee headlong in flight together with all your followers, and those who favor and assist you, through Saint Abel, who was innocent in life, greatest in obedience, pleasing to God in his sacrifices, and patient in death:

And finally, wise in interpretation, he was released with great honor; prudent in his counsel regarding future things, and returning the grace of good retribution for evil, he magnified the people of God beyond measure.

I confound † you, cast down from the grace of God, by Moses, who was the Leader and Prophet in Israel. For the safety of the people, he feared neither threats nor weapons, but raised his rod against the Dragons <note>original: *Dracones*; referring to the serpents of Pharaoh’s magicians</note>, and struck the King’s Empire with the greatest plagues. And finally, ascending the peak of Mount Sinai, he received the Holy Law and spoke to the Lord face to face. The Lord magnified him in the fear of his enemies, and by his words he appeased monsters, and showed him His glory.

I scourge † you, Scorn of the entire Angelic Nature, by Aaron the high Priest, the first in the law, who was like unto God. The Lord blessed him in glory and girded him with the Belt of Justice; He clothed him in the robe of glory and crowned him in a vessel of virtue. He also expressed the future Passion of Christ through the sacrifice and the blood of victims.

I suppress † you, ejected from the company of the Angels of God, by Phineas, the son of Eleazar, the third in glory. In the goodness and alacrity of his soul, he pleased the God of Israel, and the Lord gave him the peace of His covenant, because he was zealous for his God <note>Phineas is celebrated in the Bible for his decisive action to stop an apostasy, for which he was granted a "covenant of peace"</note>.

I prostrate † you, head and origin of all rebellions, by Joshua, the successor of Moses—great according to his name <note>The name Joshua/Yeshua means "The Lord is Salvation"</note> and greatest in the salvation of the elect of God. He invoked the Most High, and the Sun stood still, so that one day became as if two, destroying and dissipating your members. He made the course of the Jordan stand still so that he might introduce the inheritance of Israel into the land of promise, flowing with milk and honey. He distributed that land by lot, struck the enemies, overthrew the kingdoms of the Canaanites, and extinguished kings, so that you may know, wicked one...

[...spirit] you lead back: and there is none who may escape your hand: For you yourself chastise us for our iniquities, and you will save us for your mercy's sake. <note>This opening line completes a prayer acknowledging that while God allows suffering or "chastisement," He alone has the power to deliver the soul.</note>

I torture † you, **opponent of holy patience**, by the most patient **Job**, a man just, upright, and God-fearing. Though by God’s permission he was robbed of his wealth and children by Satan, and struck in his own person, he did not sin with his lips. Indeed, he was glorious in the struggle, calm in his grief, and patiently endured the wounds of his body, always glorifying God amidst his punishments. Falling to the ground, he worshipped, saying: *“Naked I came from my mother’s womb, and naked I shall return there; the Lord gave, the Lord has taken away; as it pleased the Lord, so it has been done. Blessed be the name of the Lord.”* <note>original: *Nudus egressus sum de utero Matris meæ... Sit nomen Domini benedictum.*</note>

I destroy † you, **persecutor of the righteous and the innocent**, by the **three boys, Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego**, of noble royal stock. Mindful of their fathers’ laws and sustaining their lives on legumes and vegetables, they refused to worship the Idol and were cast into the horrific flames of the furnace; yet, with open voices, blessing the Lord in a hymn, they emerged unharmed. 

I annihilate your pride, **Beast most beastly of all beasts**, by the **Prophet Isaiah**, saying: *“Behold, a virgin shall conceive, and bear a son, and his name shall be called Emmanuel; he shall eat butter and honey, that he may know how to refuse evil and choose good.”* <note>original: *Ecce virgo cōcipiet, & pariet filium...*</note> And elsewhere: *“The Ox knows his Owner, and the Ass his Lord's manger.”* <note>original: *Cogovit Bos Possessorē suum...*</note> But you, ungrateful one, did not recognize your Creator. 

I consume † you, **nocturnal Phantom**, by the vision of that same **Isaiah**; for he saw the Lord sitting upon a high and elevated throne, and the things that were beneath Him filled the Temple. **Seraphim** stood above it, one having six wings and the other six wings: with two they veiled his face, and with two they veiled... <note>The text describes the terrifying majesty of the angelic hierarchy to demonstrate the demon's insignificance in the face of the celestial court.</note>

...filth, because he acted proudly. I submerge † <note>The ‘†’ symbol indicates where the exorcist makes the Sign of the Cross over the possessed person to signify a spiritual blow against the entity.</note> thee in the deep, **luxurious Dragon**, through the Prophet **Amos**, saying: *The Lord shall roar from Zion, and utter his voice from Jerusalem:* and elsewhere: *I raised up Nazarenes from your young men.* <note>original: *Dominus de Syon rugiet...* — Amos 1:2 and 2:11. The "chariots" and "lions" represent the demon's power and pride being dismantled.</note> I destroy † thee, **dung-stained sow**, through **Habakkuk**, saying in his prayer: *God will come from the South, and the Holy One from Mount Paran: his glory covered the heavens, and the earth is full of his praise: his brightness shall be as the light, horns are in his hands: there is his strength hid, and death shall go before his face.* <note>Habakkuk 3:3–5</note> I destroy † thee, **stable-boy**, <note>term: *Agaso* — Literally a groom or mule-driver; used here as a humiliating title to lower the demon's status.</note> through **Zephaniah**, saying: *Praise, O daughter of Zion, shout, O Israel, be glad and rejoice with all thy heart, O daughter of Jerusalem: The Lord hath taken away thy judgment: the King of Israel, the Lord, hath turned away thy enemies in the midst of thee, thou shalt fear evil no more.* <note>Zephaniah 3:14–15</note> I attack † thee, **senseless one**, through **Haggai**, crying out: *Yet one little while: I will move the Heaven, and the Earth, and the Sea, and the Dry land, and I will move all nations, and the Desired of all nations shall come.* <note>Haggai 2:7–8. "The Desired of all nations" is a traditional Christological title.</note> I hang † thee on the infernal gallows, **robber**, through **Zechariah**, prophesying: *Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion: shout for joy, O daughter of Jerusalem: behold thy King will come to thee, the Just and Savior, he is poor, and riding upon an ass, and upon a colt the foal of an ass:* and elsewhere: *and they weighed my price—*

...my price: thirty pieces of silver. I will destroy the names of the idols from the earth, and they shall be remembered no more. <note>original: *triginta Argenteos... Disperdam nomina Idolorum...* — References to Zechariah 11:12 and 13:2.</note> I **strangle** † <note>The ‘†’ symbol marks where the exorcist makes the Sign of the Cross over the afflicted person.</note> thee, accursed one, through the proclamation of **Malachi**: *I will come to you in judgment, and I will be a swift witness against sorcerers, and adulterers, and perjurers, and those who cheat the laborer of his wages, and humble the widows and orphans, and oppress the stranger.* <note>Malachi 3:5</note> I **weaken** † thee, **mad dog**, through the seven **Maccabean brothers**, firm in faith and strong in hope, who stood as the most constant observers of the divine law; for which law they were cruelly put to death by the impious King Antiochus amidst the most bitter torments, enduring it with their Mother. In her womanly body, a manly spirit was shown, along with an admirable growth of a great soul. For her own maternal heart was torn beyond the custom of nature; for she did not merely pity her sons who were in torments, but was choked with fear lest they should fail in them; wherefore she anxiously exhorted them to endure the same most constantly. <note>Referencing 2 Maccabees 7, which tells the story of Jewish martyrs who died rather than break religious law.</note> I **vanquish** † thee at last, **drunkard**, through all the righteous and holy ones of the **Old Testament**, who, fearing the Lord, walked in His ways, and despising you iniquitous ones, entered the gates of Paradise through many tribulations and hardships while serving the law of the eternal God, and now reign with Christ forever.

Therefore, accursed Devil, recognize thy sentence, and give honor to the **living** † and true God: give honor to **Jesus** † **Christ** His Son, and to the **Holy** † **Spirit**: recede from this creature of God, because God and our Lord Jesus Christ have deigned to call this person to His holy grace and bless † ing, and to the font of baptism by the gift of His grace. This sign of the **Holy** † **Cross**, which we place upon his forehead, mayest thou never dare to violate, thou accursed Devil, but remain forever damned in hell, driven away from this person. Flee, with all those who favor thee: In the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit: To whom be praise, honor, and glory, forever and ever. Amen.

### EXORCISM.

I exorcise thee, afflicted Creature of God, by the power
of God the † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the priest makes the Sign of the Cross over the person.</note> Father, by the wisdom of God the † Son, and by the
mercy of God the † Holy Spirit: by God, Three
in Persons and One in Essence; the living and true God,
so that thou mayest be healthy and free from all the filth of Demons,
through Him in whose name thou wast baptized. Receive the grace
of the Holy Spirit through the King of Kings and Lord of
Lords, to whom belong honor and power forever and 
ever. Amen.

->Let us pray.<-

Graciously hear the supplications of all Thy Saints,
we beseech Thee, O Lord, so that we, who are
rightly afflicted by our own actions, may find relief by the
consolation of Thy grace. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

At the invocation of Thy † name, O Lord, strengthen
me, so that these Accursed ones may go headlong into flight,
just as the army of Holofernes did when the Hebrews pursued them. <note>original: *exercitus Holophernis* — Referring to the Book of Judith, where the general Holofernes' army fled in terror after his decapitation.</note>

Flee, hunter and devourer of souls, by the Holy
Trinity: the Father, † and the Son, † and the Holy † Spirit.

# THE COMPLETION OF THE ART
<meta>catchword: do ce- (likely referring to the start of "docuit" or "doceat" on the next page)</meta>

And I cast thee down † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the priest makes the Sign of the Cross to physically repel the spirit.</note>, O mist in the darkness, through Saint John the Baptist, the beloved precursor and the burning lamp before the Lord, who prepared the way for the Lord in the Wilderness: and pointed out the Lamb of God: and who alone among the Prophets earned the right to point out Jesus Christ with his finger, to see Him with his own eyes, and to baptize Him. This is the Herald and the Witness of the Heavenly Word: This is he whose birth, name, and merits were foretold through the Archangel Gabriel: This is he who is preferred above all mortals, as the Lord said: "Among those born of women, there has not risen one greater than John the Baptist": And finally, after a miraculous, holy, and perfect life, he was consecrated by the eternal glory of a Holy death.

I drive thee † into the Abyss <term>Barathrum (a deep pit or underworld)</term>, O most ravenous Wolf <note>The demon is frequently addressed as a "wolf" in these rites to characterize its predatory nature toward the "flock" of the faithful.</note>, through Saint Peter, Prince of the Apostles, to whom the Lord entrusted His sheep and delivered the <term>Keys of the Kingdom of Heaven</term>, saying: "Whatever thou shalt bind on earth, shall be bound also in Heaven: and whatever thou shalt loose on earth, shall be loosed also in Heaven": who founded the Church of Antioch and the Church of Rome; who filled Pontus, Galatia, Cappadocia, Asia, and Bithynia with the laws of Evangelical preaching: and who, with Paul as his companion, did not fear to approach Rome—which was then a forest of roaring beasts and an ocean of the most turbulent depth. Indeed, there he trampled upon the opinions of Philosophy, dissolved the vanities of earthly wisdom, refuted the worship of Demons, destroyed the impieties of all Sacrileges, cast down the filth of the Magic arts along with the Mages themselves, and revealed the light of truth to all. This is Peter, upon which rock the Lord built His holy Church: This Keeper of the Keys, at last, after infinite miracles and divine works—fearing nothing of this earth—ascended the frame of the Cross, and rejoicing, passed on to Christ his Master.

I muzzle thee †, O Raven of ill-tidings, through Saint Paul, who from a persecutor of Christians was made a herald of Christ: and after many evils, obtained grace and earned the right to possess the twelfth Throne: and who earned the right to be called by the Lord a <term>Vessel of Election</term> <note>original: "vas electionis" — a chosen instrument</note> and a preacher of truth, [carrying] the name of Jesus Christ throughout the entire world...

<summary>This page of the exorcism ritual invokes the spiritual authority of John the Baptist, St. Peter, and St. Paul to bind the demon, using metaphors of a wolf and a raven to describe the evil spirit. It recounts the hagiographic triumphs of the Apostles over paganism and magic in Rome as a precedent for the current casting out of the demon.</summary>

### THE THIRD PART OF THE EXORCISM
<sig>Ooo 4</sig>
