[the name of the Lord] before the gentiles, and Kings, and the children of Israel he carried, and intrepidly preached: He saw the Heavens opened, and the secrets of God, which it is not lawful for man to speak: Thrice beaten with rods: once stoned: Thrice he suffered shipwreck for Christ’s name: He wrote many Epistles to diverse peoples, and converted them to the faith: and finally, after very great wonders, here the light of the World, with his head cut off for Christ’s name, was crowned with martyrdom.

I bind thee with a holy song † <note>original: "Carmino" — derived from 'carmen' (song/incantation), here used as a ritual binding through sacred speech</note>, O Subverter of the Gospel, and Master of error, through both the aforementioned glorious Apostles, the Princes of the Earth, through whom the Gospel of Christ shone forth at Rome, which, while it was once the Mistress of error, was made the Disciple of truth: and by as much as it was more tenaciously bound by the Devil, by so much more through them in the name of Jesus was it wonderfully set free. These are they who, from the honor of many gems joined into one Diadem, crowned the whole world: who are like the light of the eyes in the head of Christ: What prisons did they sanctify? how many chains did they adorn? How many torments did they sustain? how many curses did they endure? their tongues are blessed instruments: their members were sprinkled with blood for the sake of the church: their sound has gone out into all the Earth, and their words to the ends of the World: Peter, the most faithful of all the Apostles, loved the Lord with a fervent spirit, and Paul’s virtues can be explained by no one in words: and as they loved each other in life, so also in death they were not separated. I rend thee † <note>original: "Dilanio" — to tear apart or scatter</note>, O charlatan <term>circulator (literally a wandering performer or peddler; here used to mock the demon as a deceptive vagabond)</term>, through Saint Andrew, the worthy Apostle of Christ, who as soon as he heard the voice of the Lord preaching, left his nets and followed the Giver of the rewards of eternal life: And who, when brought to the place of martyrdom and having seen the Cross from afar, began to preach and to cry out:

> O admirable Cross: O desirable Cross: O good Cross, which received beauty from the members of the Lord, long desired, earnestly loved, sought without ceasing, and at last prepared for a soul that longs for it, secure and rejoicing I come to you. Receive me from men and restore me to my Master, that through you He may receive me, who through you redeemed me:

and finally hanging for two days on the cross alive, and without ceasing preaching the faith, and many [he converted]...

...converting [them] to the faith, he passed to Him whose death he had desired and undergone. I gnaw at you † <note>original: "Corrodo" — literally to gnaw or wear away; used here as a ritual action of spiritual erosion against the demon</note>, O **Door-keeper of hell**, hateful to all, through **Saint James, son of Zebedee**, and brother of John, who among the rest was beloved by the Master and was a witness to the transfiguration and many miracles of Jesus. He was present on the Mount of Olives with Christ when He was about to pray to the Father: and finally, in Samaria, Judea, Spain, and in various parts of the world, he sowed the Gospel: and he was the first of the Apostles in Jerusalem to give testimony to Jesus Christ by the shedding of his blood.

I shake † your foundations, O **chamberlain and Charioteer of Satan**, and I overthrow and conquer you through **Saint James** <note>This refers to Saint James the Less, the first Bishop of Jerusalem</note>: whose holiness of life was so great that men would compete to touch the hem of his garment: who, when he most constantly preached Christ the Son of God, was attacked with stones, and soon after was led to the highest point of the Temple and cast down from there: who, with his legs shattered and lying half-dead, stretched out his hands to heaven and prayed to God for their salvation: and during this prayer, his head was struck heavily with a **fuller's club** <term>fuller's club: a heavy wooden tool used by cloth-washers to beat wool; it became the traditional symbol of this Saint's martyrdom</term>: and he returned his soul to God.

I thrust you down † into the **kitchen of Hell**, O **Tartarean Churl**, through the noble and **Holy John, Apostle and Evangelist**, who reclined upon the breast of the Lord at the Supper: and to whom the secrets of heaven were revealed: for he drank the streams of the Gospel from that very sacred fountain of the Lord's breast, and by the privilege of a special love he deserved to be honored by God more highly than the others, and as a virgin was chosen by the Lord and more beloved among the rest: to whom Christ, while on the Cross, commended the Virgin [Mary].

I defile you †, O **contagious Cobbler** <note>original: "Cerdo" — a term for a low-class craftsman or laborer, used here as a humiliating insult to the demon</note>, through **Saint Thomas**, who while he felt the wounds of the flesh in his Master, healed the wounds of infidelity in us: he delivered the precepts of the Christian faith and life to the Parthians, Medes, Persians, Brahmins, Hyrcanians, Bactrians, and Indians, once their idols were destroyed: and finally, full of doctrine, holiness, and the greatness of miracles, he decorated his Apostleship with the crown of martyrdom.

I skin you † <note>original: "Decortico" — a direct reference to the method of Bartholomew's death</note>, O **bitter Executioner**, through **Saint Bartholomew**, who preached the Gospel to the nations in India and Greater Armenia, and converted very many to Christ: for whom, while living and after many labors and calamities endured by him, his skin [was stripped...]

[his skin] is cruelly torn off, and his head is cut off; and thus he gave back his soul to God. 

I stone † <note>original: "Dilapido" — referencing the stoning of Stephen to symbolically crush the demon</note> you, O **gloomy Charcoal-burner** <note>original: "Carbonarie caliginose" — a derogatory title for the demon, implying he is blackened by the fires of hell</note>, through **Saint Stephen**, who was full of the Holy Spirit. He, looking up into Heaven, said: *I see the Heavens opened, and the Son of Man standing at the right hand of the power of God.* But the Accursed ones <note>The persecutors of Stephen</note> stopped their ears and, rushing at him, stoned him with one mind. And while he was shaken by the whirlwind of clattering stones, he knelt down and cried out with a loud voice: *Lord, do not hold this sin against them.* And when he had said this, he fell asleep in the Lord, safely following the first footsteps of Jesus Christ.

I roast † you, O **starving swine**, through **Saint Lawrence the Deacon**, over whom savage cruelty had no power. For he was placed upon an iron grate which possessed the power of burning, and with fire placed beneath it so that the torture would be more vehement and the punishment prolonged, they bound his limbs as they lay upon the gridiron. And while they piled up the burning coals, the Deacon of Christ insulted them, crying out: "I worship my God, and Him alone I serve; I confess You as Lord, and being roasted, I give thanks." And so Lawrence, burning with desire for Christ, did not feel the fire of his persecutor; for the flame could not overcome the charity of Christ. And finally, O Accursed One, I do not doubt that you know how much he endured in his persecution, and how he illuminated the whole world with his passion.

I muzzle † <note>original: "Capistro" — to bind with a halter or muzzle, as one would an animal</note> you, O **dung-covered sow**, through the **Holy Innocents**, those flowers of the Martyrs, whom the impiety of Herod snatched as infants from their mothers' breasts and slew. These are they who were not defiled with women, for they are virgins; they follow the Lamb without spot, singing a new song before the Throne of God and the Lamb, the earth Resounding with their voices, blessing Him who lives forever and ever.

I cast you down †, O **suspended cut-purse** <note>original: "crumeniseca" — literally a pickpocket or one who cuts purses; used here to label the demon as a thief of souls</note>, into the depths of the Abyss, through **Saint Clement**, to whom Saint Peter entrusted the Church. For the faith of JESUS CHRIST, he endured much and was renowned for miracles; an anchor was tied to his neck and, while weeping he said: *Father, receive my spirit,* he was cast into the deep sea. There, the Lord gave him a dwelling in the form of a marble temple, prepared by the hands of angels.

I tear † you, O enemy of Jesus Christ, through Saint—

<meta>This page continues the liturgical sequence of exorcisms where the physical sufferings of Christian martyrs are ritually turned against a possessing demon. Each saint's specific mode of death becomes a symbolic weapon used by the exorcist.</meta>

<page-num>914</page-num>
<header>THE COMPLETION OF THE ART</header>
<meta>catchword: flayed/tortured</meta>

...[Saint] **Ignatius**, who carried the name of Jesus written in golden letters upon his heart. Desiring to be set free and to be with Christ, he said: "I hope I may enjoy the beasts; I pray they will be swift to bring about my end and my torment, and that they may be enticed to devour me, lest—as with other martyrs—they dare not touch my body. Let fire, the cross, beasts, the breaking of bones, the dividing of limbs, the crushing of the whole body, and all the torments of the devil come upon me, so long as I may enjoy Christ." And as he approached the roaring lions with a burning desire for suffering, he said: "I am the wheat of Christ; I shall be ground by the teeth of beasts, so that I may be found to be pure bread." And having suffered in this way, he was received into the bosom of **JESUS CHRIST**.

I cast you down headlong †, O **treacherous boar**, through **Saints Faustinus and Jovita**, who were cast into many prisons, thrown to wild beasts and flames, and—with hands and feet bound—submerged in the sea, yet were miraculously rescued unharmed. Through the most exquisite and bitter punishments, they were tested like gold in the furnace. Finally, after many had been converted to Christ, they were struck with the sword and flew safely to the Lord.

I root you out †, O **envious Crocodile**, through **Saint Simeon**, who, when he had reached the age of one hundred and twenty years, was afflicted with many of the most bitter pains of the cross and the same kinds of suffering that our Savior had endured.

I overwhelm you †, O **horned beast**, through **Saints Gervasius and Protasius**, who, when they were compelled to deny Christ and sacrifice to profane idols, refused with loathing. For this reason, by the Tyrant's command, Gervasius was beaten for so long that he expired amidst the lashes, and Protasius was crushed and struck with the sword; both flew to the Heavens.

I pierce you with arrows † and shatter your ranks, O **twisting serpent**, through **Saint Sebastian**, who, seeing Christians trembling in the midst of torments, so strengthened them with his encouragement that they offered themselves willingly to the torturers for the sake of Jesus Christ. For this reason, he was ordered to be tied to a stake and pierced with arrows; but having recovered his health, he was beaten with rods for so long until he gave up his soul to God.

I flay you † <note>original: "Excarnifico" — literally to tear the flesh from the bone</note>, O **inventor of all evils**, through **Saint Boniface**, who, while being tortured with various pains for the Christian faith, encouraged others to constancy and to the enduring of punishments. Having been seized, and with iron claws ex— <note>the text cuts off as Boniface is being tortured with iron combs/claws</note>

# THIRD PART OF EXORCISMS #

<meta>catchword: through</meta>

...tortured, with sharp reeds driven into the flesh under his fingernails, and molten lead poured into his mouth; soon after, he was cast head-first into a cauldron of boiling pitch, and finally struck with an axe. Thus, illustrated by many miracles and having confounded the demon, he passed to the Lord. I liquefy † you, you **foul menial spirit** <note>original: *mediaštine fœtide*. A "mediastinus" was a low-ranking slave, used here as a term of contempt for the demon.</note>, through **Saint Erasmus**, who was beaten with lead-weighted clubs and drenched with molten lead, resin, sulfur, boiling pitch, wax, and oil, yet escaped unharmed; and being bound with iron chains, and through the most grievous torments and punishments, he overcame all through divine power, converting many to the faith of Christ, and at last, full of miracles, he attained the noble palm of martyrdom.

I crush † you, **impious phantom**, through **Saint Cyriacus**: by whose prayers the daughter of the King of the Persians and many others were freed from wicked spirits; and finally, after many miracles, he was drenched in liquid pitch and stretched upon a rack <note>original: *catasta*. An elevated platform or rack used for torture.</note>, and along with his companions **Largo**, **Smaragdus**, and thirty others, he was struck with an axe.

I tame † you with fists, O **infernal blacksmith**, through **Saints Cyprian and Justin**: the latter, having abandoned the magical arts, followed Christ; and both were beaten with blows and whips and cast into a mixture full of boiling pitch, fat, and wax; and finally, struck with an axe, they passed to the Lord.

I ensnare † you, **blackest Raven**, through **Saint Hippolytus**, who for the name of Christ was dragged by wild horses through places thick with thistles and briars, and with his body torn to pieces, he gave back his spirit to God.

I bend † you, **dreadful serpent**, through **Saint Vincent**: whose unconquered soul—since it could be drawn away from the faith of Jesus Christ neither by the force or bitterness of torments, nor by the gentleness of words—was placed upon a gridiron with burning coals beneath, and he was flayed <note>original: *excarnificatus*</note> with iron hooks and scorched with glowing plates. Having overcome the cruelty of fire, iron, and torturers, he flew as a victor to the heavenly crown of martyrdom.

I drip [liquid] upon † you, O **Basilisk of the throat** <note>original: *Basilice faucitate*. This likely refers to the demon's influence on speech or the "breath of life."</note>, through **Saint Timothy**, who, because he could not bear the sacrifice due to the one God alone to be made to the idols of demons, and therefore tried to turn the people away from the sacrifice to Diana, was overwhelmed with stones and fell asleep in the Lord.

I defile † you like an **adulterous viper**, through...

...through **Saint Blaise**, who healed a boy whose soul was departing because of a bone stuck across his throat, and many other sick people; and refusing to sacrifice to the [pagan] gods, he was beaten with rods, torn apart on the rack with iron combs, and finally, his head having been removed, he gave an illustrious testimony of faith to Christ. I wound † you, O **rusty asp** <note>original: *Aspis ferrugineæ*. A derogatory name for the demon; "ferrugineus" can mean rust-colored, dark, or foul.</note>, through **Saints Processus and Martinian**, who, when they were being forced in vain to renounce the faith they had accepted, had their faces bruised with stones, their teeth crushed, were twisted on the rack, and beaten with clubs. Finally, while singing "Blessed be the name of the Lord," they were struck with the axe. I entangle † you, O **stinking beast**, through **Saint Marcellus**, who, neglecting to sacrifice to the idols, was assigned to a stable <note>original: *catabulum*. A public stable or animal pen; the saint was forced to perform menial labor among animals as a humiliation.</note> to take care of the beasts. He led his life in constant fasts and prayers, and after many holy ordinations for the beauty of the holy Church, he was worn out by the filth and stench of the place, and fell asleep in the Lord. I poison † you, O **swollen toad**, through **Saints Marius and Martha**, spouses, and **Audifax and Abachum** their sons: who were seized while burying the bodies of martyrs and venerating them. But since they were moved neither by the threats of the impious nor by terror to sacrifice to the gods, they were weakened with clubs, dragged with ropes, burned with glowing plates, and their flesh torn with iron hooks. And finally, with their hands cut off and tied around their necks, they were led through the middle of the city and killed. I turn you to dust † [or pulverize you], O **inextricable spider**, through **Saint Ambrose**, Doctor of the Church, in whose infancy a swarm of bees settled on his mouth to foreshadow his divine eloquence from God. He most fiercely defended the Catholic faith and ecclesiastical discipline; he converted many **Arians** <term>Arians: followers of a 4th-century theological belief that Christ was not co-eternal with God the Father, a view the Church declared heretical.</term> and other heretics to the truth. Among these, he gave birth to the brightest light of the Church, **Augustine**, for Jesus Christ. When he had completed his greatest labors and cares for the Church of God, and having written many outstanding books, he predicted the day of his death, on which, with his hands shaped in the likeness of a cross, he returned his soul to God. I gnaw † you, O **louse-ridden swineherd** <note>original: *porcarie pædicose*. A highly insulting term implying both filth (swineherd) and parasitic infestation.</note>, through **Saint Gregory the Great**, who daily [invited] pilgrims to his table...

...he used to provide, in which he received both an Angel and the Lord of Angels in the guise of a pilgrim. He restored the Catholic faith, which had been undermined in many places; he adorned the Church with most holy institutes and laws, and established many things in various Councils. He wrote many books, which, as the Holy Spirit dictated them, was often seen sitting by his head in the form of a dove <note>A common iconographic motif showing the Holy Spirit inspiring Saint Gregory's writings.</note>. And finally, after many wonders which he did, said, wrote, and decreed for the glory of God and the confusion of demons—and after performing many miracles—il he was called away to heavenly bliss.

**I extinguish** † you and your stench, O **donkey beaten with sticks** <note>original: *asine baculis cæse*. The ritual uses humiliating animal imagery to degrade the demon's pride.</note>, through **Saint Augustine**, who, after receiving baptism, never ceased preaching the word of God. Many followed him in holiness of life; he wrote many things with such piety and subtlety that he greatly illuminated Christian doctrine. He destroyed heresies, repelled errors, and prostrated heretics, freeing many from their delusions. And finally, reading the Psalms of David that pertain to repentance <note>The "Penitential Psalms," which Augustine reportedly had hung on the walls around his deathbed.</note>, which were placed in his sight, while shedding many tears, he passed into Heaven.

**I dissolve** † you and your pride, O **agent of evil deeds**, through **Athanasius**, that most fierce champion of the Catholic religion, who for Christ’s sake hid himself in a dry cistern for six years, and lay hidden in his father's tomb for four months. At last, having been divinely rescued from so many and such great dangers, he flew up among the choirs of Angels. His life and death were ennobled by many miracles; and for the illustration of the Catholic faith, he wrote many things piously and brilliantly.

**I impose** † an iron and servile yoke upon you, O **treacherous mule-driver** <note>original: *mulio perfidiose*.</note>, through **Saint Martin**, the gem of priests, who was a magnificent raiser of the dead, and through whom such great miracles shine for us; he who neither feared to die nor refused to live for the people of God. At his passing, Michael with an army of Angels and the whole company of all the saints met him; and he placed his soul, honored with heavenly hymns, in the bosom of Abraham.

**I fight against** † you, O **lowest little creature** because of your apostasy, through **Saint Nicholas**, who in his infancy sucked his nurse’s milk only once in the evening on the fourth and sixth days of the week <note>Wednesday and Friday, traditional fast days. Legend says Nicholas was so holy he began fasting as a newborn.</note>; which custom of fasting [he kept] for the rest...

...he held for the rest of his life, and who calmed a terrible storm with prayer; he always cherished chastity, and constantly practiced persistence in prayer, vigils, abstinence, generosity, hospitality, and gentleness. **I consume** † you with pain, O **gluttonous glutton**, through **Saint Bernard**, who was so devoted to fasting that whenever food had to be taken, he seemed to undergo a torment. He exercised himself wonderfully in vigils and prayers; and cherishing Christian poverty, he led a heavenly life on earth, estranged from all care and desire for fleeting things. Humility, mercy, and kindness shone in him. Moreover, he was so addicted to contemplation that he scarcely used his senses except for duties of piety. And finally, after much divinely-handed-down teaching, and illustrious for his miracles, he fell asleep in the Lord.

**I make an attack** † upon you, O **lustful one**, through **Saint Benedict**, who, when burning torches of lust were suggested by the Devil, rolled himself in briars for so long until, his body being torn, the feeling of pleasure was overwhelmed by pain. He shattered the vessel containing a poisoned drink with the sign of the Holy Cross; he smashed the idol of Apollo, overturned the altar, and burned the sacred groves at Monte Cassino. And finally, with eyes lifted to Heaven and praying, he breathed out his soul in the hands of his disciples, which the disciples saw adorned with a most precious cloak and shining lamps.

**I speak against you** †, O enemy of the Cross of Christ, through **Saint Symphorosa**: who was a Teacher of the faith to her sons and stood as a leader to them in their martyrdom. The throat of the first was pierced with iron; the chest of the second was stabbed; the heart of the third was struck through; the navel of the fourth was pierced; the fifth was cut limb from limb; the sixth was fixed with spears; and the seventh was divided into two parts from the chest. And Symphorosa the Mother, with a stone tied to her neck, was cast into the depths; and thus, confessing Christ, eight victims most pleasing to God were sacrificed. 

**I gnash my teeth at you** †, O root of all evils, through **Saint Euphemia**, who constantly suffered various kinds of torments—the rods, the rack, the wheels, and fire—and, torn apart by lions, she received the palm of martyrdom in the confession of Jesus Christ. 

**I root you out** †, O cruel Minister, through **Saint Apollonia**, who (having despised the Idols) when she preached that Jesus Christ was the true God to be worshipped, had her teeth beaten and plucked out <note>Saint Apollonia is the patron saint of dentistry because of this specific torture.</note>, and her body being consumed in the fire, she flew away to eternal life.

...to [vindic]ate <note>The Latin text begins mid-word, likely "vindicare" (to avenge or claim) or "communicare" (to share).</note>, through all the Saints, not only men, but also women: Not only young boys, but also tender virgins: who for the Holy faith feared not iron chains, nor dark prisons, nor harsh lashes, nor inhuman exiles—nor hunger, nor fire, nor the tearing of wild beasts, nor boiling oil and molten lead, nor cauldrons full of boiling pitch, fat, and wax, nor the attacks of bulls, nor pits full of serpents: nor the grinding of teeth: nor stones: nor drowning in rivers: nor steep precipices: nor the tearing and butchering of their bodies: nor, finally, the most exquisite or bitter punishments. Instead, for the love of Jesus Christ, they fought even unto death, and having prostrated their enemies and exulting in the captured prize, clothed in White Robes and bearing Trophies, they follow the Lamb wherever He goes, singing:

> Holy, Holy, Holy Lord God of Hosts <note>original: "Sanctus, Sanctus, Sanctus Dominus Deus Sabaoth"</note>: Heaven and Earth are full of your glory, Hosanna in the highest: Blessed is he who comes in the name of the Lord, Hosanna in the highest.

Behold how they are now counted among the children of God: like stars shining for all eternity, and shouting for joy in glory because of the exultation of their hearts. Flee, therefore, through the merits of all the holy men and women of God. For it is hard for you to kick against the goad <note>A reference to Acts 9:5; "kicking against the goads" means to fruitlessly resist a superior power.</note>; flee, you opposing parties! The lion of the tribe of Judah, the root of David, has conquered. Take flight, you cursed ones; depart quickly! Why do you delay? Be gone, now! Away with your delays! Remove yourselves from here! Leave, relinquish, and desert this creature of Almighty God, of the Most High God, of the True God. Leave her <note>The Latin "eam" refers to the person being exorcised, indicating the subject in this instance is female.</note>, to whom the portion of the elect is promised; for she is a living and vigorous member of Christ, along with the other fellow-members of that same head: eating together of one and the same bread, as one who believes, hopes, professes, and loves the communion of the saints and the elect of Jesus Christ. Depart immediately, and suddenly fall into hell <note>original: "tartara"</note>; now go headlong to the caverns of the infernal regions. In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the † Holy Spirit, Amen. Let it be, Let it be, Amen.

# EXORCISM IV.
## *Drawn from the Psalter.* <note>The Psalter refers to the Book of Psalms. This section uses biblical poetry to structure the ritual of casting out spirits.</note>

> God, † come to my assistance.
> Lord, † make haste to help me.
> Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:
> As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end.
> Amen. Alleluia.
> Lord, hear my prayer.
> And let my cry come unto you.
> The Lord be with you.
> And with your spirit.

->Let us pray.<-

> Lord God, who chose this house for the invocation of your name, that it might be a house of Prayer and petition for your people: Grant, we beseech you, vengeance upon this Demon; let him fall in battle. Remember his blasphemies <note>original: "blasphemariū," referring to the insults the demon hurls against the divine.</note>, and do not permit him to persevere in his harassment. But send your Angel Michael, who shall strike and crush him today, so that all may know that he has spoken evilly against your holy things. And judge him according to his malice. Do not repay us, Lord, according to our transgressions, but according to your mercies. Crush, O Lord, the great enemy: so that with praise, palm branches, cymbals, hymns, and songs, we may praise you for ever and ever, Amen.

### HERE HE SHOULD LAY HANDS
->upon the head of the possessed creature,<-
->saying:<-

## EXORCISM.

> I exorcise you, [Name] <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen*, indicating where the exorcist should speak the name of the possessed person.</note>, a weak creature of God, by the mercy of God the Father, by the intercession of God the Son, by the gentleness of God the Holy Spirit: that you may become a clean creature, purged of every stain of iniquity, to receive the power of the Holy Spirit coming upon you. And may you cast out all the wickedness of Satan at the invocation of the Most High.

> Through him, who descending to the underworld <note>original: "inferos." This refers to the 'Harrowing of Hell,' where Christ is believed to have descended to liberate the souls of the righteous after his death.</note> broke the gates of brass, and confidently leading forth the Holy Fathers, sits at the right hand of God in the glory of the Father. Amen.

->Let us pray.<-

> Almighty eternal God, I invoke your mercy: that you may deign to exorcise † and bless † this weak creature <note>original: "creaturam infirmam." In liturgical Latin, "creature" refers to any created being—here specifically the person or object being delivered from evil.</note> by your piety, so that every unclean spirit may no longer have a place in it: But may the Host of your Angels descend to guard it, and protect it for ever and ever. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

->with a scourge made of three twigs<-
->of blessed Olive, let him say.<- <note>The use of olive twigs represents a physical enactment of the spiritual battle; the olive branch is traditionally a symbol of peace and divine favor, used here to drive out the "pestilence" of the demon.</note>

### CONJURATION.

> Flee, in the name of the Almighty and of the striking God, all you Cursed ones: who walk in the counsel of the ungodly: and stand in the way of sinners, and sit in the seat of pestilence. <note>This section is a direct adaptation of Psalm 1, traditionally interpreted as a contrast between the righteous and the damned.</note>
> For the Lord will cast you out from his face, like the dust which the wind drives from the face of the earth.
> The ungodly shall not rise in judgment, nor sinners in the counsel of the just: but the way of the ungodly shall perish.
> He who dwells in the Heavens shall laugh at you: and the Lord shall mock you.
> And he shall speak to you in his anger: and in his fury he shall trouble you.
> The Lord shall break our adversaries with a rod of iron, and like a potter's vessel. <note>original: "vas figuli." A biblical metaphor for something easily shattered by divine power.</note>
> He has struck you who oppose us without cause: he has shattered the teeth of sinners.

The Evil ones shall not dwell near the Lord, nor shall the unjust remain before his eyes.
And he hates you workers of iniquity; he will destroy all those who speak lies.
Because there is no truth in your mouth; your heart is empty.
Lord, rebuke them in your fury: and in your anger correct them.
Depart from this creature <note>original: "creatura." As noted previously, this refers to the person being delivered from the demon.</note> all you workers of iniquity:
For the Lord has heard the voice of our weeping.
Be ashamed and greatly troubled, lest at any time, like lions, you seize our souls.
Pursue, and seize, and trample into hell <note>original: "inferno."</note> these rebels, and lead their glory into the dust, O Lord.
Your wickedness shall be consumed, for the Lord has brandished his sword, he has bent his bow, and made it ready.
You have brought forth injustice, O Cursed One <note>original: "Maledicte." A direct address to the demon.</note>; you have conceived sorrow, and given birth to iniquity.
You have opened a pit and dug it out, and you have fallen into the ditch which you made. <note>This imagery, taken from Psalm 7, suggests that the demon’s own traps for the soul will eventually cause its own downfall.</note>
Your sorrow shall be turned back upon your own head, and your iniquity shall descend upon your own crown.
Destroy, O Lord, your enemy and the avenger: in the severity of your Justice.
You have rebuked them, and the impious one has perished: you have blotted out their name forever, and for age upon age.
Add to these things swords: and destroy their cities.
Your memory has perished with a sound, and the Lord remains forever.
In the snare which you have hidden, your foot has been caught.
Let the sinners be turned back into hell: those who forget God.
Whose mouth is full of cursing, bitterness, and deceit: under their tongue is labor and sorrow.
You sit in ambush to kill the innocent, and you lie in wait in secret, like a lion in his den.

*Lord, break the arm of the sinner and of the evil one in his own snare.* <note>original: "laqueo suo." This continues the theme of the demon falling into its own traps.</note>
*Because he has bent his bow, he has prepared his arrows in the quiver, that he might shoot in the darkness those who are upright of heart.*
*He shall rain snares upon sinners: fire, sulfur, and the spirit of storms shall be the portion of their cup.*
*They have spoken false things, each one to his neighbor: they spoke with deceitful lips in their mouth and heart.*
*May the Lord destroy all your deceitful lips and the boastful tongue.*
*Their throat is an open grave: they have acted deceitfully with their tongues: the poison of asps is under their lips.* <note>The "poison of asps" refers to the deceptive and spiritually deadly nature of the demon's speech.</note>
*Their mouth is full of cursing and bitterness: their feet are swift to shed innocent blood.*
*You are brought to nothing in his sight, O Evil One: but he glorifies those who fear the Lord.* <note>original: "Maligne." A direct address to the spirit being cast out.</note>
*You have invaded this Creature of God <note>original: "Creaturam Dei." Referring to the person being exorcised.</note> like a lion ready for its prey.*
*Encircle them with the sorrows of death, and let the torrents of iniquity disturb them.*
*The sorrows of Hell <note>original: "Inferni."</note> have surrounded you; the snares of death have overtaken you.*
*The earth shook and trembled; the foundations of the mountains were disturbed and moved because he was angry with you.*
*The Most High thundered from Heaven, and hail and coals of fire fell upon you.*
*He sent out his arrows and scattered you; he multiplied lightnings and troubled you.*
*The Lord will repay you according to your iniquity, and he will humble the eyes of the proud.*
*Pursue these enemies of ours, O Lord, and I shall seize them.*
*Crush these, O Jesus Christ; in your name let them fall under my feet, and scatter them: I shall not turn back until they fail.* <note>The exorcist here takes an active, dominant role, commanding the spirits to fall "under my feet" through the power of Christ.</note>

*And crush them like dust before the face of the wind; blot them out like the mud of the streets.*

*Let your heart be like melting wax, because of your iniquity and your pride.*
*You lifted up your gates, and the eternal gates were raised: because the King of Glory has entered.* <note>A reference to Psalm 24, frequently used in exorcisms to symbolize Christ's triumphal entry into the "stronghold" of the possessed person to evict the demon.</note>
*He has crushed you like a calf of Lebanon: and the beloved like the son of unicorns.* <note>original: "filius unicornium." In the Vulgate Bible, this term (monoceros/unicornis) denotes great, untamable strength; here, it signifies the overwhelming power of God breaking the demonic presence.</note>

*Why do you glory in malice, you who are powerful in iniquity?*
*Your tongue has devised injustice all day long; like a sharp razor, you have practiced deceit.*
*You have loved malice more than kindness: iniquity more than speaking righteousness.*

# ->THE COMPLETION OF THE ART<-

You have loved all words of ruin, O deceitful tongue.
Because of this, God will destroy you in the end; He will pluck you away and cast you out of your dwelling place, <note>original: "Tabernaculo tuo." In an exorcism, the "tabernacle" or tent refers to the body of the possessed person which the demon has illicitly occupied.</note> and uproot you from the land of the living.
Cast them down and divide their tongues, for I see iniquity and injustice in their midst.
Let death come upon you, and let you descend alive into Hell. <note>original: "infernum."</note>
Hear me, O God, and scatter these who defile your vineyard, and lead them down into the pit of destruction. <note>original: "interitus."</note>
They have prepared a snare for our feet, but You have brought those lying in wait for us into disgrace.
They dug a pit before my face, and they have fallen into it themselves.
But God will crush your teeth in your mouths; the Lord will break the great teeth of these lions.
You shall be taken away like wax that melts; fire has fallen upon you, and you have not seen the sun; thus He shall swallow you up in His wrath.
The just man shall rejoice when he sees the vengeance; <note>original: "vindictam."</note> he shall wash his hands in your blood.
And You, O Lord, shall laugh at them; You shall bring them to nothing.
Scatter them by Your power, and bring them down, O Lord my protector.
Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered; and let those who hate Him flee from before our face.
As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish; as wax melts before the fire, so let the sinners perish before the face of God.
You have ascended on high, O Lord; You have led captivity captive; You have received gifts for men. <note>This verse from Psalm 68 is used here to celebrate Christ's authority to liberate the "captive" soul from the demon.</note>
Truly, God will crush the heads of His enemies: the hairy crown of those who walk in their sins.
Scatter those who desire wars; they shall come bound from Egypt; Ethiopia shall stretch out her hands to God. <note>In the mystical geography of this period, Egypt and Ethiopia represented the most distant and ancient lands, signifying that no corner of the world or the spirit realm is beyond God's reach.</note>
Pour out Your wrath upon them, and let the fury of Your anger trample them down.
For they persecute those whom You have struck; therefore, add pain to their wounds.
Add iniquity to their iniquity, so that they may not remain in Your Tabernacle.

--- Page 151 ---
<meta>This page continues the aggressive recitation of imprecatory Psalms, intensifying the verbal assault on the demon. The text shifts from general pleas for protection to specific, historical precedents where God crushed the enemies of Israel, using these biblical victories as a spiritual blueprint for the current exorcism.</meta>

<page-num>969</page-num>
# ->THIRD PART OF THE EXORCISM<-

May those seeking our souls be confounded: and may they be caught in their own pride.

You who broke their powers—the bow, the shield, and the war—crush them.

They have devised a malicious plan against us, and plotted against your Creature. <note>In the context of an exorcism, "Creature" (Latin: *creatura*) refers to the possessed person, who is a created being of God currently occupied by an intruder.</note>

Therefore, do to them as you did to Midian and Sisera: as you did to Jabin at the brook of Kishon. <note>These are references to the Book of Judges, where God miraculously defeated the enemies of the Israelites.</note>

Make them like Oreb and Zeeb, and like Zebah and Zalmunna. <note>These were Midianite princes and kings executed by Gideon; the exorcist invokes their total defeat as a fate for the demon.</note>

My God, make them like whirling dust, and like straw before the face of the wind.

As fire that burns the forest, and as a flame burning the mountains.

So shall you pursue and disturb them: in your wrath and in your tempest.

May they be confounded and trampled down forever and ever.

Place them in the lower pit: in darkness and in the shadow of death.

You have humbled the proud one like a wounded man: with the arm of your power you have scattered your enemies.

You shall walk upon the asp: and you shall trample the lion and the dragon. <note>This verse from Psalm 91 is a cornerstone of exorcism rites, symbolizing Christ’s—and by extension, the priest's—authority over various forms of evil.</note>

Behold, you our enemies shall perish: and you who work iniquity shall be destroyed.

Repay them for their iniquity: and in their malice destroy them: destroy them, O Lord our God.

Deliver us from the arrow flying by day, from the plague that walks in the darkness, from the assault and the Noonday Demon. <note>original: "Dæmonio meridiano." The "Noonday Demon" was a famous concept in medieval theology, originally associated with the spiritual fatigue of monks, but here treated as a literal entity of mid-day assault.</note>

Let fire go before you: and you shall set fire to our enemies all around.

He who practices pride shall not dwell within my house; he who speaks wicked things did not direct his path before my eyes.

In the morning I killed all the sinners of the earth: that I might destroy all workers of iniquity from the City.

The Lord has crushed the gates of bronze: and broken the bars of iron.

<page-num>970</page-num>
# COMPLETION OF THE RITE
<note>original: "COMPLEMENTUM ARTIS". In a ritual context, "Art" refers to the specific practice or methodology of exorcism.</note>

He rebuked the proud: cursed are those who turn away from his commandments.

The just Lord shall cut the necks of sinners: let all who hate Zion be confounded and turned backward. <note>Zion here represents the Church or the soul of the afflicted person which the demon "hates" and occupies.</note>

Let them be as grass of the rooftops: which withers before it is plucked up. <note>original: "foenum rectorum". This is likely a transcription or printer's error for "tectorum" (rooftops), referencing Psalm 129:6, comparing the demon's power to weak grass growing on a roof with no deep roots.</note>

Let burning coals fall upon you: you shall be cast into the fire. Destroy all who trouble us, for we are your servants.

Let their table become a snare before them, and a trap: and a stumbling block, and a retribution.

Let their eyes be darkened so they may not see, and forever bow down their backs.

The wicked shall be destroyed from the earth: and the way of the wicked shall be dark.

May the Lord set his face against you: so that you flee, even when no one pursues. <note>This invokes a state of divine terror, where the demon is driven by fear even without a visible attacker.</note>

God has dried up the roots of proud leaders, and has planted the humble.

The Lord has overturned your lands and destroyed them even to the foundation.

He has withered and scattered you: and the memory of you has ceased in Heaven and on earth.

The inheritance of the children of sinners shall perish: and with their offspring, a constant reproach.

Strike, O Lord, the backs of these enemies: who hate you, let them not rise again.

Because of your sin, you have fallen into a curse: and your peace shall always be in a curse.

The mourning of the damned is in their spirit: and the name of the wicked shall be blotted out.

The Lord has broken the staff of the wicked: the rod of the rulers, in indignation, and with an incurable wound.

Let them be confounded who persecute me, but let not me be confounded: let them be afraid, but let not me be afraid: bring upon them the day of affliction, and crush them, O Lord.

&nbsp;&nbsp;your pride.

Christ will scatter you with his gaze when he sits upon the throne of his Judgment.

Your inheritance lacks a blessing: because you have returned evil for good.

# EXORCISM V.
### *Called the Exorcism of Rebukes.* <note>original: "Improbationum". This refers to a series of formal disapprovals or condemnations meant to shame and weaken the demon.</note>

> O God, † come to my assistance.
> O Lord, † make haste to help me.
> Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:
> As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world
> without end. Amen, Alleluia.

Lord, hear my prayer.
And let my cry come unto you.
The Lord be with you.
And with your spirit.

->Let us pray,<-

> Almighty eternal God, who when you have been angry, show mercy, and in the time of tribulation <note>original: "tribulationis", referring to times of great distress or suffering</note> forgive sins: To you, Lord, we turn our faces; to you we direct our eyes. We ask, Lord, that you snatch this creature <note>original: "Creaturam"; in this context, it refers to the possessed person as a created being of God.</note> from the hands of demons: For you do not delight in destruction: but after the storm, you make it calm: and after weeping and wailing, you pour in exultation: May your name, O God of Israel, be blessed forever: and hear our prayers in the sight of your glory. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

# HERE LET HIM LAY HANDS UPON
->the head of the possessed person, and say:<-

## E X O R C I S M,

> I exorcise you, N. <note>The letter "N." is a placeholder where the priest would speak the name of the possessed individual.</note>, a weak creature of God, by the † living God, by the † true God, by the † Holy God, by God the † Creator of all things, that you may show yourself clean against every disease, and the snares or diabolical powers of the enemy: and that you may be made a creature <note>In this ritual sense, it means the person is being "re-made" or restored as a pure vessel of God.</note> for the emptying out and driving away of the enemy, and all his power and rot: adjured in the name of our Lord Jesus Christ: Who is to come to judge the living and the dead, and the world by fire.

->Let us pray,<-

> We ask, almighty eternal God, for this your creature, that being fortified by the sign of the Holy † Cross of your only-begotten Son, and our Lord Jesus Christ, it may empty out, consume, and dry up every power of the Adversary <note>original: "Adversarii", a standard title for the Devil, meaning "the opponent."</note>, and let nothing unclean remain in it, nothing pestilential, but by the invocation of your name let it be clean and blessed. Through the same Lord Jesus Christ your Son: Who lives and reigns with you in the unity of the Holy Spirit, God, for ever and ever, Amen.

## ONCE THIS IS DONE, LET HIM SPRINKLE N.
->with holy water, and with eyes raised, let him say:<-

> The grace of our Lord Jesus † Christ, and the love † of God, and the fellowship of the Holy † Spirit, be with us all. <note>original: "Gratia Domini nostri Jesu Christi..." This is a standard liturgical blessing used to fortify the space and the participants before the command begins.</note>

## CONJURATION.
I adjure † you, Cursed Serpent, that you flee from this creature of God. In the name of the Father † and of the Son † and of the Holy † Spirit. For are you not 그 that rebellious Angel, who wished to seize by robbery <note>original: "per rapinam"; a theological reference to the devil trying to steal the status of divinity that belongs naturally to Christ.</note> the equality of divinity which Christ possessed by Nature? Are you not the one who did not ask the Lord for wisdom and prudence to discern judgment, but, lifted up in pride, fell from Heaven? Are you not that most foul Dragon, who prepared poison for the first man and, by your persuasion, seduced his offspring through the eating of forbidden food, and consigned them to the yoke of perpetual slavery? Are you not the one who boasted that man, deceived by your fraud, lacked divine gifts, and, stripped of the dowry of immortality, underwent the harsh sentence of death: But God was made man, and being subject to the laws of death, he crushed you. Were you not, filled with envy, the one who in Cain <note>original: "Caym"</note> killed the righteous Abel, and through him said to the Lord: My iniquity is greater than that I may deserve forgiveness? Have you not, with no one suggesting it to you, brought upon yourself the cause of eternal death? Were you not overturned in Sodom and Gomorrah? Did you not corrupt the earth and fill it with iniquity: until finally, like the Raven from the Ark, you were submerged in the waters of the Flood? <note>original: "Corvus de Arcâ"; referring to the raven Noah sent out which did not return, often interpreted in medieval exegesis as a symbol of the sinner or the devil.</note> Did you not in Ham reveal the nakedness of the Father? Did you not incite Esau against his brother Jacob, saying: The days of mourning for my father shall come, and I will kill my brother Jacob? Did you not in Shechem <note>original: "Sichem"</note> snatch away Dinah, and by force oppress the virgin? Did you not provide the fuel of hatred and envy to the brothers of Joseph, and plot to kill the Boy: and finally sell him to the Ishmaelites? Did you not, enraged at the mistress of Joseph, deliver him into prison?

Were you not destroyed in the prophets, worshipers, and priests of Baal, when the statue was shattered...

Are you not that thief who grumbled through Judas about the precious ointment with which Mary anointed the feet of the Lord? Are you not that proud one who even placed yourself in the Assembly of the Apostles through Judas? And in the end, when the traitor Judas was hanged, did you not both fall together into the depths of hell?

Are you not a murderer from the beginning, and you did not stand in the truth? Rather, when invited to the wedding of the eternal God, did you not ignore it? Did you not bury the <term>talent</term><note>original: "talentum"; a unit of currency, but here referring to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25, representing spiritual gifts or duties.</note> which the Lord had given you in a field armed with thorns? Therefore, you shall deservedly be on the left side! <note>The "left side" refers to the place of the damned in the Last Judgment.</note> Did you not squander your goods most proudly and luxuriously? And having become a wolf to others, you seize the sheep. Therefore, with hands and feet bound...

Refers to the sound of a frog.</note> your miseries and ruminate in all manner of filth? But the devout care nothing for your trickeries <note>original: "versutiis." Meaning clever but deceitful maneuvers.</note> and fantasies, however base and horrendous they may be. Instead, purging themselves and swiftly spitting out the poison, and hastily taking the remedy, they hurl all the phantasms back upon your own head; and thus, you wretch, you are despised and mocked. Are you not more wicked and more iniquitous than all the Wicked, because you are the root of all evils? You commit sacrileges and thefts, and you carry out robberies and plunderings; you wage wars and murders. Like a simonist, <note>term: "Simoniace" — referring to Simony, the sin of buying or selling sacred things or church offices, named after Simon Magus.</note> you sell and buy; you unjustly ask and receive; you trade falsely; and you are made fertile with filth, frauds, and deceits. You dissolve agreements and violate oaths, you corrupt testimonies and pervert judgments. Was it not through Judas the traitor that you attained the infamy of treachery, when, intoxicated by the poison of greed, you became so foolishly impious that you sold for a cheap price your Lord, who wished to redeem the whole world? You are distressed on every side because you wished to boast apart from God, and you did not establish yourself in true goodness and joy; for divine **Love** <note>original: "Charitas." In this context, it refers to the theological virtue of Charity or the selfless love of God.</note> conquers all things and expands all the powers of the soul, because no one is good except God alone, who is to be praised above all things and blessed in all things. Are you not the enemy who attacks us from both the right and the left, so that we might not reach the palm of victory of the **Blessed**? <note>Referring to the "Beatorum," the saints in heaven who have won their spiritual battles.</note> But Jesus Christ has granted us the necessary spiritual weapons, so that we may fix our hearts firmly upon him, with a pure will to suffer all things for his sake. And so, manfully and with a powerful hand, for the sake of his love, we willingly undergo all things: namely, labors and pains, temptations, vexations, anxieties, needs, infirmities, injuries, slanders, confusions, and corrections, because these things assist in the building of virtues; they test Christ’s **Recruit** <term>Tyronem: A Latin term for a new soldier or novice. In spiritual writing, it refers to a person newly committed to the "warfare" of Christian life.</term> and they forge a heavenly Crown. And thus, God will return to us an eternal reward for our brief labor, and infinite glory for our transitory confusion. For is the Lord not with us in every tribulation? Are you not the ancient enemy, opposing all good things, who never ceases from temptation? Day and night you devise heavy snares, hoping perhaps to cast the unwary into the trap of deception. But *watch and pray*, says the Lord, *lest you enter into temptation*. Are you not a wicked plague? For you drag souls away from true glory and despoil them of heavenly grace, which is Christ, whose name we praise and bless, and whose work we magnify. For he alone stretches out a helping hand; he alone is able to aid us and to strengthen us to such a degree that my face may no longer be turned toward different things. But let my heart be converted to him, and may you find rest: He is my truth, and my God is my mercy; the **Blessed Trinity**, to whom alone be praise, power, honor, and glory, through infinite ages of ages. Amen. Have you not been deprived of the light of God the Father, the splendor of Christ, and the illumination of the Holy Spirit? Did you not, through **Judas**, drink the blood of the Lord? But you were not satisfied, nor did you quench the thirst of the eternal fire, because you received the mysteries of Christ in fire? <note>This refers to the medieval belief that when Judas Iscariot took the bread at the Last Supper, the devil entered him, effectively "consuming" the sacrament to his own damnation.</note> Are you not the one who strove to place your nest of wickedness and pride in **Heaven**? But you quickly lost grace...

How you have fallen from Heaven, confused and impoverished, so that you might learn not to fly on your own wings, but to hope under the divine wings! Are you not that ancient enemy who strives in every way to hinder our desire for the good, and to empty out every devout exercise: the **veneration of the saints**, the pious memory of **Christ’s passion**, the useful recollection of sins, the guarding of one's own heart, and the firm purpose of growing in virtue? But, you most foul Spirit, blush with shame, you wretch; you are entirely unclean to bring such things to our ears. Depart from this **Creature of God** <note>original: "Creatura Dei." In this context, it refers to the human person being exorcised, who is a created being of God.</note>, you wicked Seducer; you shall have no part in them. Instead, Jesus will be with them like a strong warrior, and you shall stand confounded. Be silent, be muzzled, and depart from them, for it is written:

> The Lord is my light and my salvation; whom shall I fear?
> The Lord is the protector of my life; of whom shall I be afraid? If armies should stand against me, my heart shall not fear.
> The Lord is my helper and my redeemer.

Are you not, in the end, the root of all evils? Indeed, you have always remained confounded, just as it is read in the **Book of the Wars of God** <note>original: "libro bellorum Dei." This is a reference to a lost book mentioned in the Bible (Numbers 21:14), here used to emphasize the demon's historical defeats.</note>. I am a witness, as are Heaven and Earth and all things that are in them: Leave this Creature of God, you Accursed One! For what agreement is there between the **Lamb and the Wolf**? Between **Christ and Belial** <note>Belial is a Hebrew term used in the Bible to personify worthlessness or wickedness, often synonymous with the Devil.</note>? Between a holy man and a dog? Withdraw, therefore, in the name of the Father † and of the Son † and of the Holy † Spirit. Amen.

Are you the one who cast out the **First-Formed** <term>Protoplastum: A Greek-derived Latin term for Adam, the first created human being.</term> from the dwelling of Paradise? The one who armed Pharaoh to persecute the people of God? The one who finally incited the Hebrew people so that, with envy taking hold, they persecuted the Lord? Depart from the Servants of God, by whom you have often been overcome and sent away in confusion. For you were cast down in Cain; supplanted in Esau; prostrated in Goliath; hanged in Judas the traitor; and on that very **Cross of the Lord’s power** †, you were triumphed over and crushed along with your powers and dominions. Hide your head now, enemy, and humble yourself under the sign of the Divine Cross †. For you have no portion with the Servants of God, whose inheritance is the Kingdom of Christ. Get thee behind me, Satan! You shall be able to do no further harm to me or to this person in the name of my Christ. Amen.

<header>THIRD PART OF THE EXORCISTIC RITUAL.</header>

## EXORCISM 6.
### Of the Most Holy Trinity.

O God, $\dagger$ <note>The cross symbol indicates that the priest makes the Sign of the Cross at this moment.</note> come to my assistance.
O Lord, $\dagger$ make haste to help me.
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:
As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end, Amen, Alleluia.
O Lord, hear my prayer.
And let my cry come unto thee.
The Lord be with you.
And with your spirit.

->Let us pray.<-

Almighty and merciful God, look upon your **Creature** <note>original: "Creaturam." A term emphasizing that the person is a created being belonging to God, not the demon.</note> whom you have created and redeemed with the precious blood of your Son. Look upon them, tortured as they are by bitter pains, and expel all pains of soul and body from them. With the Demon cast down, mercifully restore internal and external health to them, so that, corrected by these pious chastisements, they may feel themselves healed by your medicine. Through the same Christ our Lord. Amen.

->HERE WITH HANDS PLACED UPON<-
->the head of the possessed person, let the priest say:<-

## EXORCISM.

I exorcise you, **[Name]**, a Creature of God tortured by Demons, by the Most Holy Trinity and the equality of the Persons, and the Unity of the divine essence: the Father, $\dagger$ and the Son, $\dagger$ and the Holy $\dagger$ Spirit. And by the excellence and power of the God-man <note>original: "hominis Dei," referring to Jesus Christ as both fully human and fully divine.</note>, I command that you have no fellowship with the apostate angels nor with their works. Instead, through the soul and body and the most bitter passion of Jesus Christ your Redeemer, may you <note>The priest here addresses the possessed person, praying for their cleansing.</note> be free and clean from all their iniquities. Depart, therefore, you most foul **ancient Serpent** <note>original: "Serpens antique." A traditional biblical title for the Devil, referencing the Garden of Eden.</note>, along with all your cursed spirits; I adjure you in the name of the Most Holy and undivided Trinity. Amen.

#### Let us pray.

Holy Lord, Almighty Father, eternal God, at the invocation of your name, graciously assist: and guard your **creature** <note>original: "creaturam," referring to the possessed person as a being created by God.</note> with your manifold piety: and once they are restored to health, raise them up with your right hand: protect them by your power, strengthen them by your virtue: and patronize your Holy Church and your Altars with all desired prosperity. Through Christ our Lord, Amen.

### CONJURATION.

I adjure you † Demons, and proud beasts, of whatever condition you may be, who by your malice harass this body signed with the mark of the Most Holy Cross †, whether you favor those things, or you who have been accustomed to aid [evil] power, that without delay, and without injury to anyone, you depart, by the one God, in whom there is nothing else, besides that which he is: and nothing in himself, except himself. I adjure † you, by God, whose will is Almighty, the most benevolent virtue, who is eternal light, immutable reason, supreme bliss, invisible, and inaccessible: visible in his works, and alone wonderful: than whom nothing better can be imagined: from whom all things, and through whom all things were made. I adjure † you, by the equality of the persons, which consists in three, according to the quantity of virtue: which is power: according to the quantity of duration, which is eternity, according to the quantity of magnitude, which is within all things, outside all things, and which contains and locates all things: Wherefore none precedes the other in eternity, or exceeds in magnitude, or surpasses in power. I adjure † you, by God, who is within all things, because he fills all things, and is everywhere present: who is outside all things, because he contains all things, and is not contained, who is above all things, because he presides over all things, nor [does] anything <note>The sentence continues onto the next page.</note>

<note>original vocabulary: Let us pray (Oremus), holy water (aqua benedicta), I adjure (Conjuro), Demons (Dæmones), Trinity (Trinitas), power (potentia), eternity (æternitas)</note>

<meta>This page continues the formal conjuration (the command for demons to depart) started on page 982. It utilizes a series of theological arguments based on the nature and attributes of God—His eternity, immensity, and incomprehensibility—to assert authority over the demonic entities.</meta>

<page-num>983</page-num>
<header>THE THIRD PART OF EXORCISM.</header>

...anything is equal to him: who is beneath all things, because he sustains all things, and without him nothing would subsist: and finally, who was before the creation of the world, where he is now, namely, in himself, since he is sufficient unto himself. I adjure <note>original: "Conjuro," a formal command or binding oath used in exorcism.</note> † you, by God, who is the beginning without beginning: and the end without end: For present time does not apply to God: nor the past, nor the future: because being is not an accident <note>In scholastic philosophy, an "accident" is a property that can change (like color or size) without changing the essence of a thing; for God, His existence is His very essence.</note> to God, but a subsisting truth, and a permanent cause. I adjure † you, by the breadth, length, height, and depth of God, spoken not literally, but metaphorically. By the breadth, therefore, of his charity, by which he recalls us from error: by the length of his patience, with which he waits for the wicked, overlooking their sins for the sake of repentance <note>original: "pœnitentiam"</note>: by the height of his wisdom, by which he surpasses all understanding: by the depth of his Justice, by which he condemns sinners. I adjure † you, by the immensity <note>original: "immensitatem," referring to God's omnipresence and lack of spatial limits.</note> of God, who is called immense: because he is everywhere through the presence of his majesty: and which immensity embraces four things in God: infinity, incomprehensibility, uncircumscriptibility <note>The quality of not being limited by space or physical boundaries.</note>, and eternity. I adjure † you, by the very incomprehensibility <note>original: "incompræhensibilitatem"</note> of God: although we may understand God as good without quality, great without quantity: Creator without need, Presiding without posture, containing all things without a boundary, whole everywhere without a location: everlasting without time, performing wonders <note>original: "murabilia," likely a variant of "mirabilia," meaning marvels or miracles.</note> without change to himself, and suffering nothing: yet he is incomprehensible, because whatever is in God, is God. I adjure † you, by the excellence of divine dignity, which is so great that nothing can be worthily or properly said of him: For the mind thinking of God fails, the senses do not perceive him, the tongue does not explain him, space does not contain him, time does not measure him, virtue does not reach him; he himself transcends even desires and vows: he alone knows the thoughts of men through himself: he creates things from nothing, he works in an instant, he rests in his working, by his authority he does what he wills with any creature: in the blink of an eye he raises the dead: he forgives sins: he pours in grace and preserves bodies in perpetual fire. I adjure † you, by the knowledge of God, who knows temporal things eternally: mutable things immutably—

thrice <note>original: "ter"</note>: contingent things infallibly: future things as if present <note>original: "præfentialiter"</note>: dependent things independently; created things uncreatedly: other things from himself, other things in himself, and other things through himself. I adjure <note>original: "Conjuro," a formal command or binding oath used in exorcism.</note> † you, by the **predestination** <term>The divine foreordaining of what will happen, especially concerning the salvation of souls.</term> of God, which through grace prepares glory, by the **calling**, which offers grace: by **justification** <term>The act by which God moves a willing person from the state of sin to the state of grace.</term>, which bestows it: and by the **magnificence**, which through grace multiplies glory. 

I adjure † you, by the **justice** of God, which renders to each what they deserve, fulfills what was promised, and perfects what is imperfect. I adjure † you, by the **mercy** of God which rewards beyond merit, and punishes less than what is deserved <note>original: "citra condignum"</note>, which leads all toward God: it brings back those who wander, raises those who fall, does not cease to sustain those who stand, and introduces those who arrive into glory. 

I adjure † you: by the **five notions** <term>In Scholastic theology, these are the characteristic properties used to distinguish the Persons of the Trinity.</term> of God, namely, **paternity** <note>Fatherhood</note>, **filiation** <note>Sonship</note>, **procession** <note>The Holy Spirit's origin from the Father</note>, **innascibility** <note>The Father's property of being unbegotten</note>, and **common spiration** <note>The joint action of Father and Son in producing the Holy Spirit</note>, and by all the names of God—essential, personal, and nominal. 

I adjure † you, by the **eternal Father**, most powerful, most wise, and most good: who is able, knows, and wills to communicate from his highest self: that is, to give the fullness of his majesty to another: For from eternity he begot for himself a Son equal to himself, to whom he communicated the essence of his divinity. 

I adjure † you, by the **manifold meanings** <note>original: "sumptionem," referring to the various ways the title of "Father" is applied</note> of paternity: For God is called Father by eternal generation, predestination, creation, redemption, sacramental generation <note>baptism</note>, instruction in faith, adoption by grace, and the bestowal of glory. 

I adjure † you, by the correlation of God the Father and the Son, through which the Son is shown to be eternal: because the Father is eternal: who is the most abundant fountain of all goodness: to whom **emanation** <term>The process by which the Persons of the Trinity proceed from the Godhead.</term> belongs most perfectly: for he begets a Son equal to himself, to whom he communicates the fullness of his majesty. 

I adjure † you, by the **Son of God**, who is called **Sense**: by which all things are thought: **Wisdom**, by which the things thought are arranged: **Power** <note>original: "virtus"</note>, by which the things arranged are perfected: **Light**, by which once perfected they shine bright: the **Word**, by which once brightened they are announced. 

I adjure † you, by the image of equality of God the Father, which is his Son: and by the image of the Trinity which is within us: namely, in memory, intelligence, and will, or in the mind's knowledge,

<meta>This page continues the formal exorcism ritual, moving from the attributes of God the Father and the Son to a detailed adjuration based on the person and nature of the Holy Spirit. and love: in which we are, in order, made like unto the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit. I adjure † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the exorcist makes the sign of the cross over the possessed person.</note> you, by the **divine emanation** <term>The theological concept of how the persons of the Trinity "flow" from one another: the Son from the Father, and the Spirit from both.</term>, by the way of nature, which is the **generation** <note>original: "generatio," referring to the Son being "begotten" of the Father</note> according to which the Son is from the Father: and by the emanation which is by the way of the will, which is properly called **spiration** <term>The "breathing" or proceeding of the Holy Spirit from the Father and the Son.</term>: according to which the Holy Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son. 

I adjure † you, by the **Holy Spirit**, who is the love of God the Father and the Son essentially, personally, and exemplarily: **Essentially**, insofar as He is one God with the Father and the Son: for the three themselves, insofar as they are one in essence, love each other in essential love; **personally**, insofar as He is the bond <note>original: "nexus"</note> between the Father and the Son; **Exemplarily**, since the Holy Spirit is not only the efficient cause of the free love that is within us, but also its model and its end. 

I adjure † you, by the Holy Spirit, who is called **effective and exemplary charity**: because He is not only the love of the Father and the Son, but because He makes us lovers of God. 

I adjure † you, by the **Holy Spirit**, who appeared in visible signs: in **fire**, so that He might warm the cold; in **tongues**, so that He might teach the ignorant; in **sound**, so that He might terrify the rebellious; in a **dove**, to show the fullness of grace that was in Christ; in a **breath**, after the resurrection, to signify the justification of the spirit’s affections. 

I adjure † you, by the **fourfold effect** which the Apostles obtained from the presence of the Holy Spirit: namely, eloquence in tongues; boldness in torments; efficacy in miracles; and protection from vices. 

I adjure † you, by the **grace of the Holy Spirit**, by which we are cleansed from sins, strengthened in good works, and separated from earthly things; and by His **holy names**, which are: **Holy Spirit**, **Bond**, **Unity**, **Embrace**, **Gift of the Most High**, **Living Fountain**, **Fire**, **Charity**, **Spiritual Anointing**, **Finger of God’s Right Hand**, **Paraclete** <term>A title for the Holy Spirit meaning Comforter, Counselor, or Advocate.</term>, and the **Best of Teachers**. 

I adjure † you, by the **Holy Spirit**, who is moving in the ways of life but stable in the homeland <note>referring to Heaven</note>; moving in active works but stable in contemplative lives; moving in the Saints, and stable in Christ. 

I adjure † you, by the **three persons** in the unity of the divine nature: the first of whom is from none; the second is by generation from the first alone; the third is by common spiration from the first <note>original: "prima," the catchword for the next page likely leading to "and the second."</note>

first, and second: Which does not exclude from the essence of the **Trinity** its unity, simplicity <term>In theology, "simplicity" means God is not made of parts, but is a single, indivisible being.</term>, immensity, eternity, and immutability. I adjure † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the exorcist makes the sign of the cross over the possessed person.</note> you by the holy Trinity, which is made known in three ways: For the Father is shown in the scriptures; he is revealed in **figures** <note>original: "figuris" — symbolic representations or "types" found in the Old Testament that prefigure the New.</note>; he shines forth in created things: because he is the one God, strong and mighty in battle, performing justice, and rendering punishment to sinners according to their works. 

I adjure † you, by **Christ**, in whom one person and three essences consist: The **Deity** according to which he was begotten; the **Soul**, and thus he was created; and the **Flesh**, according to which he was made. Wherefore, from every state of our first parent <note>Referring to Adam before and after the Fall.</note> he assumed something: for he received immunity from sin, the fullness of grace, the compensation of punishment, the passion of pain and sorrow, and the full enjoyment of glory. 

I adjure † you by the **incarnation of Christ**, in which it is shown with what charity he loved us: For, so that he might deliver us from your hands, God was made man; the Lord, a servant; the eternal, temporal; the immense, small; the highest, thus the lowest; the incomprehensible, localized; the simple, composite; the invisible is seen, and he who cannot be touched is handled. 

I adjure † you, by the **triple birth of Christ**: by the **divine birth**, by which he is born eternally and always from the Father, without a mother; by the **gracious birth**, by which he is born in the mind spiritually, and often from the Father and from a mother <note>Likely referring to the spiritual "rebirth" of the believer through God and the Church.</note>; and by the **human birth**, by which he is born in time from a Mother, without a Father. 

I adjure † you, by the **circumcision of Christ**, by which at his birth he was circumcised by the Father with the knife of poverty; by the circumcision by which his parents circumcised him on the eighth day with the knife of the Law; by the circumcision by which he circumcised himself in all his life with the knife of humility; and by the circumcision by which he was circumcised in death with the knife of the **Passion** and the **Cross**. 

I adjure you, by the **baptism of Christ**, in which his humility is noted—coming as one not needing salvation, yet subjecting himself to the humble element of water to be baptized by his servant John. And our benefit is marked therein, because he opened heaven, and made man a **Temple of the Holy Spirit**, and poured out grace, making us, who were children of wrath, into children of grace. I adjure † you by the merit of Christ,

in which all our merits are rooted: whether they are for the satisfaction of punishment <note>Referring to "satisfactory" works that pay the debt of temporal punishment due to sin.</note>, or merit for eternal life; because we are not worthy to attain God as our reward, except through the merit of Jesus Christ himself. I adjure † <note>The cross symbol indicates where the exorcist makes the sign of the cross over the possessed person.</note> you, by the most bitter passion of Christ, in which he demonstrated both insurmountable patience, and admirable humility, and inestimable charity. I adjure † you, by the patience of Christ, which he showed in the roughness of the nails, in the piercing of the thorns, in the tearing of the whips, in the striking of hands, in the heaping on of insults <note>original: "opprobrium confuratione" — likely a scribal error for "congestione" or "confutatione," referring to the mockery Christ endured.</note>, in the bitterness of vinegar and gall, and in the shedding of tears: And by his charity, by which he delivered us from your hands, renewed grace, and opened heaven. 

I adjure † you, by the descent of Christ to the lower regions <note>Commonly known as the "Harrowing of Hell," where Christ was believed to have rescued the souls of the righteous who died before his resurrection.</note>, where by the satisfaction of his passion the bonds of captivity were loosened: and by the opening of the gate the prison was illuminated: And devouring hell, he drew out the elect existing in the bosom of Abraham <term>A biblical term (Luke 16:22) referring to the place of rest for the righteous dead before the opening of Heaven.</term>, who were being held only for the guilt of Nature <note>original: "reatu Naturæ" — referring to Original Sin inherited by all humans, as distinct from personal sins committed by the individual.</note>: but the sinners he left behind, not capable of such great merit, to be tortured perpetually. 

I adjure you, by the resurrection of Christ, whom neither the great stone rolled before the tomb, nor the guard of soldiers could hold back, but joined by many saints who had slept <note>A reference to Matthew 27:52, describing the resurrection of holy people at the time of Christ’s own rising.</note>, impassible <term>In theology, "impassible" means a body that is no longer subject to suffering, pain, or physical decay.</term> and immortal by his own power, he rose again to reign eternally. 

I adjure † you, by the ascension of Christ, which was marvelous to the Angels, terrible to the Demons, but to men, salvific: For with his obedience to the Father completed, and peace and harmony made between God and man, like a vigorous champion against the Demons, with hands raised, with two hosts—namely, of Angels and of men—he ascended in jubilation, and was placed at the right hand of God the Father: so that he may hear the vows and prayers of men. 

I adjure † you, by his coming, and the general judgment, horrible, unappealable, and inevitable; in which there will be against you a remorseful conscience accusing your sins, Christ as an angry and severe judge: with the Saints also confirming the sentence of the judge, hell will be open, and the good Angels will drive you into it along with your followers. Vanish therefore, you outcasts...
