<header>COMPLEMENT OF THE ART</header>

*Sodom*, and *Gomorrah*: and may the smoke of your pride ascend in the sight of the Most High, so that you may be consumed. <note>original: "Sodomam, & Gomorrham" — ancient cities destroyed by fire in the Book of Genesis.</note>

May the Lord of Hosts <note>original: *Sabaoth* — a Hebrew title for God as a military commander of heavenly armies.</note> scourge you with the greatest plagues: Just as He did to *Pharaoh*, and his house because of *Sarah*, the wife of *Abraham*.

May the Almighty <note>original: *Saday* — from the Hebrew 'Shaddai'.</note> slay you: just as He did to *Er*, the firstborn of *Judah*, because he was wicked in the sight of the Lord.

May God-With-Us <note>original: *Emanuel*</note> strike you: Just as He did to *Onan*, because he did a detestable thing, so that children might not be born in his brother's name. <note>A reference to the biblical account in Genesis 38.</note>

May the fire of hell devour you; just as the rod of *Aaron* devoured the rods of the Magicians <note>original: *Maleficorum* — literally 'evil-doers' or sorcerers.</note>.

May the Savior <note>original: *Sother* — from the Greek 'Soter'.</note> strike you: as He struck *Egypt* by the hands of *Moses*, and *Aaron*; so that when the water of the river was struck, it was turned into blood, and it rotted, and the fish died.

May the Holy One <note>original: *Agyos* — from the Greek 'Agios'.</note> strike you: as He struck that same *Egypt* with frogs; so that the frogs entered through the houses, and beds, through the ovens, and stoves, and through all places.

May the Strong One <note>original: *Yschiros* — from the Greek 'Ischyros'.</note> strike you; just as by striking the dust of *Egypt*, gnats <note>original: *Ciniphes* — often interpreted as lice or stinging insects.</note> were made upon men, and beasts through the whole land.

May God <note>original: *Otheos* — from the Greek 'O Theos'.</note> fill you with all kinds of torments, and pains: just as He filled the houses of the *Egyptians* with most heavy flies <note>original: *Muscis*</note> of diverse kinds: so that the land was corrupted.

May the Four-Letter Name <note>original: *Thetragrammaton* — referring to the sacred Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note> strike you with a most heavy plague: just as He struck the fields of the *Egyptians*, and the horses, donkeys, and camels, sheep, and oxen, so that they died.

May the Immortal One <note>original: *Athanathos* — from the Greek 'Athanatos'.</note> strike you, just as He struck the people, and *Pharaoh* with boils of swelling blisters by ashes scattered from a furnace.

May the Lord <note>original: *Jchova* — Jehovah.</note> break all your strengths, and your pride; just as He broke every tree of that region with hail, fire, thunders, and lightnings.

May the Mighty One <note>original: *Agla* — a Hebrew acronym used in magic and liturgy meaning 'Thou art mighty forever, O Lord'.</note> strike you, just as He struck *Egypt* with *locusts*; and may hell devour you, just as those locusts devoured every herb.

May God <note>original: *Heloym* — Elohim.</note> strike you, just as He struck that whole land for three days with horrible darkness.

May God <note>original: *Heloa* — Eloah.</note> strike you, just as He struck every Firstborn

<summary>This page lists a series of imprecations or "curses" directed at a possessing demon, using divine names in Hebrew and Greek alongside the Ten Plagues of Egypt to command its submission. It frames the exorcism as a reenactment of God's victory over Pharaoh and ancient biblical enemies.</summary>

<page-num>991</page-num>

-># THE THIRD PART OF THE ART OF EXORCISM<-

...[the firstborn] in the land of Egypt: and there was a great mourning. <note>The text begins mid-sentence, concluding a reference to the final plague of Egypt from the previous page.</note>

May He who is the Beginning and the End <note>original: *principium & finis*</note> drown you in the depths of the Abyss <note>original: *Barathri* — a term derived from Greek meaning a deep pit or the underworld</note>, just as, when the fierce and burning wind blew all night, He engulfed the chariots and all the cavalry of Pharaoh's army in the midst of the waves.

May He who is the First and the Last <note>original: *primus & novissimus*</note> crush you and burn you, like the molten calf, and give you as a prey to your enemies. <note>A reference to the golden calf destroyed by Moses in Exodus 32.</note>

Flee from the Consubstantial One <note>original: *Homousion* — from the Greek 'Homoousios', a theological term meaning 'of the same substance', referring to the Trinity</note> at the edge of the sword: just as Joshua put Amalek and his people to flight: and may He blot out your iniquity from this Creature <note>In an exorcism, "Creature" usually refers to the possessed person being cleansed.</note>, just as He blotted out his memory from under Heaven.

May the sword of the Lord rush upon you in His fury, just as the sons of Levi rushed upon the people because of their idolatry: so that about thirty thousand men fell.

May the perpetual fire now devour you, just as the fire descending from the Lord devoured Nadab and Abihu, who offered strange fire before the Lord Himself. <note>A reference to the story in Leviticus 10 where Aaron’s sons were consumed for ritual errors.</note>

May the Alpha and Omega <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, representing God's eternity.</note> crush you, just as the children of Israel crushed the son of Shelomith <note>original: *Salumith*</note> with stones, because he had blasphemed the name of the Lord and cursed Him.

May the kindled, burning fire of *I AM* <note>original: *Eyerehiæ* — a Latinized form of the Hebrew 'Ehyeh', the divine name revealed to Moses</note> be sent out to devour you, and may His fury strike you with an exceedingly great plague: just as He struck the people who murmured and craved meat.

May all the devils who are your enemies overwhelm you, leading you down into hell: just as the crowd overwhelmed with stones the man gathering wood on the Sabbath day.

May the earth open its mouth and devour you, along with all who support you: so that you may descend into hell alive, to be tortured for eternity, just as it devoured Dathan and Abiram <note>original: *Abyron*</note> with their tents and all their substance. <note>A reference to the rebellion against Moses in Numbers 16.</note>

May a fire go out from the Lord to destroy you: just as it destroyed two hundred and fifty men offering incense.

May Life <note>original: *vita* — here used as a title for God as the source of all life</note> strike you with the edge of His sword: just as He struck Sihon <note>original: *Sehon*</note>, King of the Amorites, and Og, King of Bashan <note>original: *Basan*</note>, with their peoples: and may He deliver you into my hand, just as He delivered them to the people of Israel.

<page-num>992</page-num>

### THE COMPLETION OF THE ART

May the Lord strike you with the edge of the sword and kill you: just as He did to the city of Jericho and everything that was within it.

**May the Celestial Lion** <note>original: *Leo Cœlestis* — likely a reference to the Lion of Judah, a title for Christ</note> strike you with leprosy, just as He struck Miriam when she spoke against Moses. <note>Referring to Numbers 12, where Miriam was temporarily stricken with leprosy as divine punishment.</note>

**May God** <note>original: *De⁹*, a common scribal abbreviation for *Deus*</note> drive you out and stone you: just as all Israel drove out and stoned Achan, because he was an Accursed Thing <note>original: *Anathema* — something set apart for destruction</note> in Israel.

**May the Holy One** trample you and hang you on an infernal gallows: just as the five kings of the Amorites were trampled and hanged. <note>A reference to Joshua 10, where Joshua defeated five kings and executed them.</note>

**May the Almighty** burn you with sulfur and the sun’s heat, as an example of the ruin of Sodom and Gomorrah, Admah and Zeboiim: which the Lord overthrew in His anger and His fury.

**May the immortal God** strike you with a double-edged sword: just as the savior Ehud <note>original: *Aod*</note>, extending his left hand, took the dagger from his right thigh and thrust it into the belly of Eglon, King of Moab.

**May the eternal God** destroy you: just as He destroyed the walls of Gath, and the Canaanites, and Azotus.

**May the ineffable God** strike you: just as Samson struck the Philistines with a mighty blow.

**May God strike you** with blindness: just as He struck the people of the King of Syria who were pursuing Elisha in Dothan <note>original: *Dotaym*</note>.

**May God strike you**: just as He struck Jeroboam when he was pursuing Abijah.

**May the Messiah strike you**: just as the Stone, which is Christ, cut from the mountain, struck the Statue <note>original: *Statutam* — referring to the dream of King Nebuchadnezzar in the Book of Daniel, where a stone topples a great idol</note> and became a great mountain.

**May God drive a nail** into your brain and strike it with a hammer: just as Jael <note>original: *Jahel*</note>, placing the tent peg <note>original: *clavo Tabernaculi*</note> against the temple of Sisera’s head, struck him with a hammer and drove it through his brain into the ground, and he died.

**May the hand of God** be very heavy upon you, just as it was upon the Philistines because of the Ark of the Lord being brought into the Temple of Dagon.

**May the Lord strike you**: just as He struck the camps of the Assyrians through His Angel.

<meta>catchword: Con</meta>

<summary>This page lists a series of violent biblical precedents used as ritual curses to command a demon to depart. By invoking historical divine punishments—such as the fall of Jericho or the slaying of Sisera—the exorcist claims the same divine authority to destroy the possessing spirit.</summary>

**May God shatter your head** and cut off your hands, just as the palms and the head of the cursed Dagon were cut off. <note>original: *Dagon* — Referring to the Philistine deity whose statue repeatedly fell and broke before the Ark of the Covenant in 1 Samuel 5.</note>

**May the God of Hosts** <note>original: *Deus Exercituum*</note> strike you, just as David struck Goliath with a stone fixed in his forehead.

**May God hand you over to Lucifer**: just as He handed Saul over to an unclean spirit.

**May God divide you from this Creature**: just as the waters are divided, and as David divided the camp of the Philistines.

**May God hang you on the gallows of hell**: just as the seven men from the sons of Saul were hanged. <note>Refers to the execution of Saul’s descendants in 2 Samuel 21 to end a famine.</note>

**May the God of Israel kill you**, just as He killed the prophets of Baal. <note>Referring to the contest on Mount Carmel in 1 Kings 18.</note>

**May fire from Heaven devour you**, just as it devoured the two captains, and the first and second groups of fifty belonging to Ahaziah. <note>original: *Ochozię* — Ahaziah, King of Israel, whose soldiers were consumed by fire called down by the prophet Elijah.</note>

**May the Lord pour out His fury upon you**: just as He poured it out upon Jerusalem through the man dressed in linen, with an ink-horn at his back. <note>A reference to the vision in Ezekiel 9 where a divine scribe marks those to be spared while others are struck down.</note>

**May God cast you down into hell**: just as Jehu caused Jezebel to be thrown down; and may the infernal dogs eat you, just as the dogs ate her.

**May your enemies rush upon you**: just as the demon Asmodeus <note>In the Book of Tobit, Asmodeus killed seven successive husbands of Sarah.</note> rushed upon the seven men.

**May the right hand of God seize you**, and bind you in the Pit: just as you, or another demon, were seized and bound by the Angel Raphael in the desert of upper Egypt. <note>original: *Barathro* — the Abyss or Pit. This refers to the end of the Book of Tobit where Raphael binds the demon Asmodeus.</note>

**May God unsheathe the sword of His vengeance** and justice, and seizing the hair of your head, strike you until you descend to hell: just as Judith, striking Holofernes, cut off his head.

**May the flame of fire kill you**: just as it killed the men who threw Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego <note>original: *Sydrach, Misach, & Abdenago*</note> into the furnace.

**May the infernal lions crush and devour you**: just as the lions crushed the men who accused Daniel.

**May God cast you into the place of the Pit**: just as He cast into the lake those who were the cause of Daniel’s destruction, so that you may be devoured in a moment.

**May God strike you through His Angel**: just as He struck Herod with worms, because he did not give glory to God. <note>Referring to the death of Herod Agrippa in Acts 12:23.</note>

**May Christ cast you out with a whip**: just as He cast out those buying and selling from the Temple: and may He overturn you in fury: just as He overturned the tables of the money-changers.

**May Jesus hurl you into the sea of hell**: just as He has already hurled the demons existing in the swine into the sea. <note>A reference to the Gerasene demoniac and the "Legion" of spirits in Mark 5.</note>

**May Christ confound you**, just as you were confounded in many women, and especially in Mary Magdalene. <note>Refers to the tradition that Mary Magdalene was delivered from seven demons (Luke 8:2).</note>

**May Christ cast you out from this body**, just as He cast out the moon-struck spirit, mute and deaf: saying, "You deaf and mute spirit, I command you, come out of him: and enter into him no more." <note>original: *Spiritum Lunaticum* — Literally "moon-struck," a term used in the period to describe conditions like epilepsy or specific types of possession believed to be influenced by the moon's phases.</note>

**May Christ cast you out**, just as He cast the demon from the daughter of the Canaanite woman, and another from the man in the Synagogue, and many others He cast down in His name.

**May the Lord hang a millstone around your neck**, and sink you into the deep. <note>original: *molam asinariam* — specifically a large millstone turned by a donkey, a reference to Christ’s warning in Matthew 18:6.</note>

**Strike them, O Lord**, so that they fall backward, just as the crowds with clubs and weapons did when wanting to seize You. <note>A reference to the soldiers in the Garden of Gethsemane falling back when Jesus identified himself (John 18:6).</note>

**May God kill you**, just as He killed the two false witnesses maliciously accusing Susanna. <note>Susanna: A heroine from the additions to the Book of Daniel; her accusers were sentenced to the same death they intended for her.</note>

**May the Lord hang you on the gallows of hell**, just as He did Judas with a noose.

**May God hand you over, you wicked servant, to the infernal torturers**, until you return obedience to the Priest.

<margin>C</margin>
**May the Lord repay you who do evil** according to your malice, and like the lame and the blind, you shall not enter the Kingdom of God.

**May God make you tremble and put you to flight**, along with all those favoring you, at the invocation of Jesus Christ.

**May the Lord hand you over to fall before your enemies**, and may you be scattered through all the torments of hell.

**May the Lord crush the pride of your hardness now and always**, and for ages of ages.

**May the Lord send the beasts of hell**, which shall consume and afflict you, so that your ways may be reduced to nothing.

<header>EXORCISM PART THREE. 999</header>

May the Lord afflict you greatly, until, confessing your iniquities, you flee from this creature. <note>In this context, "creature" refers to the possessed person, viewed as a created being of God.</note>

May there be against you, in the name of the Lord, fiery Serpents, your enemies, so that they may drag you from this body and lead you down into the Abyss. <note>original: *Barathrum* — a Greek-derived term for a deep pit or the underworld, often used in Christian Latin to denote hell.</note>

May the Lord strike you with poverty, leprosy, fever, cold, heat, scorching, corrupted air, and blight: and until you depart, these shall pursue you, and thereafter forever. <note>This list of afflictions mirrors the curses for disobedience found in Deuteronomy 28.</note>

Humble yourself therefore, Accursed One: in the name of him who uprooted pride and introduced humility: For our Lord Jesus Christ himself, who is praised by the voices of the Angelic Host, presses down the heights of the world and holds those swollen with pride under his feet: therefore, with your roots torn out, your branches broken, cut to pieces, scattered, and withered, cast yourself down and flee from this creature. In the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit. Amen.

## E X O R C I S M  VIII.
### ->*Called the Exorcism of the Angels.*<-

> O God, † come to my assistance.
> O Lord, † make haste to help me.
> Glory be to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit:
> As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end. Amen. Alleluia.
> O Lord, hear my prayer.
> And let my cry come unto you.
> The Lord be with you.
> And with your spirit.

->Let us pray.<-

O God, who wonderfully created the Angelic nature for your service: and by a wondrous order assigned them to the governance of the universe: Grant, we beseech you, that we may be helped by their prayers in Heaven, and that in this your holy place, through their presence, this your creature may be liberated and the Accursed ones confounded. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.

### HERE HE SHALL PLACE HIS HANDS UPON

## EXORCISM.

I exorcize you, N. <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen*, indicating the exorcist should insert the name of the person being treated.</note> a creature of the Most High: regenerated by the blood of Jesus Christ, by the font of baptism, and by the power of the sacraments: numbered among the faithful in the bosom of the holy Mother Church: so that you may not have the power to retain the evil Angels, the satellites of Lucifer, nor their defenses within you; but through the choirs of the Angels of God, and especially through the seven Angels standing before the divine Majesty, <note>A reference to the seven archangels mentioned in the Book of Tobit and the Book of Revelation who stand in the presence of God.</note> may you expel them, and cast them down in the unity of Jesus Christ.

->*Let us pray.*<-

*O God, who in a wonderful order dispense the ministries of Angels and men: graciously grant that our life on earth may be protected by those who always assist you, ministering to you in heaven. Through Christ our Lord. Amen.*

### THEN HE SHALL SPRINKLE WITH WATER
->Blessed, and with eyes raised, say:<-

*I have lifted up my eyes to the mountains, from where will help come to me? My help is from the Lord, who made heaven and earth.* <note>This is the opening of Psalm 121, traditionally used to express trust in God's protection.</note>

Next let the following Invitatory be said, and the Psalm, just as in the double office. <note>The "double office" refers to a high-ranking category of feast day in the traditional Catholic liturgy, implying that this exorcism should be performed with the solemnity of a major holy day.</note>

The Lord, King of the Archangels, come let us adore.
The Lord, King of the Archangels, come let us adore.
Come, let us exult in the Lord: Let us rejoice in God our Savior: let us come before his face in praise, and with Psalms let us rejoice in him.
The Lord, King of the Archangels, come let us adore, etc.

## CONJURATION.

I conjure <note>original: *Conjuro*; in this context, it is a formal command or binding oath used by the exorcist to compel a spirit.</note> † you, accursed Angels, that you flee from this creature of God, without any injury to him, along with all your followers and your filth: In the name of the Most Holy Trinity: and by the holy Angels, who, with drawn swords, encounter you as mighty ones in battle. And first, by Saint Michael, the Prince of the Heavenly Host, I conjure you: of whom the holy Church sings: *There was silence made in Heaven, while the Dragon committed himself to war: and Michael fought with him: and he won the victory: Alleluia:* <note>This refers to the "War in Heaven" described in the Book of Revelation 12:7.</note> and again: *The sea was shaken, and the earth trembled, when Michael the Archangel descended from Heaven.* For he is the one set over the Church of God, more glorious than the others, who leads souls into Paradise: he fights against the ancient Dragon, and comes to the aid of the people of God. I conjure † you, by Saint Gabriel, who was the distinguished messenger of the betrothal of God and Mary: In whose embassy, before all things, he reverently saluted the Virgin with innocence, and salvation, and

...indicated the fullness of grace, saying: *Hail, full of grace:* And commending her, excelling in joy, he said: *Blessed are you among women:* and he sweetly comforted the trembling Mary: *Do not fear, Mary:* and completing his mission, he said: *Behold, you shall conceive and bear a son.* And the virgin responding, "How shall this be, since I know not a man?" he explained the way: *The Holy Spirit shall come upon you:* and finally, he explained the nobility and excellence of the Offspring: *Therefore, that holy thing which shall be born of you shall be called the Son of God:* <note>The text here recounts the Gospel of Luke's account of the Annunciation, using Gabriel's message to Mary as a source of divine authority.</note> whom Daniel also saw, saying: *Behold, a certain man clothed in linen, and his loins were girded with fine gold; and his body like chrysolite, and his face like the appearance of lightning, and his eyes like a burning lamp; and his arms, and those parts downward even to the feet, like the appearance of glowing brass; and the voice of his words like the voice of a multitude.* <note>Quoting Daniel 10:5-6. This celestial vision is used to emphasize the terrifying majesty of the divine agents the exorcist is invoking.</note>

I conjure † you, finally, by the celestial Hierarchy—upper, middle, and lower: each of which contains three orders of those blessed Spirits standing before the divine Majesty.

I conjure † you, by the **Angels**, who lead men to divine knowledge, and instruct them to live justly: through the love of God they reach upward: they are strengthened in themselves through their own self-guardianship: and they go forth with great care to help their neighbors below them.

I conjure † you, by the **Archangels**, to whom the management of great matters belongs: and because they announce the highest things, they are called the "highest messengers": For they deliver men regarding matters belonging to the promotion of the faith, and they remove Demons from men, and reveal prophecies.

I conjure † you, by the **Virtues** <note>original: *virtutes*; a specific rank in the angelic hierarchy believed to govern the laws of nature and perform miracles.</note> who perform the working of miracles: These teach leaders so that they may execute the duties belonging to the office of leadership easily, perseveringly, and patiently bearing spiritual burdens.

I conjure † you, by the **Powers**, who [constrain] the wills and strengths of the apostate Angels <note>original: *Angelorum apostatarum*; a reference to fallen angels or demons.</note>, so that they may not harm the world and oppress it as they desire: and they strengthen those struggling in the spiritual battle: and they defend them lest they lose the spiritual Kingdom.

I conjure † you, by the **Principalities**, who arrange the Angels over whom they preside for the fulfillment of divine ministry: they lead the universal governance of a single Kingdom or nation into the likeness of God: they teach men to show reverence to each person according to their rank: and they lead them to execute justice, for the love of God alone,

-># THE COMPLETION OF THE ART<-
**1006**

...they promote love and honor, and not for their own personal benefit. I conjure <note>original: *Conjuro*; a formal command in an exorcism, often accompanied by the sign of the cross.</note> † you, by the **Dominions** <term>Dominions (or Dominations): the fourth rank of angels, believed to regulate the duties of the lower angelic orders and represent the sovereignty of God.</term>, who rule and govern the offices of the other Angels, and are subject to no inferior: they provide instruction in spiritual warfare, teaching how each person ought to exercise the office of leadership, how to rule over subjects, and how—having set aside servile fear—to be moved toward God and to serve Him incessantly. I conjure † you by the **Thrones** <term>Thrones: the third rank of angels, through whom God dispenses His judgments; they represent divine steadfastness.</term>, in whom the rectitude of the divine office especially shines: because the Lord, sitting upon them, determines His judgments through them. I conjure † you, by the **Cherubim** <term>Cherubim: the second rank, associated with the "fullness of knowledge" and the reflection of divine light.</term>, who reflect the ray of divine light most principally before all others, because they are joined to the First Truth, and they pour out and transmit the fullness of light which they receive by communicating it to those below them, thus moving men toward divine knowledge. I conjure † you, by the **Seraphim** <term>Seraphim: the highest rank of angels, whose name means "the burning ones," representing pure love and fire.</term>, who (as is their specific nature) burn in the love of God, and move others toward the fire of divine love, and who, by establishing the pattern of loving God, bestow that form upon them. I conjure † you, by the ranks of the blessed Spirits, persisting in them through eternal ages: namely, through the intensity of their bliss, their degree of dignity, and the nobility of the gift of grace and of Nature. I conjure † you by their confirmation through grace—the grace of the blessed spirits, by which they are bound in this stable, eternal, and indissoluble bond of charity <note>original: *charitatis*; in this context, the highest form of Christian love (agape) that binds the celestial court.</note>: by their confirmation through glory, because of which they do not wander, seeking anything more desirable or more joyful than God; rather, possessing God through glory, they have their desires fulfilled: so that there is nothing that can turn them away from the fountain of divine sweetness. I conjure † you by their confirmation through nature, by which, having turned toward God through the freedom of their will, they were immediately confirmed as faithful and made happy in their will through grace and glory, and illuminated in their reason: so that they might know things not only in the specific nature of a thing, but also in the Art <note>original: *in arte, id est, in verbo*; a philosophical concept meaning they see things as they exist in the "Word" or the divine mind of God.</note>—that is, in the Word: and thus receiving perfection in their active and contemplative power, running within God, they rejoice forever and ever. I conjure † you finally, by all the heavenly Spirits, whose glory no tongue can explain, nor mind can grasp:

<meta>catchword: These</meta>

**1007**

### THIRD PART OF THE EXORCISMS.

For these are the members of God’s household, and they always assist Him: Citizens of Heaven, and Princes of Paradise: Teachers of wisdom, and Masters of knowledge: illuminators of souls, and their guardians: protectors of bodies, and their defenders: most diligent guardians of provinces and dignities, both spiritual as well as temporal. For these remove the obstacles to good, and the occasions of evil: they mitigate temptations by opposing themselves to the diabolical powers, so that they may not harm us as they might wish: they rouse us from sins, and lead us to the weeping of contrition and penance: they obtain victory against Demons, and instruct us in the fervor of doing good works: they show the way of salvation: they present pious works to God: they pray for us, they love us, and they protect us: and they desire that we might fill the seats of the fallen Angels <note>A common medieval belief that humans were created to fill the void in Heaven left by Lucifer and the rebel angels.</note>, waiting for our arrival with joy; they lead us to prayers, and are present with those who pray, rejoicing: they assist us as we sleep: in the sea, and on land, and in all things they help us, they enlighten the mind, they comfort us in tribulations, and they often deliver us from infirmities. For these are they by whom, at the very end of our lives, we are strengthened in faith, and moved toward penance against the serpent <note>original: *serpentem*; a traditional name for the Devil, referring to the Garden of Eden.</note>, and having obtained victory, we are carried by them to Paradise, or to <term>Purgatory: in Catholic theology, a place or state of suffering inhabited by the souls of sinners who are expiating their sins before going to heaven.</term>: and in Purgatory we are often visited and comforted by them, as they promise that we shall enter the celestial Jerusalem. 

Depart therefore, Cursed Apostate <note>original: *Maledicte Apostate*; a title for the devil as one who has abandoned his faith and station in God.</note>, through the morning and midday knowledge of the Angels, and through the accumulation of evening merit, which increases for them from hour to hour <note>These terms—morning, midday, and evening knowledge—refer to a scholastic theory popularized by St. Augustine regarding how angels perceive things in God (morning) versus in themselves or created things (evening).</note>: through the demonstration of love by which they incessantly serve us, and through the wondrous mysteries announced and worked by them as the timing requires. What portion have you with the blessed spirits? Fly, therefore: in the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy † Spirit. Amen.

### ADORATION OF THE CROSS.

All things around us reflect the worthiness of your mercy, O Lord: O inestimable love of charity! To redeem a slave, you gave up your son. O exceedingly sad spectacle, O cruel punishment paid to Christ; O mocked King, crowned with such an unseemly death. O certainly necessary sin of Adam, which was blotted out by the death of Christ! O happy fault <note>original: *fœlix culpa*; a famous phrase from the "Exsultet" of the Easter Vigil, suggesting that the fall of man was "happy" because it resulted in the coming of Christ.</note>, which deserved to have such and so great a Redeemer. O glorious Cross, O Cross to be adored, O precious Cross, O admirable sign, through which the Devil is conquered, and the world is redeemed by the blood of Christ. O holy Cross, in whose width works of charity are signified, and in whose length perseverance itself is demonstrated; in its height we hope for the reward of heavenly prizes, and in your depth the inscrutable judgments of God are designated <note>This passage uses the four dimensions of the cross—breadth, length, height, and depth—as a traditional metaphor for the virtues of a Christian life and the mysteries of God.</note>. O noble tree, no forest brings forth such a one in leaf, flower, or seed. O Cross, alone more lofty than all the wood of the cedars: on you the life of the world hung, in you Christ triumphed, and death overcame death forever. O most worthy tree, set in the middle of Paradise, in which the Author of salvation, by his own death, overcame the death of all. O Cross radiant with excellent beauty, which Helena <note>St. Helena (c. 246–330 AD), the mother of Emperor Constantine the Great, who according to tradition discovered the "True Cross" in Jerusalem.</note>, the Mother of Constantine, sought and found with a longing soul. O noble Trophy of the Cross, you who are adored by all Christians through the ages. O Cross of our Lord Jesus Christ, it is right for us to glory in you; for on you hung Christ, in whom our salvation, life, and resurrection consist. O holy Cross, we adore you, and we recall the glorious passion of our Lord Jesus Christ, through whom we have been saved and liberated. O sacred pledge, while you are revealed, the faith of Christ is strengthened: divine wonders are present, which were first prefigured in the rod of Moses <note>A reference to "typology," where the staff of Moses used to perform miracles in the Old Testament is seen as a prophetic "type" or symbol of the Cross.</note>. O Cross, brighter than all the stars, when you appear in Heaven, you will shine wondrously, and you will obscure the lights of the sky; and when the stars have fallen, you alone will radiate. O Cross, celebrated by the world, greatly beloved by men, holier than all things: which alone...

THE THIRD PART OF THE ART OF EXORCISM.

## THE HYMN OF SAINT AMBROSE,
### regarding the Passion of the Lord.

> Let us sing a hymn to the Lord,
> Praises to God with a song,
> Who, upon the timber <note>original: "patibulo" — specifically the cross-beam of the crucifix.</note> of the Cross,
> Has redeemed us with His own blood.
> 
> When the day had run its course toward evening,
> On which Christ was handed over to death,
> The wicked man arrived for the supper,
> He who was the betrayer of Christ.
> 
> Jesus announces things to come,
> To the Disciples as they dine:
> "One of those reclining at the table
> Is the very one who will betray me."
> 
> Judas, that most wretched merchant <note>original: "mercator pessimus." Medieval writers often used the metaphor of a "bad merchant" for Judas because he traded something of infinite value—the Son of God—for a paltry sum of silver.</note>,
> Seeks out the Lord with a kiss;
> He [the Lord], like an innocent Lamb,
> Does not deny the kiss to Judas.
> 
> For a sum of silver coins,
> Christ is handed over to the Jews;
> Innocent and harmless,
> He whom the wicked Judas betrays.
> 
> Pilate the Governor proclaims,
> "I find no guilt in Him";
> Having washed his hands with water,
> He delivered Christ to the Jews.
> 
> The deceitful and impious Jews
> Demand that the thief should live;
> They gravely accuse Christ,
> "Let him be crucified, he is guilty!"
> 
> And Barabbas is set free,
> He who had been guilty of death;
> The Life of the world is hung [on the cross],
> Through whom the dead rise again.
> 
> Glory to the Father, the Lord,
> Glory to the Only-Begotten,
> Together with the Holy Spirit,
> Forever and ever <note>original: "in seculorum secula" — literally "into the ages of ages."</note>, Amen.

<summary>This page features a rhythmic Latin hymn attributed to St. Ambrose of Milan, detailing the betrayal, trial, and crucifixion of Christ. In the context of an exorcism manual, such hymns were recited to invoke the redemptive power of the Passion against demonic influence.</summary>
<keywords>Passion of Christ, Saint Ambrose, Judas Iscariot, Pontius Pilate, Barabbas, Crucifixion, Christian Hymnody, Redemption</keywords>
